A bare "Code", "Enter code" and split boxes named "Digit 1" still type; a bare "code" would also refuse "Zip code", "Promo code" and "Country code", which is the owner's to decide.

**Reason.** The floor says the model never types a one-time code or a card security code, and that the amber frame is around exactly what the model sees. In the real app the first was silently off and cost 20 seconds per piece, and the second was wrong whenever the window moved, was maximised after Start or was restored from minimised. The tests called the backend directly and never went through the desktop's message list, which is why it slipped through; the new tests take every method of the bridge and assert the desktop handler has it, run a typing step over a channel, the desktop's loader and its real handler list, and run the two questions about the focused field through the real Windows code (`index.cjs` and `win.cjs`) on the project's UI Automation stand-in with a browser whose tree is not built yet. The older-desktop sentence exists because "click the field once more and try again" sends the model round in a circle there; only updating the desktop app helps.

**Source.** The audit findings browser-computer-1 and browser-computer-4 (12 Nov 2026), with the verifiers' verdicts (severity of the first corrected from critical to high: the password-field check itself, `computer-is-secure-field`, was registered and works), and the review of this package (13 Nov 2026: the name read during the password check's wait; the older desktop; the paused test). Code: `desktop/handlers/computer.cjs` (`TYPES`), `desktop/computer/index.cjs` (`METHODS` exported; `methods` in the probe reply; `overlay`), `desktop/computer/win.cjs` (`focusedLabel`), `lib/computer/bridge.mjs` (`LABEL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `focusedLabel`, `remote`, `zoom`), `lib/computer/run.mjs` (`gateTyping`, `TEXT.fieldNameUnread`, `TEXT.oldDesktop`, `TEXT.oldDesktopNotice`, `followWindow`, `overlayArgs`), `lib/computer/policy.mjs` (`SECRET_LABEL`).

**Status.** Built on 12 and 13 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-computer-guard/guard.test.mjs`, 20 tests; the four added in the fix pass fail on the code as it was reviewed, and the paused-frame test fails when the paused branch is broken on purpose). Neighbouring suites pass: computer-backend 191, computer-gate 136, computer-generic 33, computer-smoke 31, computer-ui 24, fix06-computer-floor 65, password-guard-mac-linux 48, stab913d 33. Not run on a real screen or a real Windows desktop (nothing may touch the owner's desktop): the 2 second limit and the 503/504 refusal are covered through a request-style bridge, the overlay through a fake Electron, and the Windows field code through the UI Automation stand-in. The real UI Automation answer for the focused field's name has still never been read on a real Windows desktop; a read-only look (as `build/v06/stab913d/real-smoke.test.cjs` does for the password question) is owed before the code and CVV refusal is relied on. The frame is drawn again after a look (a screenshot or a zoom), not while the model acts, so between an action that moves the window and the next screenshot the frame is where the window was. **This item ships as a full install of the desktop app:** three of its files are under `desktop/`, which the Updates panel does not replace, and a server with this item and an older desktop app refuses all typed text (and says to update Plenum). `desktop/computer/capture.cjs` and `desktop/computer/overlay.cjs` were not changed.

## 100. Stop from a phone does not depend on the phones file, and a failed read of that file heals by itself

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (findings remote-1 and remote-2). Several things about `phones.json`, the file that holds the computer's phone identity, the paired phones, their Stop keys and their handshake counters, were wrong.
- **Counter bookkeeping never vetoes Stop.** A signed STOP_ONLY message and a session handshake both record the phone's counter first. That record used to wait for the save and to throw when the save failed, so the socket closed with 4500 `storage` before Stop ran, and an unlocked phone could not connect either. `registry.recordHandshake` now raises the counter in memory at once, before anything is awaited, keeps it raised whatever the save does, and saves in the background. Stop runs and is answered; a paired phone connects. A copy of the same message is still a replay (equal or lower counters are refused, as before), also when two copies arrive together, because the counter rises before the first await. The per-source and per-device rate windows did not change, and no margin is added to stored counters on load.
- **A phone that still needs its Stop key is not kept out either.** A phone paired before Stop keys existed asks for one when it connects, and the computer's answer has to say whether a key comes before the key exists. The answer is now the save of the counter just taken: when that save worked the phone gets its key as before; when it failed, or has not answered after 3 seconds, the session goes on without a key and the answer does not promise one. The phone can Stop from inside the session and asks again at its next connection. A key that is not in the file is never handed out.
- **What still waits for the save.** Pairing, a new Stop key, a changed tier or name, Remove and Remove all keep `persistNow` and their rollback: a key that was not saved is never reported as paired.
- **A failed save is tried again.** After 0.25 s, doubling to 30 s, until one works. While the last save failed, Settings › Phones shows one red row with the cause and the next step. The page reads the state again every 6 seconds while the row (or the row about a failed read) shows; when the window is hidden it waits and starts again when the window is back, and coming back reads at once. The row goes by itself when the save has worked.
- **Both writers try a refused rename again.** The desktop writer (`desktop/handlers/phones.cjs` `writeRaw`) and the plain-node writer (`createFileStorage.save`) use a new temporary name for every write and try the rename again on EPERM, EBUSY and EACCES (six more tries over about 1.5 seconds). When it never works the save fails, the old file is as it was and the temporary file is removed. There is no write in place: half a file would be worse than a late save. Temporary files that a save left behind because Plenum ended in the middle of it are removed after the first good read (only the phones file's own temporary names, only when older than a minute).
- **A failed read is not remembered.** `registry.ready()` kept its first answer for the life of the process, a failure included. It now keeps only a good one, shares a read in progress, and reads again after 2, 5, 15, then every 60 seconds; a call inside the wait gets the same sentence without touching the file. The Bridge tries its start again on that clock while "Let phones connect" is on, so phones come back with Settings closed. A person turning the switch on reads the file at once, not after the wait. A timer never turns phones on after the switch went off.
- **Nothing is saved over a file that could not be read.** While the read has not worked, no save is issued, so a failed read can never be followed by a save of an empty registry.
- **A decryption that fails does not lose the phones.** On the desktop a decryption that threw was reported as a damaged file, so the first save replaced it: a new computer identity, and every phone "not paired". A thrown decryption is now "not now" (the registry reads again). The file is called damaged only when the same contents have failed at least four reads AND at least a minute has passed since the first of them (with phones on that is the read about 82 seconds after the first), or when it decrypts to something that is not the phone data. Reads inside that minute, however many, are never proof: pressing "Let phones connect" again and again cannot replace the identity.
- **A file nobody could read is kept, never written over.** A file that could not be parsed or decrypted is set aside as `phones.json.damaged` (then `-2`, `-3` ...) before the new one is written. The same now holds on the desktop for a file that decrypts but that this build's registry cannot use (for example after going back to an older build): the server tells the desktop with its next save, and the desktop sets aside exactly the file its last read returned. When the file cannot be set aside (the rename is refused, after the same tries as above), the save fails, the file stays, and the save is tried again; before, the new file replaced it. Plain node does the same.

**Reason.** The floor says Stop always works, and item 71 says a locked phone can always Stop. A scanner holding a file for a moment is ordinary on Windows (the owner's log already shows `rename EPERM` for the state file), and it must not be able to switch off the one control that has to work, or the phones until the next restart, or the pairing of every phone. The cost is small: if Plenum restarts before a failed save is repeated, the counters not yet saved could be played again; a replayed STOP_ONLY only stops again, and a replayed handshake message cannot finish without the phone's private key. A file that truly cannot be decrypted now keeps phones off for a little over a minute before Plenum starts fresh (the old file is kept); that is the price of never starting fresh because of a moment's failure.

**Source.** Audit findings remote-1 and remote-2 and their verdicts (12 Nov 2026), and the review of this package (13 Nov 2026). Code: `lib/remote/registry.mjs` (`ready`, `retryInMs`, `persist`, `planSaveRetry`, `recordHandshake`, `createFileStorage` `save`, `setAside`, `sweepTemps`, `createShellStorage` `setAside`, `status().saveProblem`), `lib/remote/listener.mjs` (`onStopOnly`, `startSession`, `STOP_KEY_SAVE_WAIT_MS`), `lib/remote/index.mjs` (`planRetry`, `start({ retryNow })`, `doStart`, `state().saveProblem`), `lib/routes/remote.mjs` (the settings route), `desktop/handlers/phones.cjs` (`createStore`: `writeRaw`, `renameRetrying`, `load`, `save(text, { setAside })`, `sweepTemps`, `DECRYPT_TRIES`, `DECRYPT_MIN_MS`), `public/ext-remote.js` (`ERROR_ROW`, the save row, `healTick`, `healOnShow`).

**Status.** Built on 12 and 13 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-phones-stop`, 5 files, 57 of 57): Stop and a session with a save that fails, never answers or throws; replay of the same and of a lower counter; two copies at once; the counter reaching the file later and surviving a reload; pairing, key top-up, change and remove still failing with the storage sentence; a phone that asks for a Stop key while the save fails or never answers; the waits 2, 5, 15, 60 s; a shared read; no save after a failed read; the Bridge starting by itself and not after the switch went off; both writers with a rename that fails twice, always, and with another error; the decryption cases, four and forty reads inside the first minute among them; the set-aside through the real handler messages and a set-aside that is refused; the temporary files; the row's timer with the window hidden and back; and one end-to-end test against a sealed `server.mjs`. Checked in a rendered page against a sealed server whose save was failing: the row is red, the state was read every 6 seconds, not at all during 13 seconds of a hidden window, at once when the window came back, and the row went by itself a few seconds after the disk was freed. Four tests of the phone track (`mobile/test/bridge.test.mjs`, `pairing.test.mjs`, `handler-phones.test.mjs`) pinned the old behaviour and were corrected to the new one, and the source check of `server.mjs` in `mobile/test/track-b.test.mjs` now allows lines between the start of the listener and `startPhones()` (wave 0 put the provider prewarm between them); with them `pairing`, `handler-phones` and `bridge` passed 188 of 188 (1 skipped) in the sealed copy. Not tried with a real phone, a real Electron `safeStorage`, or in the installed app. Open: Plenum still starts fresh by itself when a file stays undecryptable for more than a minute (the old file is kept as `phones.json.damaged`, to be put back by hand); asking the person first would be better. The phones handler's sentence for a file that cannot be opened just now (`OPEN_LATER` in `desktop/handlers/phones.cjs`) now also fits a person's press of "Let phones connect" after a failed start: it says that Plenum tries again by itself while the switch is on and that, if it switched itself off, it can be turned on again in a minute. The save row appears only at the next read of the state when a save starts failing while Settings › Phones is already open.

## 101. An agent's scan of its folder is fast, can be stopped, and says when it did not reach every file

**Decision.** Found in the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (finding agents-2): every agent with file access read and hashed the whole project folder before and after its work, one file at a time, outside the agent's stop signal and with its time limit already running; a build did it twice more; and past 20,000 files the scan stopped without a word. On this PC under load 2,000 small files took 30 to 60 seconds, Stop did nothing meanwhile, and in a folder of more than 20,000 files a new file could make an untouched file look "changed outside the scope".
- **Stoppable, with a limit.** The scan (`snapshot` in `lib/agent-run.mjs`) reads 8 files at once and looks at the stop signal and the clock between two files. An agent's scan has 15 seconds (`SNAP_BUDGET_MS`); after Stop the last scan has 3. A scan that is ended early answers with what it read and `partial: true` (`stopped: true` as well when Stop ended it). Stopped during the first scan, the model never starts and the agent ends at once. The agent's time and idle clocks stand still during the scans and start when the model does, so the scan no longer eats the time limit.
- **Later scans read only what changed.** The hashes of the last scan of a folder are remembered in memory (the last 4 folders). A file whose size, modified time, change time and file number are unchanged keeps its hash without being read; a file changed in the 3 seconds before it was read is read again next time (the rule git follows for its index). A build's first scan still reads every file, because it keeps the bytes for the review.
- **It says what it could not check.** A scan that stopped at 20,000 files or ran out of time is said in a notice (`{ type: 'notice', kind: 'agent-scan', text }`), in the agent's summary (so the calling model and the task row read it too) and as `unchecked: true` on the result; a build says it before its tasks start, tells the reviewer and adds it to the review's summary. The comparison (`changedBetween`) then lists only what is certain (a file both scans hold with different content, a file new since a whole first scan, a file gone from a whole second one), so no file is blamed that was only out of the scan's reach.
- **A slow scan shows.** When a scan takes more than a second the agent's row gets `why: 'scan'` on its `agent-state` event (and `why: null` when it ends), and the Agents tab says "Checking the project's files". A build's own notice (one without a parent task) is drawn under the build card's head.
- **Not taken from the turn snapshots.** The private git repository of item 45 leaves out ignored files, secret-named files and files over 5 MB, and refuses a folder of more than 20,000 files, so using it here would have hidden exactly the changes the scope check and the read-only check exist to catch.

**Reason.** Stop has to work at every moment of an agent's life, and a check that silently looks at part of a folder is worse than one that says so. The scan stays a full walk because the scope check is the only thing that sees what a CLI coder wrote on its own.

**Source.** The audit finding and its verifier's verdict (12 Nov 2026). Code: `lib/agent-run.mjs` (`snapshot`, `changedBetween`, `scanGap`, `runInner`), `lib/delegate.mjs` (`runBuild`, `runReview`), `public/ext-agents.js` (`stateWord`), `public/app.js` (the build card). There is no contracts file in the project; the stream's new parts are the notice kind `agent-scan`, `why: 'scan'` on `agent-state`, `unchecked: true` on an agent's result and `clocks.scanMs` (tests only).

**Status.** Built on 12 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-agent-snapshot`, 14 tests; 11 of the first 13 failed on the old code). They cover: the same files and hashes as a one-at-a-time read; Stop during a scan of 2,000 files within a second, for a scan, an agent and a build; the time limit; a file rewritten with the same size and modified time; more than 20,000 files with a file added early; the notice and the result; and that a small folder gets no notice and still fails on a change outside the scope. The suites delegate, agent-runner, fix06-snapshots-rewind-context, pro-05-turn-snapshots, agent-roles, agent-types, agent-monitor, stab913g and fix06-agents-tools-providers pass. Measured sealed on 2,000 small files: first scan 5.3 s, second 0.28 s (under heavier load 15.9 s and 1.9 s; the old code took 30 to 60 s). The delegate tool (`lib/tools/40-delegate.mjs`) never lets a result with `unchecked: true` read as "nothing changed": its answer says "Changed files: not known. Only some of the files in the project were checked, so a change may not be listed." (with files listed: "Only some of the files in the project were checked, so another changed file may not be listed."), and its row says ", changed files not fully checked" when no file is counted (tests `build/v06/integ-core/delegate-unchecked.test.mjs`, 4). The line stays when the team runner cuts the agent's summary, which holds the scan note. Open: a large ignored folder that is not one of the skipped names (`dist`, `target`, `build`) is still walked and can reach the 20,000 limit, which now shows as a notice. Known costs: the remembered hashes trust size, modified time, change time and file number (a program that rewrites a file with the same size and puts back both times is not seen); an agent can last up to about 30 seconds longer than its limit, because the scans no longer count; the disk reads have 4 threads, so "8 at once" is about a fourfold gain.

## 102. An API key can be saved when the provider's check could not run

**Decision.** Found in the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (findings api-5 and page-setup-7): a per-token provider key was saved only when the check request (`GET /models`, or OpenRouter's `/key`) answered 2xx. A limit or an account with no credit (429), a busy provider (5xx), a key that may not list models (403), a timeout and no internet all refused the save, although none of them says the key is wrong. The check now has three outcomes (`lib/keys.mjs`, `testKey`):
- **Accepted** (2xx): saved, as before.
- **Refused**: never saved, with or without force. This is 401, any status the provider lists in `rejectStatuses` (`lib/api-providers.mjs`; `[401]` when absent, `[400, 401]` for xAI and Google AI, which have answered a wrong key with 400), an empty key, and a key with a character that no key has and that cannot be sent as a request header (a curly quote, an ellipsis, an invisible space, a control character, anything outside printable ASCII). Such a key is refused before any request, with words that name the cause ("This key has a character that cannot be part of a key ... Paste it again from the provider."), not the internet connection. A `TypeError` from `fetch` that has no cause (a request that was never built or sent) is refused too.
- **Could not be checked** (403 unless listed, 404, 429, 5xx, other 4xx, a timeout, no connection, a redirect): the key is not saved by itself. `POST /api/keys` answers as it did (`saved: false` and the plain reason) plus `canSaveAnyway: true`; the message ends with "Open Accounts to save it anyway." for the setup wizard, and `reason` is the same text without that hint. In Accounts the key stays in the box and **Save anyway** appears; it sends the same key with `force: true` (only the boolean `true` counts). The check is sent again first, so a key the provider refuses in the meantime is still not saved, and force never skips the shape check or the storage check. A key saved this way is marked "not checked yet" on its card (`checked: false` in the key list) until **Test saved key** passes; the mark is a list of key names in memory for this run (`keyChecked`, `noteChecked`), never a key, and it is not written to the state file. Save anyway is offered only for the text that was checked: editing the box takes the button away, and the focus moves to the box first.
- **A redirect is never followed** (`redirect: 'error'`), so the key is not sent on to another address. It counts as "could not be checked", because the provider addresses are fixed in the code and a redirect therefore comes from something in between (a network sign-in page, a proxy) and says nothing about the key. Going back to a hard refusal is one word in `testKey` (`verdict: 'unchecked'` to `'refused'`).
- **Test saved key** says "Could not be confirmed" for the third outcome and no longer calls a busy provider a refusal. The setup wizard does the same for its own Test, and shows a check that could not run in the neutral style.
- **A saved key is replaced only on purpose.** Without force the saved key stays; the message says so. With force the new key replaces it and the message says so.

**Reason.** A person with a correct key could not finish setup during a provider outage, offline, with a restricted key or with an account that had no credit yet, and the advice shown ("check that you copied the whole key") was wrong. Saving without asking would store typos that happen to meet a 403 or a 5xx, so the person chooses, in one click, without pasting the key again. A key with a stray character from a document or a chat is the commonest pasting mistake; it can never work, so it is not something to save anyway, and blaming the connection for it would send the person the wrong way.

**Source.** The audit findings api-5 and page-setup-7 and their verdicts (`ultra/audit/findings/`); the review of 12 Nov 2026. xAI's debugging page (read 12 Nov 2026) names 401 for an invalid key; the 400 for xAI and Google AI comes from the APIs' own answers ("Incorrect API key provided", "API key not valid"). Code: `lib/keys.mjs` (`sendable`, `statusMessage`, `testKey`, `saveKey`, `keyChecked`, `noteChecked`), `lib/api-providers.mjs` (`rejectStatuses`), `lib/routes/keys.mjs` (`saveKeyRoute`, `testKeyRoute`, `listKeys`), `public/ext-accounts-api.js` (`providerCard`), `public/ext-setup.js`.

**Status.** Built on 12 Nov 2026 and corrected after review on 13 Nov 2026, with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-key-save`, 47 tests, against a fake provider on 127.0.0.1 and a stand-in key channel; 37 of them fail on the code before the change). No real provider was called. The suites keys, openrouter, setup, fix06-keys-remove, gemini-key-route and lib-bugs pass. Open: the Anthropic key on the Claude card (`POST /api/accounts/claude/key` in `server.mjs`, `testAnthropicKey` in `lib/providers.mjs`) still refuses a key when its check fails and sends a billed request to a fixed model; it needs the same contract, the same shape check and a free check (`GET /v1/models?limit=1`). The setup wizard shows the reason and points to Accounts but has no Save anyway button of its own. The "not checked yet" mark does not survive a restart (the card then says "Saved from keychain" until Test). A key copied in from the Windows user environment (Import) is not checked for stray characters. A Test of the saved key that is still running when Save anyway replaces that key says nothing about the new key: whatever its result (passed or refused), it answers that the key changed while it was checked, clears no mark and does not show a refusal of the old key as one of the key now saved (`lib/routes/keys.mjs`; `build/v06/integ-core/keys-replaced.test.mjs`, 3 tests, the refused case fails on the earlier file). The 403 sentence no longer says the check "lists models" for OpenRouter, whose check is `GET /key` (`lib/keys.mjs`). A typo that is plain ASCII and meets a 403, a 429, a 5xx, a redirect or an outage can be saved after Save anyway; the person is told the key is not checked. The panel was tested in a small fake page, not in the real window.

## 103. A checkpoint restore can be undone, and a project file says what it leaves out

**Decision.** Two quiet ways to lose work, found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (page-work-4 and store-6), are closed.
- **A restore keeps the files first.** Before a checkpoint restore writes its first file, the files it is about to replace are kept as one new checkpoint, labelled "Before restore to" and the time of the checkpoint being restored (`keepBeforeRestore` in `lib/checkpoints.mjs`). It goes through the same queue, ledger and pruning as a checkpoint taken before an agent write, counts against the same limits (1000 checkpoints, 200 MB a project; the newest is always kept), and records the same project-relative paths, so restoring it passes the same checks as any restore: the credential folders, `checkAccess`, `.git`, the project folder, links. The answer names it (`undoId` on the `restored` event), and the Checkpoints drawer shows **Undo this restore** under the status line. The undo is a restore of that checkpoint through the same route and the same click-only card, and it keeps a copy in its turn, so an undo can be undone.
- **The card says what is kept, before Approve.** The last paragraph of the card was "A restore cannot itself be undone. The current contents of these files are replaced and are not saved first." It is now "The current contents are kept first as a new checkpoint, so this restore can be undone from the Checkpoints list." A file over 5 MB is not kept, as everywhere in checkpoints, and its line says so: "replaces the current file; not kept: over 5 MB, so this cannot be undone". A file the restore creates is not removed by an undo, and the card says that too. The old sentence stays for the two cases where it is still true: every replaced file is over 5 MB, or Plenum's own data folder is inside the project (no checkpoint is ever kept there).
- **No copy, no restore.** If the copy cannot be written (a full disk, a locked folder), or a file grew past 5 MB while the card waited, nothing is restored and the message says why. The only files replaced without a copy are the ones the card marked.
- **A failure part way keeps the way back.** A write that fails after the copy was kept ends as an error that names the file, lists what was already restored, says "The files as they were are kept as the newest checkpoint: restore it to go back", and carries the same `undoId`, so the drawer offers the Undo there as well.
- **Not done, on purpose.** The restore still writes each file in place, not through a temporary file and a rename. A rename over the target drops the file's mode and hard links, puts a `.tmp` file in the person's project for watchers and git to see, and is refused more often than a plain write on a OneDrive folder, which is where this project lives. The copy kept first already covers what the rename was for: a file cut short by a crash is whole in the "Before restore" checkpoint. An undo does not delete a file that a restore created; the card says so.
- **A project file cuts nothing silently.** `cleanMessage` clipped every message to 400,000 characters and nothing said so. The limit of one message is now 8,000,000 characters (`MAX_CONTENT`), the same on export and on import, so a file always comes back as it was written; the 64 MB limit of the whole file is what bounds an export, with the same advice as before. A message over the limit is cut to fit it and ends with "[Cut: this message was N characters long. This file keeps the first M.]", so a cut reply is never read as a whole one, and reading it in again does not cut or count it twice.
- **What a file leaves out is counted and said.** Three counts: messages cut short, older messages left out (a chat keeps 300 in the store and in a file, so an export of a saved chat leaves none out; a hand-made file can), and council or build cards kept as the text of their reply. They are written into the file (`truncated`, beside `exportedAt`), sent with the download (`X-Plenum-Export-Notes`), answered alone by `GET /api/project/export?project=ID&notes=1`, and returned by the import (`truncated`: what the file says plus what the import itself cut). The New thread panel shows one line under the buttons, for example "In this file, 2 council or build cards were saved as plain text." A file that leaves nothing out adds no line.
- **What stays.** The provider's blocks, snapshot references and computer-run records still never travel (the Move chats note now says that cards travel as text and rewind points stay on this PC). The 300-message limit of a chat is unchanged (an owner decision, store-7). The import's checks, the 64 MB limit and the credential rules of the transfer are unchanged. The download is still the plain link that `public/app.js` saves with the token.

**Reason.** Checkpoints exist to protect the person's files, and the one action in that panel that destroyed newer work had no way back; a list of up to 1000 rows that differ by a time makes the wrong row easy to pick. The export is what a person runs before a reinstall or a move to another PC, and the owner's own state holds a 2.9 MB reply that would have come back as 400,000 characters with nothing said.

**Source.** `ultra/audit/findings/page-work-4.json` and `store-6.json` (the verifiers' verdicts; the verdict's "Back up everything" option is left for an owner decision, since a whole state holds more than chats). Code: `lib/checkpoints.mjs` (`keepBeforeRestore`, `keepLine`, `planRestore`, `restorePrompt`, `applyRestore`, `CheckpointError.undoId`), `lib/routes/checkpoints.mjs` (`restoring`), `public/ext-checkpoints.js` (`afterRestore`, `restoreSummary`), `lib/routes/transfer.mjs` (`MAX_CONTENT`, `newNotes`, `contentOf`, `cleanChat`, the export and import routes), `public/ext-threads.js` (`leftOutLine`, `moveSection`).

**Status.** Built on 12 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-work-safety`, 34 tests: `restore-undo` 16, `export-notes` 11, `page` 5, `server` 2 through the real `server.mjs` on a sealed port). 21 of the first 26 failed on the code as it was. Three older suites pinned the old behaviour and were brought to the new one without loosening anything else: `build/checkpoints` (the answer of `applyRestore`, the list after a restore, the card's last paragraph), `build/v06/restore-countdown` (the same card line, with `clickOnly` asserted as well, one new test for the undo through the card, and the port can be set with `RC_PORT`), and `build/v06/pro-01-threads/import.test.mjs` (the 400,000 cut). The suites transfer 25, checkpoints 53, checkpoint-diff 10, history 28, history-all 7, fix06-transfer-history-io 14, restore-countdown 12, fix06-snapshots-rewind-context 24 and pro-05-turn-snapshots 39 pass, and so do the pro-01-threads and threads-split suites (one timing test of threads-split failed once and passed when run again). Not tried in the installed app with the owner's real state. Not done: the atomic write (see above), an undo that removes a file a restore created, and the full "Back up everything" file. Optional and not built: the page's `downloadApi` could read the `x-plenum-export-notes` header of a download, which would save the panel its second request (`&notes=1`). Costs: a restore now costs one more checkpoint each time (up to 50 files of 5 MB), which counts against the 200 MB and 1000 limits, and a restore is refused where it used to go ahead when the copy cannot be written or the target is not a plain file (both say why).

## 104. API models ride out a brief failure, and a failure says what happened and what to do

**Decision.** Found in the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (findings api-1 and api-2): a request to xAI, OpenAI, DeepSeek, Mistral, Google AI or OpenRouter was made once, so one rate limit, one overloaded server or one dropped connection ended the reply, an agent run or a council seat, and the message was the raw status with the provider's text.
- **A brief failure is tried again, but only while that is safe.** `lib/api-retry.mjs` makes the request again when it failed before any of the reply was shown or parsed: a 408, 409, 425, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504 or 529, a connection that dropped or timed out (also one that dropped after the headers and before the first piece), and an error the provider sends inside the stream before any text. At most 4 requests are made for one reply. Once any text or any piece of a tool call has arrived, nothing is sent again: a second request would repeat text and could run a tool twice. The reply then ends with "The connection to <provider> dropped after N characters of the reply. Send again to continue.", and the text so far is kept.
- **Never tried again:** 400, 401, 402, 403, 404, 413, 422, an account with no credit or quota (also when the provider reports it as a 429), a rate limit on the size of one request ("Request too large"), a name that does not resolve, a refused connection, and the refused redirect.
- **The wait.** About 1, 2 and 4 seconds with jitter, raised to what the provider asks for when that is longer: the `Retry-After` header (seconds, milliseconds or a date), else the wait its message names ("try again in 6s", Google's `retryDelay`). A wait over 60 seconds (a daily limit) is not waited out: the reply fails at once and the message names the wait. The `x-ratelimit-reset-*` headers are used only on a 429 that names no other wait, and only when they are a minute or less; they say when the whole allowance is full again, and some providers send them on every answer, so a longer value is ignored rather than treated as a wait (the audit asked for them to count whenever longer; that would have stopped a retry that works).
- **Stop still works.** The wait ends the moment the turn is stopped, quietly, and no further request is made. The wait reports activity every 5 seconds, so the idle clock does not end a turn that is waiting.
- **The person is told.** Each wait sends a notice event ("OpenAI API is busy. Trying again in 8 seconds (try 2 of 4)."). It reaches the chat for a model that has the project tools and, since `streamChat` passes `onNotice`, for a text-only model too.
- **A retried request counts once.** Retries happen inside one round: the step limit and the spend-cap check do not move, and usage is reported only for the request that answered.
- **One classifier.** `lib/api-errors.mjs` turns a failure into a kind and a sentence with a next step: the key was rejected (open Accounts), the key may not use this model, no credit left (the provider's billing page, as text), a rate limit (wait N seconds), overloaded, the model is no longer offered (pick another; Plenum never switches the model by itself), the chat is longer than the model can read (/compact or a new thread), a content filter, a request the provider could not use, a problem on the provider's side, and a connection that could not be made. The provider's own words follow, on one line, at most 300 characters, with request ids dropped. The saved key, and anything shaped like a key, is replaced by `[key]`. The billing pages are fixed addresses in the file, never read from an answer, and never opened by the app.
- **The Claude API says it the same way.** `friendly()` in `lib/providers.mjs` passes every Claude API failure that has a status or an API body through the same classifier (a stop, a refusal Plenum raised itself and an error with neither stay as they are). The client for chat makes up to 3 more tries itself (`maxRetries: 3`, 4 requests in all, as for the other providers); the key test makes none, so Test key answers at once.

**Reason.** A provider's rate limit or a dropped connection is ordinary, and it passes in seconds; ending a long agent run for it wastes the work. A person who reads "returned an error (429)" cannot tell "wait a minute" from "add credit" from "pick another model". The retry stops at the first piece of the reply because the one thing worse than a failed turn is a tool that ran twice.

**Source.** Audit findings api-1 and api-2 and their verdicts (`ultra/audit/api.findings.json`, `ultra/audit/findings/api-1.json`, `api-2.json`). Code: `lib/api-retry.mjs` (`withRetry`, `retryDelay`), `lib/api-errors.mjs` (`classify`, `classifyError`, `waitFromHeaders`, `resetHintFromHeaders`), `lib/openai-compat.mjs` (`streamRound`, `roundAttempt`, `failure`, `unreachable`), `lib/providers.mjs` (`friendly`, `claudeClient`, `testAnthropicKey`, the `streamOpenAICompat` call in `streamChat`).

**Status.** Built on 12 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-api-resilience`, 36 tests: classify 12, resilience 19, e2e 5; 14 of the first 15 adapter tests fail on the code before the change). The suites openai-compat 21, openrouter 28, cost 27, images 23 and fix06-agents-tools-providers 57 pass. Two assertions of `build/openai-compat/openai-compat.test.mjs` that pinned the old raw texts were updated. Not done: the computer-use drivers (`lib/computer/generic.mjs`, `lib/computer/openai.mjs`) have their own request code and still show their own failure texts (they could use `errorFor(classify(...))`, with no retry added without a look at the step loop, because a retried step must not repeat an action); the key checks (`lib/keys.mjs`, `testAnthropicKey`) keep their texts. A request has no time limit of its own beyond Node's (five minutes without a byte); that was not part of these findings. Known costs: a turn that meets a rate limit can take longer before it fails (up to three waits, each at most 60 seconds when the provider asks for that; about 7 seconds in all when it names none; Stop ends it at once); a 5xx or a timed-out connection that the provider had in fact started to process may be billed twice, and no tool can run twice because nothing is retried after the first piece of a reply. The billing pages were checked by reading, not by opening them. Tests of other packages that pin texts this item changed: `build/v06/openrouter-roles` (the council's old sentence, now item 98) and `build/v06/stab913-8` (source lines of `lib/providers.mjs` and `lib/cursor.mjs`); `build/attachments` fails on the model discovery at server start, which is wave 0's.

## 105. The setup guide shows how to install a missing CLI, and a sign-in is noticed by itself

**Decision.** Found in the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (j-firstrun-4, j-firstrun-5, docs-3, page-setup-2, page-setup-5), and the owner said the same day that the sign-in flows confused him. On a clean PC the setup guide showed six rows that said "Not installed. Install it, then press Check again." with no command, and after Sign in nothing in Plenum changed until the person pressed Check again or Refresh.
- **Install steps in the row.** A row whose CLI is missing now shows the steps: the command as code with a Copy button, for Codex (`npm install -g @openai/codex`), Gemini CLI (the command the server builds for this computer, else `npm install -g @google/gemini-cli`), Cursor, Grok Build and Antigravity (the Windows and the macOS or Linux command, the same ones the Accounts cards show). Then "Then press Check again. If it still shows Not installed, restart Plenum.", because a CLI installed while Plenum runs is not always found until a restart. Muse Code has no install command in the repository (Open items), so its row says "Install Muse Code from its publisher, then press Check again." and shows no command. The Claude row keeps its own sentence and gains an "Add an Anthropic key" button that opens step 3. The steps are built once and kept on a repaint, so Check again does not stack a second block.
- **One place for the commands.** The steps are drawn by `MuseUI.cliInstallBlock(id, info)` in `public/ext-accounts-cli.js`, beside the commands the cards already had. The wizard looks it up when it paints; without it (the panel did not load) the old sentence stays. The Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI and Muse Code cards in Accounts use the same block (`installSteps` in `public/app.js`) in place of the old sentences ("Install Muse Code first (see the README)" and the plain npm line). No route changed for this.
- **A sign-in is watched.** `MuseUI.watchSignIn` reads the sign-in status after Sign in was pressed: every 2 seconds, every 4 after a minute, one read at a time, for 3 minutes; at once when the window gets focus or the page becomes visible again; not at all while the page is hidden. It stops when the sign-in shows, when the wizard, the drawer or the Settings page is closed, and after the 3 minutes. If the 3 minutes end while the person is away (in the terminal or the browser), nothing is decided: it reads once more when they come back, so a sign-in that took longer is still noticed. It only reads. A sign-in still starts only on a press of the button.
- **In the wizard.** The row says "Waiting for sign-in" and "Finish the sign-in in the window that opened. Plenum checks every few seconds and shows it here."; the button stays usable as "Sign in again". When the sign-in shows, the row turns to "Signed in" and a toast says "Signed in to <name>.". After 3 minutes the note says "Plenum has not seen a sign-in yet. Press Sign in to try again, or Check again if you have finished.". The read that used to run straight after the press is gone: the sign-in cannot be there yet, and for Antigravity that read fixed "Not signed in" in place for two minutes. Several waiting rows share one watch, and the watch reads only the route its rows need. A wizard that is opened again later reads the status again (when it is older than 10 seconds) instead of showing the old one.
- **On the cards in Accounts.** The pill says "Waiting for sign-in"; when the sign-in shows, that one card is built again in its place and a toast says so. The rest of Accounts is not drawn again, so a key being typed in another card stays. A card that is drawn again within the 3 minutes (Refresh, or Settings closed and opened) goes on waiting with the same deadline. The Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI and Muse Code cards watch through `watchCardSignIn` in `public/app.js`; a card that is already signed in is not watched (Sign in again), because the accounts would say "signed in" before the new sign-in had begun.
- **Fresh answers.** `GET /api/accounts/cli` answers from what the server last saw (item 91), and Antigravity is otherwise read only every few minutes. So one read in 8 seconds of a watch on Cursor, Grok Build and Antigravity, and Check again in the wizard after such a sign-in was started, use `?refresh=1`, which the route already has. The server halves are in: `antigravitySignIn` notes a pending sign-in so that a read during it is not kept for 2 minutes (`lib/antigravity.mjs`), and `cursorSignIn` and the refresh forget Cursor's 20-second status cache (`forgetCursorStatus` in `lib/cursor.mjs`, called from `lib/routes/cli-accounts.mjs`).
- **Smaller things.** The welcome sentence names Antigravity. The empty summary of step 4 has two buttons, "Add an API key" (step 3) and "Open Accounts". The Antigravity card says "Use the Google account..." where it said "Sign in with" (no card text says "Sign in with").

**Reason.** A person who has just signed in and still reads "Not signed in" signs in again or gives up, and a row that says "install it" without saying how stops a new person at step 2. Polling is bounded (3 minutes, one read at a time, none while hidden) so it cannot become a standing load, and it never acts: Ask first, the provider-terms strip and the rule that a sign-in stays with its CLI are untouched.

**Source.** The audit findings named above and their verdicts (`ultra/audit`). Code: `public/ext-accounts-cli.js` (`cliInstallBlock`, `commandRow`, `watchSignIn`, `watchCard`, `resumeWatch`, `redrawCard`), `public/ext-setup.js` (`signIn`, `startWatch`, `readWaiting`, `noticed`, `paintInstall`, `paintCli`, `showKeys`, `donePane`, `loadStatus`, `draw`), `public/app.js` (`watchCardSignIn`, `installSteps`), `lib/antigravity.mjs`, `lib/cursor.mjs`, `lib/routes/cli-accounts.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 12 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-setup-guide`, 32 of 32): `wizard.test.mjs` (17) and `cards.test.mjs` (11) run the two files in a vm with a stub page and a fake clock; 23 of the first 27 fail on the files as they were. `page.test.mjs` (4) loads the real page from a sealed server in a hidden window, through a proxy that answers the sign-in routes itself (no terminal opens, no sign-in is started): the wizard shows the install steps drawn as code, the Codex row turns to Signed in about 3.5 seconds after the press with nothing else pressed, the Cursor card does the same in Accounts while the other cards keep a mark put on them, and the console has no error. The suites setup 19, cli-04-cursor-page 26, grok-05-page 48, settings-page 18, legal-page 30 and fix06-page-ux 63 passed with it. Not built: an "Install for me" button (an owner decision: it would run a script downloaded from the vendor and needs its own route and a confirmation; only commands with Copy exist); Muse Code has no install command (Open items). The `.cli-cmd-row` rule in `public/ext-accounts-cli.css` now puts Copy beside the command in the cards. Not tested with a real sign-in: every sign-in in the tests is a stub. Grok Build reports a sign-in only as "a sign-in file is there"; if `grok login` writes that file late or under another name, the row waits its 3 minutes and then says it has not seen a sign-in. The page forces a fresh read once in 8 seconds for Antigravity (each starts an `agy acp` handshake, about 12 seconds on the owner's PC) and Cursor, bounded to the 3 minutes after a press of Sign in.

## 106. Panels act on the thread they were opened for: Pipelines, Running now, Review, and the stop key on the shortcut sheet

**Decision.** Found in the overhaul audit on 12 Nov 2026. Four panels were written when a project had one chat, and each still named only the project. With threads (item 62 and after) a request that names no thread is answered for whichever thread is the active one when it arrives, so the panel could act on another chat than the one it showed.
- **Pipelines name their thread.** The drawer takes the thread on screen when Run is pressed and sends it with the run, with Stop (`/api/cancel`) and with Keep in chat, in the shape the chat itself uses (`{ project, thread }`; Main's id is the project's id). Before, Stop sent the project alone, and the server then stopped the project's Main run when Main was running, whatever thread the pipeline ran in; the pipeline itself ended only because the page closed its own stream. Keep in chat is refused with "Switch back to the chat this pipeline ran in, then keep the reply." when another thread (or project) is on screen, and otherwise writes into the run's thread. The server already read `thread` on all three routes, so nothing changed there.
- **Running now rows open the thread.** A run's scope is its thread's id, which is not a project id, so the sidebar rows of a thread's run did nothing (the Agents tab's Open project button worked only through a wrapper in `ext-threads.js`). `MuseUI.board.openScope` now takes `{ scope, projectId }` (a bare scope id still works): it presses the project's own button in the project list, waits until that project is the active one (up to 10 seconds; the switch is answered by the server first), and then switches to the row's chat whenever another one shows. That holds for Main as well: a project comes up on the thread it showed last, so a Main row (and the Agents tab's Open project for a Main run) switches to Main by the project's id, as `open()` in `ext-threads.js` does. A newer click wins over one that is still waiting. Every way it can fail says so in one line: "That thread is closed.", "That project is no longer in the list.", "The project did not open. Try again." (the switch was refused or took longer than 10 seconds) and "The chat did not open. Try again." (the thread switch answered but another chat still shows).
- **A finished run's dot goes when its own chat is seen.** The dot was cleared by comparing the run's scope with the active project, which is never true for a thread, so Done rows of thread runs stayed until 30 newer runs pushed them out. A run is now seen when its own thread (or Main for a Main run) is on screen: when it ends there, when the thread is opened from the row, and on any later redraw that shows it. Another thread of the same project on screen does not count, and a click on a row clears the dot only after its chat is the one that shows (a switch that failed leaves it). The one exception is a thread that was closed: its row says so and goes, because that chat can never be shown again.
- **Review follows the thread.** The pane is keyed by project and thread. It reads the changes again when the thread on screen changes (also when another window changes it), names the thread on the changes, file and hunk requests from what it stored when it read the list, and names Main too (by the project's id), so no request depends on which thread is active at that moment. Stage, Unstage, Revert and "Send N comments" are refused with one line ("These changes belong to another chat. The pane is reading the chat on screen, so try again in a moment.") when the list on screen was read for another chat, and nothing is sent. Comment drafts are kept per thread; Main keeps the old per-project key, so drafts written before this are Main's drafts.
- **The stop key is on the Ctrl+/ sheet.** In the desktop app the sheet has a row "Stop a computer or browser run from any app." with the key as the start card names it: the label comes from the server (`/api/computer/probe`, `hotkey.label`: the person's own `driveStopKey`, Control+Option+Esc on a Mac). It takes the label from the probe that `ext-computer.js` already reads at load (the page event `plenum:computer-probe`) and makes its own read, four seconds after the page starts, only when that did not arrive and after a reconnect. A browser tab without the desktop app gets no row and makes no request. The row is added through the list the other panels already use (`window.MuseShortcutRows`); no new registry.
- **The other panels that named no thread now do.** Quick compare takes the thread on screen when a compare starts, sends it with the run and with Keep, and refuses Keep when another thread is on screen (`public/ext-compare.js`); the Git panel names the thread on screen and reads again when it changes (`public/ext-git.js`); the Checkpoints drawer names it on its list and its restore (`public/ext-checkpoints.js`).
- **The server's `cancelTargets` is not made strict, on purpose.** The verdict's hardening ("with a project and no thread, never Main unless Main is the active thread") would make the Agents tab's Stop on a Main run stop the active thread's run instead. If the server is to be made strict later, every caller must first name the thread (`ext-agents.js` `stopRun`: `MuseUI.stopRun(run.projectId ?? run.scope, run.projectId && run.scope ? run.scope : undefined)`), and `v06/pro-01-threads`, `v06/pro-03-agents-board` and `v06/agent-monitor` run after both changes.

**Reason.** A Stop that stops another chat's agent in the middle of an edit, a "Waiting for you" row that does not take the person to the card, and review comments about one thread's code sent to another thread's model are all the same fault: the panel and the server disagreed about which chat was meant. Naming the thread on every request removes the disagreement at its source instead of guessing it on the server. The stop key belongs on the list of keys because it is the one key that matters when a model has the mouse.

**Source.** The audit findings page-work-5, page-work-2, page-work-3 and docs-6 (12 Nov 2026) with the verifiers' verdicts. Corrections that held: docs-6 was mostly refuted (the sheet already lists the board, thread and split-view keys through `window.MuseShortcutRows`; only the stop key was missing); page-work-2 and page-work-3 were lowered to medium; for page-work-3 the hunk actions were already guarded by the diff hash, and the real harm was the comments. Found while building: the Agents tab's Open project already worked for threads through `wrapBoard` in `ext-threads.js`, so the dead control was the sidebar row, and the page's state names Main by the project's id (`activeThread` is never empty for an open project), which is why Main is named on the requests too. The review of the package (13 Nov 2026) found that a Main row did not switch to Main when the project showed another thread, and that its dot was cleared all the same; both are corrected. Code: `public/ext-pipelines.js` (`threadPart`, `startRun`, `stopRun`, `keepReply`), `public/ext-board.js` (`onScreen`, `projectOfScope`, `projectIsActive`, `openScope`, the `onRender` hook), `public/ext-agents.js` (`openProjectButton`), `public/ext-review.js` (`view.thread`, `view.scope`, `scopeKey`, `threadId`, `reload`, `act`, `showAll`, `sendComments`), `public/ext-drive.js` (`loadStopKey`, `readStopKeySoon`), `public/ext-computer.js`.

**Status.** Built on 12 Nov 2026 and corrected after review on 13 Nov 2026, with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-panel-fixes`, 49 tests in 6 files, all pass). Four files run the panel scripts in a vm with a fake DOM (38 tests; 31 of them fail on the files as they were, the other 7 are the cases that must not change). `server-contract.test.mjs` (5 tests) checks on a real sealed server what the page now leans on: Main is named by the project id (also on the thread switch), the review, cancel and keep routes act on the named thread and refuse one that is not open, and the probe route carries the key's label. `page.test.mjs` (6 tests) runs the real page in a hidden Electron window against a sealed server with two projects and a second thread. The suites pipelines 83, shortcuts 13, pro-03-agents-board 62, agent-monitor 34, pro-04-review-pane 54, pro-01-threads 79, threads-split 123 and fix06-page-ux 63 passed with it. Open: Stop in the Pipelines drawer was checked through the request it sends and the server's cancel route, not against a pipeline that is still running in the real page (the offline model ends at once); two different rows clicked within the time a project switch takes: the later click does not win, and the person can end on a third chat (`public/ext-board.js`, minor); a project whose button is not in the project list when a row is clicked returns without a message; a thread shown only in the second half of split view does not count as "on screen" for the dot; for four seconds after the page starts (and when the probe fails) the sheet has no stop key row, and if the key cannot be registered when a run starts the start card says so then, not the sheet; a thread closed while its Review list is on screen answers 404 until the pane reads again. `build/v06/drive-core/server-e2e.test.mjs` compared the written project path (through the test tree's junction) with the real path the server stores; it now resolves both sides (`sameFolder`). The owner would see a project listed under its real `D:` path after adding a folder through a moved path.

## 107. Account checks that tell the truth and answer in time, a server that one stray fault no longer ends, and a browser drive that ends with the reply

**Decision.** Found in the overhaul audit on 12 Nov 2026, built and corrected after review on 13 Nov 2026. Four faults in the server, fixed together because they are all in `server.mjs`.
- **A signed-out Codex is not "Signed in".** `GET /api/accounts` looked for the words "logged in" in what `codex login status` printed, and Codex prints "Not logged in" when signed out. `codexLoginState` (`lib/accounts.mjs`) now reads the answer: "Not logged in" is signed out whatever else is there; exit code 0 with "Logged in" is signed in; any other failure exit is signed out, with the CLI's own first line; no exit code (the CLI did not answer, or could not be started) is not known, and so is exit code 0 with words Plenum does not know. Not known is `loggedIn: null` with `status` "Plenum could not check Codex just now. Press Refresh." and `reason: 'timed_out'`. It is never `false`: a check that failed says nothing about the account. The status line goes to the page, so anything in it that is shaped like a key is taken out.
- **One slow CLI does not hold Accounts or the setup guide, and is not shown as signed out.** The route ran the provider status and a fresh `claude auth status`, and after them `codex login status`, each with a 15 second limit. Now the three reads start together and the route waits 3 seconds for each (`softCheck` in `lib/accounts.mjs`). A check that is slower is not ended: it goes on in the background and its answer is there for the next read, and for this read too if it comes in while the route still waits for something else. Meanwhile the route says what is known: the last real answer if it is younger than 5 minutes, marked `stale: true`; for Claude Code also a "signed in" from the provider status, marked stale; and "not known" (`null`, with the sentence) only when nothing is known. A CLI that ran into its 15 second limit gave no answer: nothing is kept from it, so it never takes the place of a good answer and is never served as "signed out" (`claudeCliStatus` in `lib/claude-cli.mjs` answers `loggedIn: null` when `claude auth status` has no exit code, and the check is also timed in `server.mjs`). One check runs at a time, and readers who arrive meanwhile join it. An answer of "signed in" is kept for 20 seconds, any other for 3 (that is the one a person is about to change); `?fresh=1` asks again whatever is kept (the Refresh of the cards sends it), and the Codex and Claude sign-in routes drop what is kept. Claude Code is asked only on its CLI route. Both checks are started once in the background 1.5 seconds after the start, when the License Agreement is accepted, so that the first open has an answer.
- **The provider status for that page** is a fresh read when it comes within 3 seconds, else the background snapshot of item 91 (never softCheck's own older answer). Only when there is no snapshot yet, in the first seconds after a start, does the route wait up to 3 seconds more for either, and then it answers 503 "Plenum is still checking your accounts. Press Refresh in a moment." It never waits for as long as a CLI that hangs. With a hung Claude CLI the first read takes about 6 seconds (3 + 3), because a healthy CLI that needs 5 seconds then gets its right answer on the very first open.
- **A fault nobody caught no longer ends the server.** Nothing listened for `unhandledRejection` or `uncaughtException`, so one forgotten `.catch` in any of some 180 modules ended the process with every run in it. Now a rejection nobody handled is written to the log and the server goes on. An error nobody caught is written to the log and the server goes on too, but three within a minute mean the process can no longer be trusted: the runs are stopped the way a quit stops them, the state is saved and flushed, and the process ends with code 1, which the desktop app's supervisor (item 91) answers by starting the server again, three times in five minutes at most, and then it asks the person. It ends within 8 seconds whatever happens. Before the server has started (until the last line of `server.mjs` has run) an error nobody caught is still a failed start: the process says why and ends with code 1, as it always did. The code is 1 and not 75, against the verifier's proposal: 75 means "restart for an update", reloads the page and is never counted, so a server that keeps failing would restart for ever without a word.
- **A mistake inside a route is said in plain words and written down.** The request handler answered every error without a status as 400 with the error's own text, so a mistake in Plenum reached the person as "Cannot read properties of undefined", and nothing was logged. Now a TypeError, ReferenceError or RangeError, or a thrown value that is no error, is answered 500 with "Plenum hit an unexpected problem. Try again. If it happens again, look for E-xxxxxx in the file server.log in Plenum's data folder." and the log line begins, after its time, with that id, then the method, the path without its query and the top of the stack. The same failure of the same route within ten seconds keeps its id. Everything made on purpose is as before: an error with a status keeps status and text; a plain `Error` keeps the 400 and its sentence, and so does a body that is not JSON; a web address that cannot be read and a network failure of `fetch` are TypeErrors by nature and stay 400. A 5xx made on purpose is noted in the log as one line, once in ten minutes at most.
- **What reaches the log.** One helper (`faultText` and `redactSecrets` in `lib/accounts.mjs`): the message and six stack lines at most, 3,000 characters at most. Taken out: the launch token; the values of the key variables in the environment; the keys saved in the app, which are in the key store's memory and not in the environment, read again before every line (`refreshKeySecrets`); anything shaped like a provider key, a GitHub, Tailscale or Google token or a signed web token; any 64 hex digits; whatever follows a name such as authorization, bearer, api-key or token; the name and password inside a web address; the query of a web address; and the value that an "is an invalid header value" error quotes. Never a header or a body. The same line is written once in ten seconds.
- **A listener of a desktop message may fail.** `lib/shell.mjs` ran a listener inside try/catch, which does not catch a promise that rejects later (the server's `shutdown` listener is async). The promise is now watched and a failure is reported through `onShellFault`, which the server sets to its log writer.
- **A save that waits is written before the process ends** (the server half of item 95): after an applied update (`restartSoon`), when a launcher went away (the stdin end of `MUSE_CONSOLE_CHILD`), and in the stop after three faults. `store.flush()` never rejects and gives up after 2 seconds.
- **The browser drive ends with the reply, except a hand-over that waits for the person.** A browser drive started at the first `browser_*` call and ended only by Stop, the turn's signal, or 60 seconds without a call. So for a minute after every reply that used the browser the "Model is driving" badge, the drive bar and the stop key stayed on, the project could not start a computer run, site permission prompts were denied and the phone was refused, although nothing was running. `endBrowserRun(ctx)` (`lib/browser-tools.mjs`) is now called in the `finally` of every turn, beside `agentRun.close()`, inside try/catch so that it can never touch the reply. It finds the run without making one; between the calls of one turn the drive stays up as before; a call that is still running is left to return and the drive stops the moment it has (or after the idle limit if it never returns); a call that waits on a card or at a pause the person made is not waited for, and the card is closed the way Stop closes it; a stop by the person or by the turn's signal keeps its reason; a browser tool called for that reply afterwards is refused with "This reply is over, so the browser is off until the next message." and starts no drive. The turn's signal is never aborted for it. One pause is left alone: when the model was refused a password, code or card field, the drive is paused by the model and the model is told to ask the person, which it does by ending its reply. That drive stays paused, with its note, the "Type it for me" box and Resume, which the pane shows only while such a pause is live. When the person presses Resume, nothing is driving, so the drive ends at once and the badge does not come back; Stop, the drive's own pause limit and the next message of the thread end it too. When the drive of an ended reply stops, the field it left waiting is forgotten, so it does not sit in the bridge for its 30 minutes with no way to use it.
- Route modules now get `exclusive` (the map of live project-wide operations), which the restore route of the dated copies (item 111) reads.

**Reason.** Each of these is something the owner would meet in ordinary use: a green "Signed in" on a Codex that then fails its first run, a blank Accounts page while one CLI hangs, a Claude Code that reads "Not signed in" on the first open after a start because its CLI needed four seconds, a server that is gone because of one stray promise, and a badge that says a model is driving when the reply ended a minute ago. The soft limit was chosen over ending the CLI at 3 seconds because on a loaded PC a healthy CLI can take longer than that; ended early it would never answer, left to finish it answers the next read. "What is known" was chosen over "not known" for a slow CLI because the pages read a sign-in for truth, and a signed-in account shown as signed out sends the person to sign in again for nothing. The hand-over is kept because it is the guard beside the floor: the person types the secret through the pane, and that only works while the pause is live. The last item of the floor is why the badge is not left on after a reply otherwise.

**Source.** The audit findings page-setup-1, j-firstrun-1, page-setup-6, server-3 and browser-computer-2 (12 Nov 2026) and the verifiers' verdicts (j-firstrun-1, page-setup-6 and server-3 lowered to medium; server-3 corrected: a plain Error must keep its 400, which is kept). The review of 13 Nov 2026 (two major and five minor issues). The store-save package's report (flush, item 95) and the dated-copies report (`exclusive`). Code: `server.mjs`, `lib/accounts.mjs`, `lib/browser-tools.mjs`, `lib/shell.mjs`, `lib/claude-cli.mjs` (`claudeCliStatus`), `public/app.js` (the Codex and Claude Code cards).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 and corrected after review the same night, with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-server-core`, 59 of 59: `accounts-unit` 16, `accounts-route` 7, `accounts-claude` 6, `faults` 7, `shell` 3, `browser-end` 16, `flush-exit` 4; on the files as committed 4 of the 59 pass). The suites wiring 28, setup 19, fix06-server-core 19, server-wiring-and-bugs 29, ultra-w0a-status 41, brw-03-model-drives-browser 88, fix06-browser-floor 18 and plenum-mcp 73 pass with it. Codex's and Claude Code's answers are from stand-ins; the real CLIs were not run. The restart after exit code 1 is the supervisor's own tested rule; it was not tried in the packaged app. The browser pane in the tests is a stand-in bridge. In the page, the Codex and Claude Code cards in Accounts say "Sign-in unknown" for a `null`, and their Refresh asks again (`?fresh=1`). The setup guide's rows now read a `null` as an unknown sign-in (`cliState` in `public/ext-setup.js`), its Check again asks for a fresh read (`?fresh=1`), and the installer's `spawn` in `desktop/handlers/app-update.cjs` (`runInstaller`) has `windowsHide: true`. Open: Accounts and the setup guide still wait for all their requests before they paint (page-setup-6, page half), and a 503 "still checking" carries no automatic second try; nothing in the page or the menus opens the log (the recovery box of the desktop shell has Open log; the 500 sentence names `server.log`). Risks: with a Claude CLI that hangs, Accounts opened right after a start shows "Plenum is still checking your accounts" for up to 15 seconds, and the setup guide's first-run check does not open on that visit; Claude Code's "signed in" is kept for 20 seconds, so a sign-out made outside Plenum shows that much late unless Refresh asks again; after one or two errors nobody caught the server keeps serving, and its state could be wrong in a way nobody sees (three in a minute end it); redaction is by shape and by known value, so a key of a shape nobody has seen that is not saved in Plenum would pass, and words after "token", "secret" or "password" are taken out even when they are ordinary words; after `endBrowserRun`, browser tools are refused for that turn's signal, so a later follow-up turn on the same signal that may use the browser needs a signal of its own; a password hand-over holds the project's browser drive (paused) until Resume, Stop, the 60 minute pause limit or the next message.

## 108. Long chats open and stream without holding the window, and a failed send shows one card

**Decision.** Found on 12 Nov 2026 by the audit of the page and by the hands-on testers. Four changes in the page.
- **A reply that is arriving is drawn a part at a time.** `mdSplit(text)` in `public/markdown.js` gives the same HTML as `md(text)` in two parts: the blocks no later text can change, and the open end. The page keeps the first part on the page as it is and parses only from where it ended when more text arrives, at most once in 90 ms (the first text of a turn at once; a slow drawing waits four times its own cost, up to a second). When a turn closes (the run ends, or a tool step or a card follows it) it is drawn to its last character at once. What the page shows is exactly what `md()` makes of the whole reply. A block counts as settled once the block after it starts two lines or more before the last line, because the last line may still grow and a table is known by the line after its first.
- **The HTML of a reply is made once, and a very long message is drawn in part.** A redraw of the chat (a send, the end of a run, a state change) used to parse every reply again. The page now keeps the HTML by the text it was made from (48 million characters at most, oldest dropped first). A message longer than 100,000 characters shows its first 24,000 characters (cut at a line end, unless that would leave less than half of them) and under it "This message is long (2.8 MB). The first 23 KB is shown." with **Show all**. Show all parses the rest away from the page and puts it on the page in pieces of about 200,000 characters of the text, with a pause after each piece and whenever the parsing has held the window for 12 ms; the line shows the percentage meanwhile. Only the drawing is cut: the stored message, Copy, Insert, Edit, Regenerate and Send to use the whole text, and `MuseUI.turnText(article)` gives a panel the whole text of a turn that shows only its first part.
- **What was opened stays open.** A message opened with Show all is drawn whole again when the chat is redrawn, and so are the long replies of the run that ended last in a thread (they arrived whole and must not shrink under the reader). The line under such a message says "All of it is shown." and offers **Show less**, which folds it for good. A redraw draws up to 300,000 characters of the opened messages at once, the newest first (the end of the chat is where the reader is); what is left follows by itself, in pieces. The opened messages are kept while they hold 1,000,000 characters or fewer together; past that the one opened longest ago folds at the next redraw, and one message longer than that is never kept: its line says "All of it is shown for now. It folds again when the chat changes, because this much text slows typing."
- **One error card for a failed send.** The page keeps a live error as a note so that it shows at once and stays if the refresh fails; the server also stores it with the reply, and the saved reply draws it. A note whose words are the error of a reply saved after the last question (or the error a saved council card shows) is now left out when the chat is drawn (`visibleNotes`), in the chat on screen and in the split view. Notes are never deleted: a refused send, a dropped stream and a failed hop have no saved copy and still show, and the split view's send still reads them to know how its run ended.
- **One odd event no longer ends the view of a run.** An event that is not valid JSON, or that the page cannot draw, is logged and skipped, and the reading goes on; at the end one plain line says "Plenum could not draw part of this reply as it came in. The reply is saved, and the chat now shows it as it was saved." A piece of text with no text in it adds nothing (it used to add the word "undefined"). A connection that drops says "The connection to Plenum was lost while the reply was coming in. What had arrived is kept in the chat. Press Retry to put your message back, then send it again." (or "Your message was not sent, because Plenum could not be reached. ..." when the request never got an answer) in place of the browser's "network error" or "Failed to fetch". The reader is let go when the stream breaks or is stopped. The events channel uses the same reader, so one bad frame no longer makes it reconnect.

Smaller changes that came with the package: typing in the message box no longer makes the page lay out the chat twice for every keystroke; the artifact panel reads each code block once, not every block on every change of the chat; the rows under the turns are not looked at again for changes inside a live reply; each reply has one Insert (the one in the row under it; the second one beside Send to is gone); a Settings card no longer repeats its group's name, and a Settings section that registers while the page is open gets its card on the page at once (the other sections are not drawn again; the first section of a group that is not on the page yet shows at the next opening); the default Skeptic seat and a new Muse seat are `muse:muse-wonder-1.0` (item 98), and if Muse Code's own list ever drops that too a new seat takes the first Muse model the list offers (a saved council keeps the model it names); the Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI and Muse Code cards in Accounts show a finished sign-in by themselves and show the install steps with Copy (item 105); the Codex and Claude Code cards say "Sign-in unknown" with "Plenum could not check Codex just now. Press Refresh." when the check of the CLI did not answer in time (never "Not signed in" to a person who is signed in), and their Refresh makes the server ask the CLI again (`/api/accounts?fresh=1`, item 107); a build's own notice is drawn under the build card's head (item 101).

**Reason.** With the owner's own chat (replies of 90 KB to 2.9 MB) the window froze for ten seconds or more on every open, send and end of a run, and typing lagged for as long as the chat was open. A long reply was parsed again from its first character on every frame, so the work grew with the square of its length and Stop, typing and scrolling answered late exactly where people read. Every failed send looked broken twice over, with two Retry buttons that did the same thing. Measured on this PC under that night's load, before and after, in a hidden window against a sealed server (`page.test.mjs`, `CHAT_RENDER_BEFORE=1`): the owner-shaped chat was on screen after 23 to 27 s and now after 1 to 2 s; a redraw took 10.7 to 21.6 s and now takes 0.2 to 0.7 s; the chat held 220,841 nodes and now holds 9,423; a keystroke cost 617 to 664 ms and now costs 19 to 35 ms; a 120 KB reply fed in 2 KB pieces took 12.7 to 18.3 s with 57 to 71 long tasks (worst 507 to 658 ms) and now takes 0.8 to 1 s with 5 (worst 105 to 229 ms). Opened messages are kept because the owner's replies average 159 KB: without that every long reply shrank to its first part the moment its run ended, and a message a person had opened folded on every send. They are kept by size because every node on the page costs on each keystroke and redraw: with the 2.9 MB reply all on the page a keystroke costs 470 to 640 ms.

**Source.** Audit findings page-core-1, page-core-2, j-chat-1, j-firstrun-2, j-chat-2 and page-core-7, with the verifiers' verdicts, and the review of the package (13 Nov). Code: `public/markdown.js` (`mdSplit`, `clean`, the marks in `blocks`), `public/app.js` (`visibleNotes`, `noteNodes`, `mdOnce`, `longFirst`, `appendMarkdown`, `longRow`, `longRowState`, `longStep`, `runLong`, `finishReopen`, `markStreamed`, `setOpened`, `fillMarkdown`, `fillPlain`, `MuseUI.turnText`, `readSSE`, `streamError`, `runStream`, `turnRenderer` with `draw`, `paint` and `flush`, `autosize`, `newSeat`, `watchCardSignIn`, `installSteps`, `codexCard`, `claudeCliCard`, `renderAccounts`, `settingsRegister`, `settingsAddLate`), `public/ext-turns.js` (the observer), `public/ext-artifacts.js` (`describe`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-chat-render`: `md-split.test.mjs` 9 of 9 and `page.test.mjs` 18 of 18, which runs the probes on the original four files too and checks that each fault was there). Where the verdicts and the code disagreed: j-firstrun-2 asked for the filter in the thread status helper as well, j-chat-2 said the split view's send must still answer "failed" after a failed send; the code shows j-chat-2 is right, so that helper reads the notes unfiltered. The incremental drawing ends in exactly the HTML of a full `md()` (tested at every prefix of a corpus and of 400 random texts), so no second full parse is paid at the end. Two older tests were brought to the new code (`build/v06/server-wiring-and-bugs/page-w18-fix.test.mjs` and `build/v06/cli-04-cursor-page/page-static.test.mjs`); the suites that open Settings or touch long messages pass (page-fixes 36, fix06-page-ux 63, ultra-w0b-page 40, pro-01-threads 79, threads-split 123, art-01-artifacts 44, council-seats 8). Of the stylesheet rules this item asked for, the rule for the line under a long message (`.long-more`) is in `public/style.css`, and the phone layout's "Copy reply" and "Report this reply" in `public/ext-phone.js` use `MuseUI.turnText(turn)` first. The two containment rules, `.turn { content-visibility: auto; contain-intrinsic-size: auto 160px; }` and `.chat { contain: layout style; }`, were tried on the live page on 13 Nov 2026 and left out: with `content-visibility` the chat no longer opened at its end (the page test of this item failed there) and nothing got faster (a redraw took 595 ms against 541 ms; with `contain` alone 567 ms against 541 ms, and a keystroke 29.8 ms against 29.4 ms). The optional rule for `pre` and `table` was not tried. So what is left of the redraw, typing and Show all cost stays, and the times below are those of the page as it is (Show all on a message of several MB took 11.6 to 18.7 s on this PC under load, in about 15 pieces, the window answering between them; a keystroke cost 470 to 640 ms while it was all on the page; a chat of saved council cards redrew in 0.7 to 1.2 s; each opened message of 260 KB added about 17,000 nodes), and the chat must still open at its end with the rules on. Open: (1) the optional rule above, and a measurement with the rules on. (2) Reusing the nodes of unchanged messages in place, so that a redraw costs nothing, needs one "chat redrawn" hook first (`ext-archive.js` and `ext-quota.js` take the chat's stream being added again as their sign that the chat changed). (3) Paging of `/api/state` (a later wave). (4) "Verdict based on A of N seats" on the council card: the saved card does not carry the two numbers. (5) Accounts is not painted group by group as its requests return (they are bounded now, about 3 s and 4 s at worst). Two Codex tests of other suites (`build/chatmode`, `build/v06/stab58-10`) fail on one start of the stand-in `codex.cmd` too many; the likely cause (not proved) is that the account check of item 107 starts `codex login status` beside a chat turn: the tests should count only the starts that carry a prompt, or the check must not start the CLI beside a chat turn.
