The opened state is kept by the message's length and its first and last 60 characters, not by an id: two long messages equal in all three would open and fold together. The kept HTML uses up to 48 million characters of memory. `mdSplit` relies on how `blocks()` in `markdown.js` ends a block; `md-split.test.mjs` must stay green after any change of the parser.

## 109. Switching the phone link off never touches another program's Tailscale share

**Decision.** Switching the phone link off takes down only what Plenum itself set in Tailscale (`tailscale serve --https=443 off`, and only if this Plenum set the serve up). Plenum never runs `tailscale serve reset`. No test can reach the real Tailscale program: every sealed run sets `PLENUM_TAILSCALE_CLI=off` (`ultra/seal.sh`, `build/battery3.sh`, `ultra/sweep.sh`, `build/mobile/run.mjs` and the sealed-server helper of the mobile tests), and with that value `locateTailscale` answers "not installed", so nothing is looked for and nothing runs.

**Reason.** `serve reset` removes every share on the computer, also those of other programs. On 12 Nov 2026 a phone test of Plenum ran the real Tailscale program on the owner's PC and removed the share of Plenum Hub (`--http=8790`). A product must never undo what it did not set up, and a test must never touch the owner's real network settings.

**Source.** The hub session's report of 12 Nov 2026 (the share was gone after a Plenum test run). Code: `lib/remote/carriers/tailscale.mjs` (`stop`, `locateTailscale`); tests: `mobile/test/carriers.test.mjs` (the two tests that pinned `serve reset` now pin `serve --https=443 off`), `mobile/test/helpers/sealed-server.mjs`.

**Status.** Done by the coordinator on 13 Nov 2026 and tested with a stand-in Tailscale program (the mobile runner: 54 tests of `carriers.test.mjs` and `routes.test.mjs`, 53 passed, 1 skipped by its own choice; `tailscale serve status` before and after showed only the hub's handler). The originals are in `build/v06-before/coord-tailscale`. The `PLENUM_TAILSCALE_CLI=off` lines stay in every runner. Open: before serving, Plenum should read `tailscale serve status --json` and never replace a handler on 443 that is not its own (planned with the phone link health work of wave 2). The installed build (0cf4e29) still has `serve reset` until the new build is installed: do not switch the Tailscale phone link off in the old build. Added 13 Nov 2026: Plenum answered the hub session that the installed build is 0.6.0, build 20261113.1457, commit 78c1d7e, which has this change (`ultra/plan/hub-answers.md`, section 6); that is Plenum's own report and was not read again for this text, and it is proved with a stand-in Tailscale only (the hub's share was reported on at 10:48 that day).

## 110. The phone dials its ways together

**Decision.** Found in the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (mobile-3): the phone tried every address of every way one after the other and gave each 6 s. A record holds up to four addresses for the same network, and seen from mobile data none of them answers, so leaving the house showed Reconnecting for 18 to 30 s, and a Stop pressed in that state reached the computer after about half of its 60 s window.
- **Together, a little apart.** A reconnect and a Stop without a session dial every address in one round: the first now, the next 250 ms later or at once when one has failed. The socket that opens first is the one used. The others are closed and nothing is ever sent on them.
- **One socket speaks.** The handshake, or the signed Stop, goes out on the winning socket alone, with a counter raised and stored just before, as always. Two sockets never send with counters that could arrive out of order, so the computer's replay rule needs no change. A way that opened and then failed is put aside and the rest are dialled again; every address still ends the round with one answer, so not-known, asleep and Stop's "unknown" are decided as before.
- **The address that worked goes first.** The record keeps `lastUrl` beside `lastCarrier` (kept by the shell's channel and store). The order is: that address, the rest of the way used last, then the other ways as listed.
- **Short patience for private addresses.** An address literal on a private range answers at once or never: it gets 2 s. Names (the relay, Tailscale, .local) keep 6 s. Pairing and session renewal are unchanged.
- **Mobile data.** Where the phone says it is on mobile data, plain home addresses are dialled last. They are still dialled, because the phone can be wrong (a hotspot, a VPN).

**Reason.** Stop from the phone is part of the floor; it must not wait behind addresses that cannot answer. The order of the ways (the way used last first) stays as decided for the Ways list; only the waiting in a row is gone.

**Source.** Audit finding mobile-3 and its verdict. Code: `mobile/shell/channel.worker.js` (`raceOpen`, `raceCandidates`, `attempt`, `stopLocked`, `openSocket`), `mobile/shell/carriers.js` (`dialOrder`, `isPrivateUrl`), `mobile/shell/channel.js` and `mobile/shell/store.js` (`lastUrl`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f2-phone-dial/phone-dial.test.mjs`, 18 of 18, 15 of them fail on the old code; `mobile/test/shell-core.test.mjs` group "mobile-3"; the phone harness suites `shell.e2e` 33 and `ui-phone` 120 pass with the rebuilt worker). Measured on a fake clock, phone away from home with three home addresses and the relay: before, the relay was dialled at 18 s; now at 0.75 s, with the Stop on the wire at 0.78 s; a round in which nobody answers ends after 6.25 s instead of 24 s. Not tried on a real phone or over a real relay. Open: the phone build output has to be made again after the last changes to `public/` (`node scripts/build-mobile.mjs`, then `--check`, which was stale only because other packages changed `public/`). Known costs: a way that is slower to open than 250 ms can lose to the next one (at home the relay is dialled only after every home address has had its 250 ms, and the address that worked is first next time); a home network that needs longer than 2 s to accept a connection fails that address in that round, and the round is retried on the usual schedule; `navigator.connection` is not present on iPhone, where the order is simply last address first.

## 111. Plenum keeps dated copies of the state file, and Settings can restore one

**Decision.** Found in the overhaul audit on 12 Nov 2026 (store-4). The only backup was `state.json.bak`, which is the save before the last one. While a reply streams it is seconds old, so chats removed by a mistake or a fault were gone from the backup one save later. Now there are dated copies.
- **Where and when.** `<state folder>/backups/state-<yyyymmdd-hhmm>.json` (local time). A save that `persist()` asked for makes one when the newest copy is an hour old or older. A draft save while a reply streams, the timed re-save after a failure and the save at close (`flush`) never make one. The copy is the text the save already read from the state file (it is not read a second time), so it is the state from before that save, and only a text that holds a valid state is copied.
- **Before an update.** The first such save of a build other than the one that saved last makes `state-<time>-update.json`. The last build is one line in `backups/last-build.txt` (the version and the build number from `lib/build-info.mjs`, item 91), not a field of the state file, so the state's shape is unchanged. A copy of Plenum without `lib/build-info.mjs` skips this quietly. The first start after this build is installed makes such a copy of the state as the old build left it.
- **What is kept.** The newest 24 hourly copies; then the newest copy of each of 7 earlier days; the newest 3 "before an update" copies; every "before a restore" copy younger than 7 days, and the newest 3 older ones; and the newest copy that is more than twice the size of every newer one, which is the state from before a large loss. The folder stays under 200 MB, oldest first, but the two newest copies always stay, and so does the first "before a restore" copy of each series of restores (restores less than an hour apart) until it is 7 days old. Files in the folder that are not copies are never listed or removed.
- **A copy never costs a save.** A copy that cannot be made (a full disk, a locked file, a folder that cannot be created) is skipped: the save works, nothing is retried for seconds, the temp file is removed, it is logged once, the Backups section says so, and the next try is ten minutes later.
- **What a copy holds.** `backups/index.json` keeps, for each copy, its size, its time and how many chats and messages it holds. The numbers are taken from the state in memory when the copy is made, so a save still never parses the file. A copy the index does not know (put there by hand, changed, or from before the index) is read and counted once when the list is shown; a copy that does not hold a state has no numbers. The index holds numbers only, and an index that is missing, damaged or cannot be written costs nothing but a recount.
- **Restore.** `GET /api/store/backups` lists `{ name, time, bytes, kind, chats, messages }` (never the text). `POST /api/store/restore { name, confirm: true }` takes only a name of that shape (a path is refused with 400), refuses a copy that does not hold a usable state (422), and answers a plain sentence with 409 while a reply or a task is running, or while a checkpoint restore, a rewind or a merge is live (`exclusive`, which `server.mjs` hands to route modules). It first saves the state as it is now and keeps it as `state-<time>-restore.json`, so a restore can be undone; if that copy cannot be made, nothing is restored. The restored state goes through the same checks as a state read at start (a chat that is not a list is set aside, a draft reply is marked interrupted, a copy from before threads gets its Main threads) and is saved by the store's own queue, so the check for another Plenum is not tripped. Nothing is ever deleted by a restore.
- **A series of restores.** Looking for a lost chat means restoring copies one after another. When nothing changed since the last restore, the state file holds exactly what that restore wrote, which is itself a copy in the list; the next restore then keeps no second copy and names the earlier one (`fresh: false`). So a series of trial restores leaves one "before a restore" copy, the state from before the series, and that copy is the one the limits above protect. Any save that writes a different text ends this: the next restore keeps its own copy.
- **A change during a restore.** The state is swapped only when no save was asked for since the text of the "before a restore" copy was taken; otherwise the copy is made again, and after three rounds the restore answers 409 ("The chats or settings are still changing ..."). The checks that need the disk, and the threads migration, run on the copy's state before the swap. The migration runs through a stand-in for the store that saves nothing and makes no `state.json.pre-threads-<date>` file, because the restore has already kept the state.
- **The page.** Settings > General > Backups lists the copies (time, what the copy holds, size, "before an update" or "before a restore") with Restore. Copies of one kind made in the same minute are numbered, and every Restore button has its own label. Restore asks first (Cancel has the focus) and says what the copy holds. After a restore the window loads again and says what happened, including when no new copy was needed. Restoring a copy brings back the settings of that time too, not only the chats.
- **The License Agreement record is part of the state.** A copy from before the agreement was accepted asks for it again after a restore. A restore never carries an acceptance into a state that did not have it, and the restore route is behind the gate like every POST.
- **Tonight's part of store-3.** A save no longer parses the state file a second time when the file is the text this process read at start or wrote itself (on a 48 MB state that parse blocked the window for about a quarter of a second on every save). The copy to `state.json.bak` is skipped when the backup already holds that text. The indent of the state file is kept: README tells people they may read and edit it. The split into one file per chat is not done.

**Reason.** The bar: never lose data. A copy that is an hour, a day or an update old is what brings chats back after a logical loss, and the person has to be able to see the copies, see what is in them and use one without leaving Plenum. The recovery feature itself must not lose anything: the first version kept only the newest three "before a restore" copies, so the fourth trial restore deleted the state the person started from (found by the review on 13 Nov 2026). The floor is untouched: the copies and the index are inside Plenum's own data folder, which `checkAccess` and `isCredentialPath` refuse to every tool (a test pins this), and the state holds no key.

**Source.** Audit findings store-4 and store-3 with their verdicts (`ultra/audit/findings/store-4.json`, `store-3.json`); the review's probes in `ultra/work/review-ultra-f2-store-backups/`, which now show no loss. Code: `lib/store.mjs` (the copies, the index, `restoreBackup`), `lib/routes/store-health.mjs`, `public/ext-store-health.js`.

**Status.** Done, 13 Nov 2026, with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f2-store-backups`, 61 tests in 5 files: `store-backups` 24, `review-fixes` 13, `store-backup-routes` 6 (one through the real server sealed on 127.0.0.1), `page-backups` 15, `floor-and-speed` 3). Measured on a 5 MB state of the owner's shape while other work ran: a save 163 to 248 ms, a save that also makes the hourly copy 221 to 523 ms (the copy writes the 5 MB a second time). Checked in the real page on a sealed server: the list with the numbers, Show all, the confirm, a restore, the reload, and three restores in a row with a reload between them. Open: the copies are plain JSON on the same disk as the state, which is C: on this PC (about 31 to 37 files and up to 200 MB when the list is full; the owner's state is 4.8 MB), and compressing them or moving them to another disk is the owner's choice; the per-chat split of the state file (store-3) is not built; a restore reloads only the window that asked, and another open window gets the new state through the events channel; a copy made before a module added its own default to the state (the phones settings, a project's spend record) gets that default after the restore, so the next restore keeps one more "before a restore" copy instead of naming the earlier one (nothing is lost by it); the page reload after a restore stuck twice in the browser pane of the reviewer (not caused by this item, and worth one look in the real window); the phone client's copy of the panel (`mobile/www/ui/ext-store-health.js`, build output of the phone track) is older than `public/ext-store-health.js` until `node scripts/build-mobile.mjs` is run. A suite that pins the exact list of files in the state folder would have to allow `backups`; none was found.

## 112. Isolated copies say what they leave out and can always be discarded; a slow git read says so; the git chip follows the thread

**Decision.** Four small changes around isolated copies and the git reads beside them.
1. **A new isolated copy says what it does not have.** A copy starts from the last commit, so files the person has changed or added in the project folder and not committed are not in it. Plenum now says so, twice. In the New thread panel, while **Work in an isolated copy** is ticked, one line reads "3 files in this project are not committed (a.txt, b.txt and 1 more). The copy starts from the last commit, so it will not have them.", with **Dismiss**. When the copy is made, its stream carries one notice (kind `worktree-stale-base`): "The copy starts from the last commit of main. 3 files in the project folder are not committed, so the copy does not have them: a.txt, b.txt, c.txt. Commit them first if this thread needs them." It names at most five entries and counts the rest, and the copy's log stays open while it holds this line. Files the project's copy setting names (a `.env`) do not count, because Plenum copies those in; a new folder that holds nothing but such files does not count either (git lists a new folder as one entry, so Plenum asks git once what is in it). A new folder that does count is called a folder ("1 folder ...", "3 files or folders ..."). When no name is known (the only entries are files with a merge conflict), the sentence gives the number and no list. It is a notice, never a block. Nothing is carried over into the copy: whether Plenum should do that is the owner's to decide (Open items).
2. **A copy can always be removed in one press, and Keep never deletes work it could not read.** Two things used to leave a copy half removed. First, a removal that stopped half way: git deletes a copy's `.git` file first, and a folder without it is no longer a copy for git, so `git worktree remove --force` refused it for ever (tested: git 2.55 does refuse). Second, and the ordinary case on Windows: a copy that holds a junction (pnpm's `node_modules`, a workspace, a setup command that links `node_modules`). Git for Windows answers such a removal with success and still leaves the junction and the folders above it. After git's own attempts, Plenum now deletes what is left as a folder and prunes git's record, both when git failed (where the removal may force: Discard, and Keep only when git listed no uncommitted work) and when git said it was done and a folder is still there (git checked the copy for uncommitted work before it deleted anything, so what is left is not work). It deletes only a folder that is exactly a copy's own place (`<copies folder>\<8 hex>`) and really lies inside the copies folder. A link in that place is unlinked and never walked, and every link inside the folder is unlinked before the delete, so a junction can never lead the delete outside the copy. A copy that git lists work in is never forced. Keep on a copy git can no longer read commits and deletes nothing and answers 409 with what to press: for a missing `.git` file "This isolated copy was partly removed already, so Plenum cannot read its changes. What was committed is on plenum/.... Press Discard to remove what is left of the folder; the branch stays.", and for a copy whose record in the repository is gone "Plenum cannot read this isolated copy any more: git no longer accepts the folder as a copy. What was committed is on plenum/.... Press Discard to remove the folder; the branch stays." When a program still holds the folder, the message names the folder and the cause ("a program is using ... (EBUSY)") instead of git's stale text, and the next try succeeds once the program lets go. git gets 300 seconds for a removal (it was 120), so a slow delete of `node_modules` is not cut off half way, and a removal that ran out of time is not started again.
3. **The Review pane says when git was slow.** A git read that runs out of time answers 504 "Git took too long to read the changes. Try again, or look in your terminal." at each of the four reads (the repository check, the staged diff, the unstaged diff, the untracked list). Changes too large to read keep their own text (413 "The changes are too large to read at once."), and any other failure keeps "Plenum could not read the changes. Try again." The two diffs get 30 seconds (it was 15).
4. **The header's git chip answers for the thread on screen.** `GET /api/git` takes `thread`; a thread that works in a ready isolated copy is answered from its copy (its branch, its files), and Main, a thread that shares the project folder and a request without a thread are answered from the project folder. The chip names the thread on every request (Main by the project's id) and reads again when the thread on screen changes to or from one with a copy. Threads that share the project folder share one answer, so switching between them asks nothing and nothing flickers. When the server refuses the thread (one that was closed in another window) and the thread on screen works in the project folder, the chip asks once more without the thread and shows the project folder's answer; a thread with a copy of its own is never answered that way. After a read that failed, a change of the thread on screen reads again at once. The Checkpoints drawer names the thread in the same way (item 106).

**Reason.** A thread in a copy read older code than the person saw, and nothing said so; the wasted work only showed at merge time. A copy left half removed (a file held open, a delete cut off at two minutes, or simply a junction in `node_modules`) left a thread that could be neither kept nor discarded, or that needed several presses, with a message that blamed git and programs that were not the cause; the way out was deleting a folder by hand. A slow repository made the Review pane say "could not read the changes. Try again", which kept failing and explained nothing. The chip showed the project folder's branch and files while the thread on screen worked on another branch in another folder.

**Source.** Audit findings agents-1, agents-4 and agents-5 (12 Nov 2026) with the verifiers' verdicts, the wave 1 note w1-panel-fixes-3, and the review of this package (13 Nov 2026). Corrections that held: all three findings were lowered from high to medium; agents-1 loses no data (the merge step already refuses while the project folder has uncommitted tracked changes); agents-5 is done as step 1 only. Found by the review and fixed: the fallback delete ran only when git had failed, which missed the junction case (git exits 0 and leaves the folder); a copy whose record in the repository is gone answered with an internal text about filter drivers; the warning ended in ": 1 more" when it had no name to list; a copy-setting file inside a new folder made the notice say the copy lacks the folder while the next line said the file was copied; the chip stayed hidden for up to 30 seconds after a thread was closed elsewhere. Found while building: `git status --porcelain=v2 -- .` writes paths relative to the folder it runs in, so the names in the notice are the project's own paths also when the project is a sub-folder of its repository; a Keep in a half-removed copy would have run git in a folder that is no longer a copy, where git answers for whatever repository lies above it, so Keep now stops before any git command. Code: `lib/worktree.mjs` (`uncommittedIn`, `uncommittedOf`, `onlyCopySettingFiles`, `staleNotice`, `repoInfo`, `removeLeftover`, `unlinkLinksIn`, `removeCopy`, `commitCopy`, `refusedByGit`), `lib/routes/worktrees.mjs` (`infoRoute`), `public/ext-worktree.js` (`dialogExtra`, `logEl`), `lib/review.mjs` (`readFailure`, `collectChanges`), `lib/routes/review.mjs`, `lib/routes/git.mjs` (`copyFolder`, `statusRoute`), `public/ext-git.js` (`threadId`, `scopeKey`, `refresh`, `follow`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f2-copies-review`, 61 tests in 6 files, all pass: stale-base 14, remove-leftover 13, review-timeout 12, git-thread 10, dialog-note 8, page 4). Where they test a change they fail on the original files (13 of the 14 added after the review fail on the files as they were before that fix, the other one is a control). `page.test.mjs` runs the real page in a hidden Electron window against a sealed server and a sandbox repository: the line in the New thread panel, Dismiss, the copy's log with the warning, the chip for the thread and for Main, and no console error. The suites git-status 20, pro-02-worktrees 66, pro-04-review-pane 54, pro-01-threads 72 and ultra-f1-panel-fixes 49 pass. Open: carrying uncommitted work into the copy (the owner's decision, see Open items); agents-5 step 2 (listing first and reading each file's diff on demand, which needs `public/ext-review.js` and changes the hash contract between the pane and the hunk route); a progress line while a slow removal runs. Not tested against a git that is really slow (Plenum starts `git` without a shell, so a `.cmd` stand-in cannot take its place; the tests answer chosen git calls as timed out through a module hook); the junction case is tested with junctions the test makes itself, not with a real pnpm install; the chip's second request for a closed thread is tested in a stand-in page, not in the real window. The count in the notice is not kept in the copy's record, so it is not shown again after the window is reloaded. Known costs: a removal now gives git up to 300 seconds, so a Keep or Discard of a very large copy can take minutes with no progress line; Keep (and Merge) on a copy git can no longer read now answers 409 with the new texts (it was a 500); a git read that prints too much at the repository check or the untracked list is now a 413; the count of uncommitted files covers the project folder only, counts an untracked folder as one entry and reads at most 200 paths. The phone's `mobile/www/ui/ext-git.js` is build output that still holds the old panel until the next phone build, and whether the phone's `/api/git` route passes `thread` through was not checked. `build/v06/r1418-wt/r1418-wt.test.mjs` (line 96) compares the written path of the copy with the real path git lists behind the test tree's junction: the test now uses `(await fs.realpath(rec.path))` (5 of 5 with that change), and `build/v06/pro-01-threads/shot.test.mjs` ("two Echo runs with a tab switch") was unstable under load and passed 7 of 7 after this change.

## 113. A CLI installed while Plenum runs is found without a restart (Windows)

**Decision.** Found by the overhaul audit on 12 Nov 2026 (finding page-setup-4). A Windows program gets its PATH once, when it starts. The installers the Accounts cards name (Cursor, Grok Build, Antigravity, npm, the Muse Code setup) add their folder to the PATH in the registry, and a program that is already running never sees that. So a CLI installed while Plenum ran stayed "Not installed" after Refresh, and the Sign in button stayed off, until Plenum was started again (the desktop shell reads the registry when it starts the server, `registryEnv` in `desktop/main.cjs`, which is why a restart helped).
- **A second look, only after a miss.** When a tool is found neither on the PATH of the process nor in Plenum's tools folder, Plenum reads the PATH value from the registry (`reg query <key> /v Path` for the computer's key and the user's key, `REG_SZ` and `REG_EXPAND_SZ`, `%NAME%` filled from the environment) and looks in the folders that are on it and not on the PATH it already searched (`lib/platform/winpath.mjs`, `locateOnRegistryPath`, called from `findExecutable` in `lib/proc.mjs`). A hit is the full path of the file, which is what a run starts. The choice among several files is the one of the usual lookup: a real `.exe`, then a `.cmd` or `.bat` shim, then the first one, with the extensions of `PATHEXT`.
- **Nothing that worked changes.** A tool that is found the usual way never causes a registry read and is never looked for anywhere else. The tools folder still comes before the registry. A registry folder is never put in front of anything, the PATH of the process is never changed (a child must not inherit a surprise), and the PATH a child gets is the one it got before. macOS and Linux are untouched.
- **It cannot hold the server.** The registry is read with `reg.exe` in the background (never a blocking call), both keys at once, each with a 3 second limit; every lookup that starts in the meantime shares that one read (the status route looks for seven CLIs at once). The registry answer is kept for 5 minutes and the file lists of the new folders for 30 seconds, so a status read does not start `reg.exe` every few seconds while some CLI is missing, which is the usual state. Refresh in Accounts drops both (`forgetRegistryPath`, called from `forgetExecutables`), and a run reads again unless the last read began less than 2 seconds ago. A registry that cannot be read, a `reg.exe` that is missing or never answers, a folder that is gone or slow: each is a miss, as before, never an error.
- **Only the Path value is asked for.** The shell's own start reads every variable of the two keys; this read names the one value, so no other variable of the user (a key saved with `setx`, for example) is read by it. One exception: a variable that an entry of that Path names and the process does not have, because the same installer just made it (`%PNPM_HOME%`, `%VOLTA_HOME%`, `%NVM_HOME%`). Such a name is asked for by itself (the user's value wins over the computer's), at most four of them, and never one with KEY, TOKEN, SECRET, PASSWORD or CREDENTIAL in its name. Its value is only ever used as a folder to look in. Nothing is ever written to the registry.
- **Only folders on a drive.** An empty or relative entry means "the current folder" to a shell and is left out, as `findOnPath` does, so a tool planted in a project folder is never picked up. A network folder (`\\server\share`) is left out too, because reading one that cannot be reached can take a long time, and so is an entry that still holds a `%NAME%` that is set nowhere.
- **Off unless the desktop app turns it on.** The second look runs only with `PLENUM_PATH_REFRESH=1`, and only the desktop app sets that, for the server it starts (`serverEnv` in `desktop/main.cjs`, on Windows and not in smoke mode; a value already set stays, so `0` in the user's environment turns it off), whose PATH was itself built from the registry. A plain `node server.mjs` and every test stay off, whether the test's server is a plain process or a utility process of a hidden Electron: a test must only ever see the PATH it was given, and the real computer's CLIs are on the registry PATH. The server reads the switch once, when `winpath.mjs` loads, and takes it out of its environment, so nothing it starts (a CLI, a command of the shell tool, a test run a model starts from a chat) inherits it. `PLENUM_PATH_REFRESH=1 node server.mjs` turns it on for a plain run.

Not taken from the verdict, on purpose: adding known Windows install folders to `cliDirs`, passing a refreshed PATH to `where.exe`, and giving children the refreshed PATH. The planner kept this package to "no lookup that works today changes", and each of those changes the PATH of a lookup or a child that works today.

**Reason.** The first minutes with the product are installing a CLI and pressing Refresh. A card that still says "Not installed" after the person did what it said looks like a broken product, and the only cure was one nothing on most cards named. The switch is opt-in because the first build of this item turned itself on in any Electron utility process, and three sealed Electron test harnesses start their server that way: with the lookup wired in, their servers would have found and started the real CLIs of the computer.

**Source.** Audit finding page-setup-4 and its verdict (`ultra/audit/findings/page-setup-4.json`); the review of wave 2 (the gate, the installer-made variables, the kept time); the real `reg.exe` answer read on 13 Nov 2026. Code: `lib/platform/winpath.mjs` (new), `lib/platform/index.mjs` (`locateCommand`, the three exports), `lib/proc.mjs` (`findExecutable`, `forgetExecutables`), `desktop/main.cjs` (`serverEnv`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f2-path-refresh`, 41 tests in 4 files (`winpath` 21, `proc-edit` 9, `locate-command` 7, `main-edit` 4; `setup.mjs` is a helper with no tests), 41 of 41 pass against the live files: the parser against the real answer's shape and against the real `reg.exe`; expansion; which folders count as new; the switch (off in a plain process, in a process that looks like Electron's utility process, in smoke mode; on with the variable; the variable gone from the environment afterwards); a CLI found after an install through Refresh, through a run and by itself; seven lookups sharing one read; no `reg.exe` in 4 minutes 59 seconds of status reads; installer-made variables, and names that are never asked for; a registry or a folder that fails or never answers; the PATH never changed; `locateCommand` on Windows; `findExecutable` with the edit; and `serverEnv()` of `desktop/main.cjs` with its edit). The two edits in files of other packages (`lib/proc.mjs`: the import, `forgetRegistryPath()` in `forgetExecutables`, the registry lookup in `findExecutable`; `desktop/main.cjs`: `PLENUM_PATH_REFRESH` in `serverEnv()`) are in the live files (checked 13 Nov 2026). Not run: a real installer, because the registry of the owner's PC is not changed by a test, and the desktop app itself (`serverEnv()` is run as a function, not inside Electron). Known limits: `reg.exe` answers in the console's code page, so a folder whose name has letters outside ASCII may not be found this way (a restart finds it); a CLI found this way is started by its full path with the PATH from before, so a tool it needs that was installed in the same sitting (Node for an npm shim, for example) is still found only after a restart; and without pressing Refresh a new CLI can take up to 5 minutes to show (a run finds it at once). The Cursor and Grok Build cards now say, as the other cards do, "If it still shows Not installed after Refresh, restart Plenum." (`public/ext-accounts-cli.js`). The Antigravity card keeps its "then restart Plenum": whether Refresh is enough there was not tried. Open: `seal.sh` and `battery3.sh` do not set `PLENUM_PATH_REFRESH=0` as a second lock (the switch is off unless the desktop app sets it, so it is not needed for safety).

## 114. A CLI that fails or is cut off is shown as failed, in plain words with a next step

**Decision.** Found in the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (cli-1, cli-2, cli-6, j-chat-3): a CLI that died in the middle of a reply (a usage limit, a dropped connection, a crash, a kill) left half an answer in the chat as a finished reply, and a council seat or relay hop passed it on as finished work. A CLI that failed with no output showed its process name, an exit code and its raw error output ("muse exited with code 1: ...").
- **A cut-off reply is a failed reply.** `streamProcess` (`lib/proc.mjs`) takes a new option, `completed`: a function of the adapter that answers true once the CLI's own end marker was read. A non-zero exit (an exit by a signal counts as one) after output, with `completed` answering false, throws after the text that was already shown; the server keeps that text and stores the error with it (item B-49's path, unchanged). Codex with `--json` marks the end with `turn.completed`, Gemini CLI, Cursor and Grok Build in print mode and Claude Code with their result event: a non-zero exit after the marker is still a whole reply. Muse Code and Codex in plain text have no marker, so any non-zero exit after output is a failed reply with a hint to send again. A caller that passes no `completed` (the Muse usage probe) behaves as before. Stop is never an error. "Output" is text a person could see: a CLI that printed only a blank line before it failed has written nothing.
- **Claude Code says in its result when a reply failed.** It usually stays alive when its service fails in the middle of a reply (a busy service, the plan's limit, its own step limit) and ends with a result event that reports an error. Such a result ends the run as failed whether or not text came before it and whatever the exit code is; the text stays on screen with the error. A rejected rate limit event gives the time the limit resets.
- **One table for what a failure means.** `lib/cli-errors.mjs` (pure, no file, no process) reads the CLI's own failure report first (an error event, a result's error, Claude Code's rate limit event), then the end of its error output, never the reply text, and answers with a kind, a sentence, a next step and whether sending again can work. Kinds: not signed in, model not offered, usage limit, service busy, no network, an option the CLI refused, output Plenum could not read, crashed, unknown. The message is the sentence and the next step; the CLI's own line follows on a new line, short, with keys taken out. The exit code is a field of the error, never part of the words.
- **The cause is the CLI's last word.** Of the error output only the last six lines are read (the lines of a stack are not counted), from the last line back, and the first line that says a cause is the cause and the only line shown. A warning, a tool's output or a line of a log above it names nothing. Codex in plain text writes its whole progress log to its error output (the prompt, its thinking, its commands and what they printed), so there only what follows Codex's own last "ERROR" line is read; a long log with no such line names no cause, and the message says only that Codex stopped, with the log's last lines. An error output of a few lines is no log and is read as always (a refused option, "Not logged in").
- **Numbers and limits are read with care.** A status number counts only where the text gives it as one (after "status", "code", "HTTP" or "error", in brackets, or at the start of a line before a colon), never as a line number in a stack or a port. A retry limit, a command's time limit, a step limit and the length of a chat are not the plan's limit: "exceeded retry limit, last status: 503" is a busy service. "Not found" is a model that is not offered only when it is said of the model; Anthropic's answer for a retired model (`not_found_error`, "model: ...") is one.
- **An option the CLI no longer takes** (cli-6, stage 1): "Claude Code did not accept the option --x that Plenum sent, so nothing ran. The CLI has probably updated itself. Update Plenum. Until then, pick another model." Only the line that names the refusal is kept, never the CLI's usage page. It is looked for before anything else, because a usage page lists words like "login" and "model", and only in a run that wrote nothing.
- **Never a silent switch.** A model that is not offered says "Pick another model in the model menu"; nothing picks one. Its sentence starts "<name> does not offer", which is what the page already looks for to lead to the model menu. Muse Code's own guided message of item 91 stays first.
- **Codex with the saved OpenAI key.** When Codex runs on the key route and the service refuses the key, the message is "Codex could not use the saved OpenAI key. Open Accounts and save a valid key, or switch Codex back to the ChatGPT sign-in.", not the sign-in message: signing in to ChatGPT does not help a run that uses the key.
- **Gemini CLI.** A run whose result reports an error after part of a reply is now a failed reply (it was the text plus a bracketed note). Output that is not the CLI's JSON at all says "Gemini CLI sent output Plenum could not read" with a short piece of it.
- **Keys.** The table takes out everything with the shape of a provider key, a bearer token, the name and password inside an address ("//name:password@host"), a value given to a key, token or password field when it can be a secret (it holds a digit or is 16 characters or longer, so "token: expired" keeps its words), and long unbroken words. `withoutKey` now cleans every text field of an error, not only its message and stack.
- **Plan use for Claude Code.** A sign-in check that gave no answer (a slow or hung CLI) is said to be that ("Claude Code did not answer the sign-in check. Refresh to try again."), never "not signed in"; a check that Plenum had to end at its 15 second limit counts as no answer on Windows too, where the kill leaves exit code 1.

**Reason.** Bar item 3 (every failure is explained, with a next step) and item 6 (no stuck or false "done" states). A person who is shown half a sentence as an answer trusts it; a council that is handed it compares it. A wrong cause is worse than none: it sends the person to sign in, to wait for a limit or to update Plenum for nothing, and hides the line that would have told them. So the table reads little and reads it from the end, and where it cannot know it says so and shows the CLI's last lines. The opt-in keeps the adapters this package does not own exactly as they were. The words are chosen so that the texts other parts already read keep working: the team's failure table (`lib/team.mjs`), the limit banner and the page's "does not offer" rule, and the sign-in rules of Cursor, Grok Build and Gemini CLI (a message that is not about signing in never holds their sign-in words; a test pins this).

**Source.** Audit findings cli-1, cli-2, cli-6 and j-chat-3 with their verdicts (`ultra/audit/findings/`); the auditors' probe `ultra/work/audit-cli/probe1.mjs`; the review of 13 Nov 2026 and its probes (`ultra/work/review-ultra-f1-cli-failures/`). Code: `lib/cli-errors.mjs` (`classifyCliFailure`, `cliError`, `CliError`, `redactSecrets`, `cutOffWords`), `lib/proc.mjs` (`streamProcess`, `withoutKey`), `lib/providers.mjs` (`streamMuse`, `streamCodex`, `codexEvents`, `codexKeyRefused`), `lib/gemini.mjs` (`runGemini`), `lib/claude-cli.mjs` (`streamClaudeCli`, `claudeCliUsage`, `capture`), and `completed` in `lib/cursor.mjs` and `lib/grok.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 and corrected after review the same night, with tests (`build/v06/ultra-f1-cli-failures`: `classify.test.mjs` 13, `run.test.mjs` 33, `plan-use.test.mjs` 3, `server.test.mjs` 1; 50 of 50 pass at the report and again on 13 Nov 2026 after the later edits; 17 of the first 24 run tests fail on the code before the package, and the 13 tests added after the review fail on the code as reviewed). Every CLI in the tests is a stand-in; no real CLI was run with a prompt, so the real CLIs' error texts are those the audit and the review recorded and the ones their documentation and help pages give; that Codex writes its own errors as "ERROR: ..." in plain text is from its documentation and the review, not seen here. Open: the buttons under a failed reply (Sign in, Pick another model, Open the log) and hiding Retry where it cannot help need `public/app.js` and `public/ext-connection.js`: the server already sends `kind` and `retryable` on the error event and stores `errorKind`, and the page does not read them yet. The page's `NOT_OFFERED_ERROR` (`public/app.js`) now also matches a model that is refused after part of a reply, and the team's `SIGN_IN_RE` (`lib/team.mjs`) reads "could not use the saved <name> key" as a sign-in problem (both edits are in the live files, checked 13 Nov 2026). The print mode of Cursor and Grok Build (on only with `MUSE_CONSOLE_CURSOR_TRANSPORT=print` or `MUSE_CONSOLE_GROK_TRANSPORT=print`) words a run that wrote only stream events and no reply, and then exited non-zero, as a failure without a cut-off reply (`partial: false`), so it does not say a reply was cut off when nothing was written (`build/v06/integ-core/print-nothing-written.test.mjs`, 6 tests with stand-in CLIs; the real CLIs' output was never seen). The ACP agents (Cursor and Grok Build through ACP, Antigravity; `lib/acp.mjs`, `lib/antigravity.mjs`) do not yet say when a reply was cut off and still show their own failure text. The shell tool's output limit in `lib/proc.mjs` (`runShell`: keep the start and the end, count what was dropped) is not built. Reading each CLI's version and checking its flags once per version (cli-6, stage 2) is not built. Muse Code or Codex in plain text that exits non-zero after a complete answer, or a Claude Code result that reports an error after a complete answer, is shown as failed with the text kept; neither has been seen. A refresh of Plan use sends one very small real request to Muse Code (`lib/usage.mjs`) and, when no reply is recent, to Claude Code: older, deliberate reads of the plan's limits. The attachments suite's "every other model refuses a PDF ..." test counts the model-list readers' background starts as run starts (24 of 25), and `build/providers-a` fails two catalog checks against `frontier.json`: neither is this item's file.

## 115. The team: the owner's own list of models for each kind of work

**Decision.** Plenum can hold a team: for each kind of work, the owner's ordered list of models across his subscriptions. A lead that hands work over names the kind, and the first model in that list that can run now does it. It is off until the owner turns it on, and with no saved team nothing is written. The logic is `lib/team.mjs`, the log `lib/team-log.mjs`, the routes `lib/routes/team.mjs` (all under `/api/team`).
- **The registry is computed, never a list in code.** One entry for each model in the catalog (which already holds what Codex, Muse Code and Antigravity list for the account) plus every model the saved team names. A model that a CLI lists tomorrow is there with no release. Luna and Terra are not written into code as models: they arrive from the Codex account's list.
- **Size** (small, mid, top) comes from the owner's own value, then the published input price of the model or of the same id on the API provider that prices it (up to $0.80 small, up to $3 mid, above that top), then a whole word of the id from one table (`SIZE_HINTS`; "gemini" is not "mini"), else unknown. Sizes are starting points; the page says where each comes from.
- **Can it run now** is read from memory only: the provider snapshot and the key list (the program is not installed, needs sign-in, needs a key), the catalog (not offered, and for a model missing from a list only when that list was read), the last plan-use reading against the line in force (90 percent, or the owner's), and what a failed task taught (a limit for 15 minutes, a sign-in until the provider snapshot changes or 15 minutes pass, a model that is not offered until the catalog changes).
- **A plan-use reading counts for 30 minutes.** After that it is "not known", and not known is tried. The reading is asked for in the background, at most every 10 minutes, never by a choice or a page read (the reader can start Codex's app server). So the evening's reading cannot keep a kind refused the next morning, and an old percentage is never shown as current.
- **The choice** goes through the list in the owner's order and passes a model over, with its reason, when it was already tried, is below the size asked for, cannot run the kind's agent type, is billed per token where that is not allowed, cannot run now, or cannot ask before it writes in a project that refuses such coders in Ask first. Work spreads over the subscriptions (three workers per subscription at once by default) and a review avoids the subscriptions that did the work; the line in the chat says so when that is why the first model did not get the task. A model that is not in the owner's list is never used. When nothing can run, the answer names every model and its reason.
- **Per-token models are out until the owner says so** (`allowApi`, off). How a model is billed is asked on every choice, not read from what was kept: a route changed in Accounts (a Gemini model's own route, a key route switched on) does not change the provider snapshot, so a kept answer could let a paid route through. While Plenum does not know how a model is billed, that model is not used.
- **A kind with no model is not a refusal.** The seven built-in kinds start empty and the switch can be turned on by hand. For such a kind the team has no say (`pick` answers `empty: true`) and the task is meant to run on the model of its agent type, as with the team off. The runner reads it (item 116).
- **The suggested team is computed on request and never applied by itself:** from every signed-in subscription one small and one mid model (review: mid and top), cheapest first, in the kind's order; no CLI default model, no model of unknown size. "Use this team" saves nothing when the suggestion holds no model at all, and a kind it has no model for keeps the owner's own list.
- **Seven kinds are built in** (find, research, code, test, write, data, review) and the owner may add 17. Kinds are data. A save sends the whole list of the owner's own kinds: one that is left out is removed (that is how the page deletes a kind).
- **The log** (`team-log.jsonl`, one line per finished task, 4 MB then one older file) holds titles, models, times and counts, and never instructions, answers or file contents. The sums for the page are kept in memory after the first read.
- **The file** `team.json` has a version and a migration: an older and a damaged file load (a damaged one is kept as `team.damaged.json` at the first save), a file from a newer version is read and never written, and a file that is there but cannot be read at the first try is said on the page and tried again at the next call.

**Reason.** The owner asked for "truly good, thoughtful inbuilt delegation abilities to smaller cheaper models eg haiku sonnet Luna terra and muse, all subscriptions should be used for their strengths and they have to be able to work together seamlessly and efficiently without significant overhead." A choice that reads memory adds no start time to a hand-over; an ordered list he edits keeps the decision his; and off by default keeps today's behaviour until he presses the button (item 55). The rules added after the review each close a way in which the team could act against him without his knowing: a paid route used while his switch is off, delegation that worked before being refused the moment the switch is on, and a kind staying shut on old numbers.

**Source.** The owner's words of 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/biz/OWNER-ADDENDUM.md`, from "Note that ultimately this tool and the agent relay hub ..." at about midnight, and "Organizational capabilities, orchestration, and communication with outside sources ..."; the same text is in BRIEF.md section 8). Design: `ultra/biz/DELEGATION-DESIGN.md` sections 4, 5, 6 and 13. The review of 13 Nov 2026 (probes in `ultra/work/review-ultra-w3-team/probe/`). Code: `lib/team.mjs`, `lib/team-log.mjs`, `lib/routes/team.mjs`. Tests: `build/v06/ultra-w3-team/`.

**Status.** Built, reviewed and fixed on 13 Nov 2026 with stand-ins only (no real model, CLI or provider): 65 tests pass at the report and again on 13 Nov 2026 after the edits below (65 of 65); 50 deliberate faults (the first 25, and 25 more in the fixed lines) were each caught by a test; the reviewer's probes A, E and G give the right answer. Not tested against the owner's real sign-ins. The two edits the team needed in `lib/team-run.mjs` are in the live file (checked 13 Nov 2026): the runner reads `empty: true` and runs such a task on the model of its agent type, and it asks for the plan-use reading when it hands a task over (`build/v06/integ-core/team-seam.test.mjs`, 5 tests, among them the real team with every list empty). Where the build differs from the design's wording, on purpose: how a model is billed is asked on every choice instead of kept until `onProvidersChange`, and a reading has an age limit. A bare "429" in any failure text still reads as a limit, because the limit words are the page's own. The team's usage log is a new local record (titles of delegated tasks, models, times, counts) that the Privacy Notice does not name yet. The team module also takes an optional overlay for a later feature, and no such feature is in this build.

## 116. The team runner: a lead hands work over by kind, several tasks in one call, and the owner's team picks the model

**Decision.** The one tool stays `delegate`. Between the lead's call and the agent runner sits one new module, `lib/team-run.mjs`, so every lead (through an API, through a CLI and the bridge, a council seat, an agent that starts an agent) gets the same thing with no change anywhere else. Nothing changes until the owner turns his team on: a call that uses none of the new arguments while the team is off reaches the agent runner with the same arguments and gives back the same result as before.
- **New arguments, all optional:** `work` (the kind of work; it names the agent type), `level` (small, mid or top: the smallest size to use), `done_when` (600 characters), `job` (40 characters, a name shared by the tasks of one piece of work) and `tasks` (up to 7 more tasks for the same call). A `level` that is not small, mid or top is refused with "level must be small, mid or top." (a wrong word that was silently dropped would let a small model take a task meant for a top one). The tool's definition also has a `program` argument, in the order the tool registry's test pins; a call that names one is refused with "This build cannot hand work to a program yet. Leave program out." and nothing is started.
- **The model.** A model the lead names is used and is never swapped, also when it fails. Otherwise, with the team on, the first model in the owner's list for that kind that can run now does the work (`lib/team.mjs` chooses). With the team off the agent type's own model does the work, as before, and if the lead asked for a level the answer says that the level was not used.
- **A short brief out.** The worker gets the lead's instructions, and only the parts that apply after them: DONE WHEN, the notes of the job (1,600 characters at most), the kind's reply sentence and "Keep your reply under N characters." The whole brief is 12,000 characters at most, and a brief that was cut says so and tells the lead to name files by path.
- **A short answer back.** The worker's answer is cut to the kind's limit (2,400 characters; 8,000 for write and data) with one line that gives both numbers and says "Ask again for the part you need." The row's result ends with the worker's model and its use, for example "Researcher · Find the date parser: done in 0:41 · Claude Haiku 5.5 · 3.1k tokens".
- **A move to the next model, at most twice,** when the team chose the model and the task failed because the model is at its plan limit, needs sign-in, is not offered on the account or is not installed. A task that took too long moves only when its type cannot edit files or run commands. A task never moves once it changed a file, left a change unchecked, or was stopped. Each move has its own row in the chat and a TEAM: line in the answer the lead reads, with the model's name and the reason.
- **Several tasks in one call** run at the same time through the same run: one Stop, and the runner's own limits on how many agents work at once. The lead reads one answer with a part for each task under "[n of m]", each cut to an equal share of 20,000 characters, and the row's result counts them by subscription: "3 tasks: 2 done, 1 not finished · Claude 2, Codex 1". A ninth task is refused with "At most 8 tasks in one call. Send the rest in a second call." A further task takes job, model, level and effort from the call, and the kind of work when it names none.
- **Job notes** are kept in memory only (200 jobs, 20 notes for each, 300 characters a note): a later task of the same job gets the earlier results as notes in its brief. Nothing is written into the project folder, and the notes are gone when Plenum closes.
- **A review** with a job avoids the subscriptions that did the job's work; when only one of those can run, the answer says that the review ran on the same subscription. A check by a second model is a review task; it has no code of its own.
- **The log.** Each finished task hands one line to `lib/team-log.mjs`: title, kind, lead, worker, the moves, the state (done, not finished, stopped), the time and the counts. Never instructions, answers or file contents.
- **One hint, once per run of Plenum,** while the team is off and a person's own chat delegates: "Delegated work uses one model for everything. Your team can spread it over your subscriptions: Settings > Agents > Team." It is shown only in a build that has the Team page.
- **Loading.** `lib/team-run.mjs` is the one new module that awaits an import at its top (the tool's definition is built at once, not later). Its neighbours are loaded inside a try with a limit of ten seconds; one that is missing, broken or too slow counts as missing, and one that arrives late is taken up then. None of them, and nothing they import at their top, may import `lib/team-run.mjs`, `lib/tools/40-delegate.mjs` or `lib/routes/index.mjs`.

**Reason.** The owner asked for "truly good, thoughtful inbuilt delegation abilities to smaller cheaper models", with little overhead. Naming the kind of work is one word for the lead; the choice of model stays the owner's (his list, his order, item 55); a named model is never swapped (his rule); a short brief and a cut answer keep the cost of handing work over small on both sides; and several tasks in one call save the lead one round for each task. The move covers the one failure that a team of subscriptions meets every day, a plan limit, without the lead doing anything, and it never repeats work that already wrote to the folder.

**Source.** The owner's words of 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/biz/OWNER-ADDENDUM.md`: "truly good, thoughtful inbuilt delegation abilities to smaller cheaper models eg haiku sonnet Luna terra and muse, all subscriptions should be used for their strengths and they have to be able to work together seamlessly and efficiently without significant overhead"; BRIEF.md section 8). Design: `ultra/biz/DELEGATION-DESIGN.md` sections 7, 8, 9, 11 and 15. Code: `lib/team-run.mjs`, `lib/tools/40-delegate.mjs` (the seven edits), and the battery edit in `build/v06/gw-02-tools-registry/registry-builtins.test.mjs` (the property list of `delegate`). Tests: `build/v06/ultra-w3-team-run/`.

**Status.** Built and tested on 13 Nov 2026 with stand-ins only (no real model, CLI, provider or program got a prompt): 70 tests pass at the report (the 64 that need no window pass again on 13 Nov 2026: unit, tool, load and real), among them three against the real `lib/team.mjs` and `lib/team-log.mjs` and six on the real page in a hidden window (light, dark and 480 px, pictures looked at); the ten suites the plan names pass with the one line changed in the tools registry suite. Not tested against the owner's real sign-ins. The two edits in this file that the team module needs (item 115) are in: it reads `picked.empty === true` and runs such a task on the model of its agent type, and it asks for the plan-use reading when a task is handed over, every time, in the background and never waited for. The team's step row reads "Team" with the layers icon (the two entries are in `public/app.js`, `TOOL_NAMES` and `TOOL_ICONS`), and the page test of this package now looks for that name. Open: (1) The tool's definition grew by about 1,400 characters (about 350 tokens for each lead that is offered the tool); the design estimated about 160. (2) The move after "took too long" is tested at the level of the module only, not on the page. (3) The module reads a few neighbours that are not in this build; it treats each as missing, and what it does without them is the behaviour described above. The rest of the delegation design is built later and is not part of this item.

## 117. The team is arranged on one section of Settings, and the page only shows what the server holds

**Decision.** Settings > Agents has a section, Team (`public/ext-team.js`, `public/ext-team.css`), and delegation gets no other new surface: no strip, no tab, no title-bar part. The Agents board and the chat's step rows already show every worker (item 61), and the team runner adds the model to those rows.
- **What it shows.** The switch with its notice; the suggested team with one button, Use this team; each kind of work with its models in the owner's order, a state dot with the word and the reason, arrows, Remove, and a menu with an Add button; the last 7 days of delegated work for each subscription beside its plan use; the other models with each one's size and where the size comes from ("guessed from the name" is said) and a way to set it; and the switch for models billed per token.
- **The server owns every fact.** Each change is one `POST /api/team` with the whole record, and the page draws the server's answer. "Saved" is said only after that answer. A refusal shows the server's sentence and nothing on the page moves. A read that a saved change overtook is thrown away.
- **No press is lost and none is doubled.** Changes go to the server one after another in the order they were pressed; one that is pressed while another is on its way waits for that answer and is made on the record the server holds by then. A switch ends as it was pressed last. Nothing is disabled while a change is on its way.
- **A menu chooses; a button changes.** The Add menu changes nothing by itself, because the arrow keys and letters move through a closed menu: the Add button, or Enter in the menu, adds the model it shows.
- **The page says only what it can know.** With the team off, a kind with no model says its task uses the model of its agent type. With the team on it says that the team does not choose for that kind and what to do, under the kind and under the switch; it says "refused" only where the server's dry run of the choice refuses, and never which model the runner then uses, because no route reports that. A model billed per token says Off while its switch is off. With no suggestion the text names no button.
- **Nothing happens until the owner acts.** The team is off until he turns it on or presses Use this team. A team he arranged is replaced only after a question. A suggestion with no model in it is not offered. Nothing is asked of the server before the section is drawn, and the page has no repeating timer: it reads again when the server says the team or the sign-ins changed.
- **Keyboard and narrow windows.** Every control is a button, a switch or a menu in reading order; each arrow, Remove and Add names its model and its kind; focus stays on the control that was used after the list is drawn again, and with the person when they moved on. At 480 px nothing is wider than the section.
- **Notices.** The page draws two registered notices through the guarded call, each after the owner turned something on (`delegation`, `api-keys-spend`), and names no new one.
- **A missing part is said calmly.** A build without the team's routes says "This build of Plenum has no team yet."; any other failure says the reason and offers Try again.

**Reason.** The owner asked for delegation to cheaper models that uses every subscription for its strengths, with little overhead. He can only trust it if he sees who does what, in his order, and what each model can do now, and if nothing changes behind him. One section that is a plain view of the server's record keeps that true: there is no second copy of the team in the page that could drift, and a later kind, model or subscription appears with no change to the page. The review of the first build showed where a page goes wrong when it says more than it knows: it promised the agent type's model for a kind with no model while the runner refused the task, and it called a model Ready that the team would never use. So each sentence about what will happen is either the server's own or is left out.

**Source.** The owner's words of 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/biz/OWNER-ADDENDUM.md`, the delegation request quoted in item 115). `DELEGATION-DESIGN.md` sections 5.3, 5.5, 9, 10 and 12. The review of the package (13 Nov 2026). Code: `public/ext-team.js` (`save`, `queue`, `send`, `flip`, `askEmptyRule`, `paintWarn`, `kindEl`, `workerEl`), `public/ext-team.css`; the routes in `lib/routes/team.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 and fixed the same night after the review. Tested at the report: `build/v06/ultra-w3-team-page` (40 tests: 13 on the files, 27 in the real page in a hidden window, keys sent as keys, both themes, 1280 and 480 px, with a stand-in for the routes and against the real team routes of a sealed server); 15 deliberately broken copies of the panel each fail the test for their behaviour. The populated states (ready, at its limit, a suggestion with models, usage numbers) were tested with the stand-in only, because a sealed server has nothing installed or signed in. The runner now runs a task of a kind with no model on the model of its agent type (item 116; `lib/team-run.mjs` reads the team's answer with `empty: true`, tested in `build/v06/integ-core/team-seam.test.mjs`). The page says so since 7:20 AM on 13 Nov 2026 (`public/ext-team.js`, `EMPTY.type` and `askEmptyRule`): with the team on, a kind with no model reads "No model yet, so the team does not choose for <kind>. A <kind> task uses the model of its agent type, as with the team off.", where the server's own dry run of the choice answers `empty: true`; it says "refused" only where that dry run refuses, and only what is sure when there is no answer. It was looked at in the real page with the team off, on with no model, and on with one kind filled. Tests of this package with the pins of the new sentence: `static.test.mjs` and `page.test.mjs`, 40 of 40 on 13 Nov 2026. Not built: adding a kind of work of the owner's own and changing the two numeric limits from the page (the routes accept both).

## 118. One product: Plenum starts with Windows in the tray, brings Plenum Hub up hidden, and one section says what runs

**Decision.** After setup the only thing on screen is Plenum's window and its tray icon. Three things make that so, each off until the owner turns it on unless said otherwise below.
- **Background services** (Settings > General, `public/ext-services.js`, `GET /api/services` in `lib/routes/services.mjs`). One row for each thing Plenum runs or depends on, with a state dot, the state in a word, the reason in a sentence and at most one button: Plenum's own server with its build line; the sign-ins (how many can answer now; the button opens Accounts); and Plenum Hub and its helper. So this section is also where setting up is finished. The route answers from memory and cached readings: it starts no CLI, reaches no network and never waits for the hub's starter. A reader that fails shows as "Not known yet" and breaks nothing. There is no registry of services: the rows are read by one list of readers in that route file, so a later service is one more reader, and a reader that has nothing to say answers no row.
- **The hub's starter, contract version 2** (`lib/hub-starter.mjs`). Plenum runs the hub's launcher (`Tabletalk.cmd` through `cmd.exe /d /s /c`, `tabletalk.sh` elsewhere; never `cli.js`) hidden, with nothing on its input, arguments checked against one pattern and a time limit: `state --json` (8 s), `up --json` (30 s), `wake <agent> --json` (15 s). The answer is the last line that is a JSON object. Exit code 2, no such line, or an answer without `"contract": 2` is an older starter and is said so, not a failure. The answer is taken when the launcher's own process ends (not when its output closes), because `up` leaves a job behind; once an answer line was read the process tree is never ended, and it is ended only when the limit passes with no answer. Unknown fields are ignored, an unknown value is "unknown", `null` means not known and never no, an `admin_url` that is not plain http on this computer is dropped, and the hub's sentences are scrubbed of keys and cut at 240 characters. One call of each command at a time. Nothing of the output is logged. `wake` is in the starter and no screen uses it yet.
- **What the launcher inherits.** Its environment is Plenum's without anything that looks like a key and without what is Plenum's own: a name that ends in `_PAT`, every `ELECTRON_*` variable, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `PORT`, `MUSE_CONSOLE_*` and `PLENUM_*`, whatever the case of the name (the list `ENV_DROP`). `up` hands its environment on to Docker Desktop, to the hub's helper and to every agent the helper wakes; inside the desktop app Plenum's server has `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, and an Electron program that inherits it runs as plain Node and shows no interface. `lib/hub.mjs`, which starts the same launcher for `agent-key` and `owner-status` (item 93), uses the same list (`STARTER_ENV_DROP`; it cannot import `lib/hub-starter.mjs`, which imports it).
- **The hub's row** follows `state`: no starter in the folder (the button opens Settings > Agents > Plenum Hub); an older starter; not set up ("Set up the hub", which lets the hub's launcher install and build in the background); setting up or starting (the hub's own message with a progress mark); not running with Docker running ("Start"); not running with Docker closed or not known ("Start anyway", after the sentence that starting the hub opens Docker's own window); running with its helper stopped ("Start the helper"); running, with the version and whether the helper starts with Windows when that is known. When the hub says why it is down (what its last start ended with), that reason comes first. A sentence of the hub that needs the person is shown as it is and never takes the way to start away: the starter keeps that sentence after a failed start until the hub runs again, so "Check again" alone stands only where Plenum can do nothing (something must be installed before the setup, or Docker Desktop is not on this computer). The state is asked while the section is on screen (a lease of 60 seconds that each read renews), every 5 seconds while something is starting and for about 30 seconds after an `up` that answered "starting" (the helper takes a few seconds, and the row says "Starting" meanwhile), at most once a minute otherwise, and not at all with the section closed and nothing starting.
- **Bring Plenum Hub up when Plenum starts** (a switch in the section; `services.json` in the data folder, `{ v: 1, hubAtStart: null | true | false }`). `null` follows Connect agents, so it is on exactly when the owner connected his agents to the hub; this is the one thing in the item that can be on without a press of its own, as the design says. With it on, Plenum asks `state` 20 seconds after its routes loaded at the earliest, and calls `up` by itself only when nothing can show on the screen and nothing is installed: the setup is ready, the hub is down or its helper stopped, and Docker is running or the engine is local. At a sign-in to Windows Docker Desktop is usually still starting at that moment. So while Docker being closed or not known is the only thing in the way (or the License Agreement is not accepted yet, or the starter did not answer), Plenum reads the state again once a minute, for ten minutes at most, and calls `up` once when it fits. Reading the state starts nothing. `up` is called by Plenum at most once in a run; after it the state is followed every 5 seconds until it settles, ten minutes at most. In every other case it starts nothing and the row says what is needed.
- **Start with Windows** (a switch in the section; `desktop/handlers/autostart.cjs`, messages `autostart-get` and `autostart-set`, no hooks). It sets Electron's login item with the argument `--background`. Started that way, `desktop/main.cjs` keeps Plenum in the tray and shows no window until the person opens it (two one-line edits: the constant `BACKGROUND`, and the body of `ready-to-show`); without a tray the window shows. It is offered only in the installed app on Windows: the installer's "Uninstall Plenum.exe" lies beside the program and the build is not the portable one. The portable and zip builds are packaged too, but the portable one runs from a temporary folder, so its login item would name a program that is gone. Elsewhere the switch is off and says "Start with Windows works in the installed app."
- **A guide, `docs/EXTENDING.md`**: how to add a route, a panel, a tool, a prompt part, a turn hook, a desktop handler and a notice; the data that grows without code; where a new background service goes; the state files with their versions; the events; the test each addition needs; what a feature must never touch. It is not part of the installed app.

**Reason.** The owner, 13 Nov 2026 at 12:35 AM: "this should be standalone software if possible, or at least the software should autostart and activate other software it depends on in the background, without it all appearing on the display. just the plenum gui should be user facing, once everything is set up. setup should aslo be simple." and at 12:20 AM "The software should also be able to be updated and changed for future feature additions". What follows from them:
- Plenum never stops or rebuilds the hub, never runs Docker, and never changes hands-off mode or whether the hub's helper starts with Windows: those belong to the hub's side of the contract, and the section says so. Starting goes only through `up`, which is the hub's own guard against a second copy.
- Docker Desktop opens its own window when it starts. So Plenum calls `up` by itself only while Docker already runs, and a button that could open that window says so first ("Start anyway"). The section's intro speaks of Plenum only ("Plenum itself opens no window for these.").
- "Activate other software it depends on in the background" has to hold at a real sign-in, when Docker is not up yet 20 seconds after Plenum: hence the wait of ten minutes. One look, as the design's words had it, left the hub down for the whole run.
- A row must never be a dead end. The review found one: after a failed "Start anyway" the hub keeps its sentence ("Open Docker Desktop and wait until it says it is running."), and the row then offered only "Check again" until Plenum was restarted.
- Three rules keep tests and a fresh install away from the owner's real hub, whose folder is the default: with `PLENUM_NO_SERVICES=1` nothing is ever started (`why: "off"`); nothing is started before the License Agreement is accepted, whoever asks (`why: "terms"`); and no timer exists in the smoke mode.
- The section's sentence about Start with Windows names the switch as the page calls it ("Keep in tray on close", under Desktop). The design's wording ("Stay in the tray when the window is closed") is not a label that exists.

**Source.** The owner's words of 13 Nov 2026 at 12:20 AM and 12:35 AM in `ultra/biz/OWNER-ADDENDUM.md` (BRIEF.md section 8), quoted above. `ultra/biz/BUSINESS-DESIGN.md` section 7, `ultra/biz/WAVE3-PLAN.json` (entry "services", fourth revision), the hub's contract `docs/PLENUM-INTEGRATION.md` version 2 in the hub's folder (read only), `ultra/hub/HUB-V2.md`; the review of the package (three major and five minor issues, with its probes in `ultra/work/review-ultra-w3-services/`), and the hub starter's source for what `state` answers after a failed `up` (`packages/starter/src/up.ts`, read only). Code: `lib/hub-starter.mjs` (`createHubStarter`, `createHubWatch`, `ENV_DROP`), `lib/hub.mjs` (`starterEnv`), `lib/routes/services.mjs`, `public/ext-services.js`, `public/ext-services.css`, `desktop/handlers/autostart.cjs`, `desktop/main.cjs` (`BACKGROUND`, `ready-to-show`), `docs/EXTENDING.md`. Tests: `build/v06/ultra-w3-services`; for the launcher's environment in `lib/hub.mjs`, `build/v06/integ-core/hub-starter-env.test.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026; reviewed once (the review failed: three major and five minor issues); all eight were fixed in a fix round, each with a test; the fixes themselves were not reviewed again. The section shows three rows in this build: Plenum, Sign-ins and Plenum Hub. The route also has two more readers for features that follow in a later build; each answers no row while the module it reads is not in the build (the test "a build without lib/routines.mjs has no Routines row" in `routes.test.mjs`). Tests (outside the repository, run sealed, against a stand-in starter in the suite's own folders; no test starts the real `Tabletalk.cmd` or calls the real hub), as of 7:00 AM on 13 Nov 2026: `build/v06/ultra-w3-services`, 96 tests (the starter 22, the watch and the start rule 17, the routes 22, the handler and the edit in `desktop/main.cjs` 8, the page's files and the guide 9, one sealed server with the page in a hidden window 18); the four files that need no port, no window and no `cmd.exe` stand-in (watch, routes, handler and the page's files) were run again on 13 Nov 2026 for this text: 56 of 56. Thirty-four one-line changes, one for each fixed behaviour, were each caught by a test, in a copy of the project. The sealed runners (`ultra/seal.sh`, `ultra/sweep.sh`, `build/battery3.sh`) set `PLENUM_NO_SERVICES=1` and `PLENUM_NO_SCHEDULER=1`; a server that a suite starts with an allow-list environment does not inherit them and sets them itself. Not proved, and owed before it is relied on: (1) Start with Windows and the `--background` start through a real sign-in to Windows in the installed app (a start from a Claude session sees a private copy of the registry, so no session can prove it); (2) `state`, `up` and `wake` against the owner's real hub, and with them that Docker Desktop and the hub's helper come up as they should when started from the installed app: that the launcher no longer inherits `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `PORT` and Plenum's own variables was tested with stand-ins only, for `lib/hub-starter.mjs` and for `lib/hub.mjs`. When Docker Desktop comes up later than ten minutes after Plenum started, the hub is not brought up by itself in that run; the row's button does it. `docs/EXTENDING.md` describes packages that follow in a later build and marks each such part "(later build)".

**Added 13 Nov 2026 (second build of that day; the hub's contract, `build/v06/hub-contract`).** The hub session's answers (`ultra/plan/hub-answers.md`, written from the hub's code on 13 Nov 2026) and two engineers changed these sentences of the Decision above. Where they differ, this paragraph holds.
- **Older is decided by the contract number** (`judge` in `lib/hub-starter.mjs`). An answer line with no `contract`, or one below 2, is an older starter. With no answer line at all, exit code 2 is an older starter (the usage text of a starter that does not know the command) and any other exit code is a failed call, not an older starter. An answer line with a contract of 2 or later is read whatever the exit code was. A contract above 2 is read as far as Plenum knows it, and the row says so ("This hub's starter speaks a newer version than this Plenum knows"). Never by `version`: that is the hub's own version and is null while the hub is down.
- **The starter's commands are a fixed list.** `lib/hub-starter.mjs` runs only `state`, `up`, `wake`, `agent-key` and `owner-status` (`COMMANDS`; `lib/hub.mjs` only `agent-key` and `owner-status`). Any other first word is refused before a process starts. `engine local` and `engine docker`, which the hub added for people, move the owner's data and can never be run by Plenum. The start at Plenum's own start still reads `state` before it calls `up` (a press on a button calls `up` at once): the hub's note said to call `up` and nothing else at sign-in, but the read is what tells Plenum whether Docker's window could open, and it starts nothing; the hub's own check of this module (13 Nov 2026) found that it fits the contract.
- **Words for a hub that runs without Docker.** The answer's `engine` is `docker` or `local`; an answer with no `engine` and a Docker value is from a hub that only ran in Docker and counts as `docker`; any other value is "unknown". Docker is named only for `docker`. With `local` the row says "It runs on this computer, without Docker." (running) or "It does not need Docker." (stopped), a stopped hub is offered **Start** (never **Start anyway**), "Docker is closed" and "needs Docker Desktop" are never said, and Plenum does not wait for Docker. With an engine that is not known the words are neutral (the section says "If the hub runs in Docker, it waits until Docker is already running."). `GET /api/services` answers `hubEngine` (`docker`, `local`, `unknown` or `null` before a reading) and the page words the two sentences of the section by it.
