The helper's line now reads "The hub's helper is set to start with Windows." or "...is not set to start...", only while the helper runs and the value is known.
- **`up` at its time limit.** At 30 seconds with no answer line the launcher's tree is not ended (the job `up` started and the hub's helper can be children of it). Plenum stops waiting, reads no more of it and answers "Plenum Hub is still starting. Plenum checks again every few seconds."; the 5 second follow of the state then does the rest. The sentence above, that the tree is ended when the limit passes with no answer, now holds for `state`, `wake`, `agent-key` and `owner-status` only. A quit of Plenum still ends a launcher that is running at that moment (see Open items).
- **`wake` by who asked.** `wake(agent, { by })`: `by: 'owner'` (the default, also when nothing is given) always runs. `by: 'handoff'` while the last `state` Plenum read said `hands_off: true` answers `{ ok: true, skipped: 'hands_off' }` and starts nothing, because the hub's helper starts the assignee itself inside its hourly limits (6 per agent and 30 in all), and a `wake` the owner asked for is outside them. A `hands_off` never read, `null` or `false` runs. The code `too_many` is read, and always says "Plenum Hub has started this agent too often this hour. Try again later, or start it from the hub." (the hub's own sentence is not shown). Nothing in this build calls `wake` yet.
- **The hub now runs with engine local.** The hub's own chat reported on 13 Nov 2026 that the owner's hub now runs without Docker. That is the hub's word: Plenum has not read it from the real hub, and the words above were tested against a stand-in starter only.
- **Tests, run sealed on 13 Nov 2026 for this text:** `build/v06/hub-contract`, 30 of 30 (`contract.test.mjs` 14, `contract2.test.mjs` 11 of which 6 fail on the starter as it was, `page.test.mjs` 5 in a hidden window, all against the suite's stand-in starter; no test starts the real `Tabletalk.cmd`).

## 119. An agent on Claude Code gets only the tools of its type: nothing of the person's own services, skills or settings files

**Decision.** Found on 13 Nov 2026 by the check of the programs package (`ultra/plan/check-programs.md`, Part A) and closed in `lib/claude-cli.mjs` by the held-hole engineer. A **held** run is one whose every request must reach `answer()` in `lib/claude-cli.mjs`: an agent of a type (`ctx.agent`: started by a hand-off, a build task, a team task or a council seat), a delegated coder of an older caller (`ctx.scope` and no type) and a council seat that may start read-only agents. Before, the child of such a run got `--tools <its type's list>` and `--setting-sources user`. `--tools` picks built-in tools only, so nothing took away the person's own Claude Code setup: the child also loaded the MCP servers and connected services of the person's user settings, and with the project's own Claude options saved, its "Always allowed tools" entries (as `--allowedTools`) and its MCP servers file. In the checker's stand-in run a held Researcher's request for a connected service's tool or for `Skill` was answered "allow" with no card in Auto-approve and in Approve all; in Ask first it got a card, so the type was asked about and not held to its list. The delegated coder had `--setting-sources user` and no strict MCP flag since f9cbdd6 (21 Oct 2026, Plenum 0.5.0) and typed agents had their tool list since 64154e0 (22 Oct 2026) (the engineer's `git log -S`).
- **The child's arguments.** A held run is in manual mode with no allow entries at all (it used to keep the project's entries that are not writes or commands), and gets `--strict-mcp-config` (the project's MCP servers file is left out), `--no-chrome`, `--disable-slash-commands` (`NO_OWN_SETUP`), and `--setting-sources=` in place of `--setting-sources user`: the one-argument form that Claude Code 2.1.296 itself uses for "no source" (`heldFlags`, decided by the one constant `HELD_SETTING_SOURCES`). A held Researcher's arguments are pinned exactly: `... --no-session-persistence --strict-mcp-config --no-chrome --disable-slash-commands --permission-mode manual --setting-sources= --tools Read,Glob,Grep`. What only takes away still reaches it: the project's blocked tools and its fallback model. A read-only run with no tool (a text-only seat, the lead's plan turn, an agent of a type with no file access) gets the same three flags and keeps its settings files: it has no tool that a rule could allow.
- **What `answer()` refuses.** A held agent is refused every tool that is not on its type's list (`cliToolsFor`), in every approval mode, before any card: "<tool> is not one of the tools <label> may use. Do not retry it; describe what is needed in your reply instead." The same holds for a Plenum type that the lead starts as a Claude Code subagent (held by its type) and for an older caller's coder (the coder's six tools). The tools on the list are answered as before in every mode.
- **Plenum's own bridge** is never given to a held run (`openBridge`); a tool named `mcp__plenum__...` in a held run's child is somebody else's server and is refused like any other. If a bridge were handed over, it would be the only MCP server and the only allow entry.
- **The lead's own turn is unchanged**: its settings files, its allow entries, its MCP servers file and the answers to its MCP and Skill requests.

**Reason.** A type's list on the Agents card is a promise to the person: a Researcher reads and reports. Two layers were open and one check cannot close both. The tools of the person's own services were there in the child, and Claude Code asks nobody for a call that an allow rule covers (its own help text), so nothing in `answer()` is reached for such a call. So the child is started without the person's setup, and `answer()` refuses what is still asked outside the list. The settings flag is one constant because the edit has one weak point that only a real run can test (below).

**Source.** `ultra/plan/check-programs.md` Part A (A.1 the proof, A.3 the edit, A.4 what it changes); the engineer's notes (`ultra/work/held-hole/NOTES.md`) and `claude --help` of 2.1.296, which lists `--strict-mcp-config`, `--no-chrome`, `--disable-slash-commands` and `--setting-sources`; the engineer read the installed binary's parser, which turns an empty value into "no source". Code: `lib/claude-cli.mjs` (`NO_OWN_SETUP`, `HELD_SETTING_SOURCES`, `scopedClaude`, `readOnlyClaude`, `heldFlags`, `heldRun`, `offListDenied`, `answer`). Tests: `build/v06/held-hole` (`held.test.mjs`, `argv.test.mjs`, `paths.test.mjs`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `build/v06/held-hole`, run sealed again on 13 Nov 2026 for this text: 35 of 35 pass. On the code as it was before the edit 6 of the 35 pass (the engineer's run; the 6 are the tests of the stand-in and of what must not change). The engineer also ran 24 other test files that drive `lib/claude-cli.mjs`: 462 tests, 0 fail, and no pin had to change (his count; not run again for this text). Every Claude Code in these tests is the stand-in `claude.cmd` (`build/v06/agent-runner/fake-claude.mjs`); no real Claude Code was started with a prompt, so what a real Claude Code does with these arguments is read from its help text and from its binary, not seen. **Not closed here:**
- **The main chat's own turn.** It keeps the person's settings files, the project's allow entries and MCP servers file. An allow rule in the person's own Claude settings (or an "Always allowed tools" entry such as `Read`) lets a call of the lead pass without reaching Plenum, so `checkAccess` (the credential-folder check) is not run for such a call, and no card shows. By Claude Code's documented rule, not seen in a real run. This is how it was before; nothing changed for the lead.
- **Plenum types that the lead starts as Claude Code subagents** run inside the lead's process with the lead's setup, so the flags above do not reach them. `answer()` holds them to their type (the tests show it), but a call that an allow rule of the lead's covers never reaches it.
- **Only a real run can show** (start one Researcher on a Claude model in the installed app): that this Claude Code accepts `--setting-sources=`; that a sign-in still works with no settings file (an owner who signs in through `env` or `apiKeyHelper` in `~/.claude/settings.json` would see held agents fail to sign in; the fix is to set `HELD_SETTING_SOURCES` to `'user'`, which keeps the MCP, Chrome, skills and allow-entry closures and gives held agents the person's own allow rules and hooks again); that MCP servers of plugins stay out under `--strict-mcp-config`; and that a subagent's own tool list keeps MCP tools away from a Plenum type that the lead starts. Whether the managed settings of a company computer still apply to a held run is read from the binary and not seen.

## 120. The settings are never used before the saved state has arrived

**Decision.** Found and fixed on 13 Nov 2026 in `public/app.js` by the morning-g engineer (his first task). When the page starts, `S` (the page's copy of the state) holds the built-in defaults until the first answer of `/api/state` arrives. A new flag, `stateLoaded`, is false until the first successful `refresh()` or a state event (`applyState`). While it is false:
- the model, Advisor and effort menus are blank (nothing selected, so the width does not jump) and cannot be used;
- a message sent with text keeps its text in the box, and a toast says "Plenum is still loading your settings. Try again in a moment." (the submit handler, and `sendMessage` for any other caller);
- an edit of the council (a seat's model, role or effort, Add seat, ×, the moderator) is refused with the same toast (`queueSettingsSave`, which every council edit goes through).
When the state arrives the saved model, Advisor and effort show, and the next message goes out as it should.

**Reason.** What could happen before, as the engineer found it: the fault was first described as a message that might go out with the default model (`muse:muse-spark-1.2`). That is not so, because with no state there is no project and `sendMessage` stops at "Add a project folder first". What did happen was this. The model menu showed the built-in default as if it were the person's choice (the test shows it against the page as it was). A message sent in that moment was taken out of the box by the submit handler first and then refused with the wrong message, "Add a project folder first.", with the add-a-folder form opened: the person's text was gone and the cause was not named. And a council edit built its copy from `S.council ?? DEFAULT_COUNCIL`, which before the state arrives is the default council, and saved it: the person's saved council would have been overwritten by the default with one field changed. The bar's rule is that a person's model is never switched or used silently (BRIEF section 3, item 5: never switch a person's model silently), and a setting is not used before it is known.

**Source.** `ultra/work/morning-g/NOTES.md` (task 1); the engineer's probe holds the `/api/state` answer back in a hidden window. Code: `public/app.js` (`stateLoaded`, `renderTop`, `refresh`, `applyState`, `sendMessage`, the submit handler, `queueSettingsSave`). Test: `build/v06/morning-g/state-first.test.mjs` (with `state-first.cjs`); `build/v06/ultra-w0-integration/page.cjs` now waits for the saved model.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `state-first.test.mjs`, run sealed on 13 Nov 2026 for this text: 4 of 4 pass: the three menus show no choice and are disabled while the answer is held back; a message sent then stays in the box and no `/api/chat` request goes out; after the state arrives the saved model (the offline model), Advisor and effort show and a send goes out; and the same probe against the page as it was shows the default model in the menu (the engineer: 2 of the first 3 tests fail on the old page). Proved by reading the old code and not by a test: the cleared text with the wrong message, and the council overwrite; no test starts a council edit before the state arrives, and the old-page probe does not assert the cleared text. The blank menus are not a loading indicator; the window is short when the server answers (Plenum's own start was made fast in item 91). If the first answer never comes, the menus stay blank and sending says it is waiting; the connection line of the page still says the server cannot be reached. The engineer saw one failure in six runs of the neighbouring hidden-window suite (`ultra-w0-integration` page test "Add seat with no Antigravity"), whose cause was not found.

## 121. The second look at the pictures: what changed on the main page and in Settings, and what was left

**Decision.** On 13 Nov 2026 four engineers looked at the page in a hidden window (1280 and 480 px wide, light and dark) and fixed what a person would see. Each line below is one thing a person can see now.

*The main page* (`public/app.js`, `public/style.css`, `public/ext-*.js` and `.css`, the morning-f engineer):
- **The Artifacts pane with no artifact open** (opened by its name, for example from the phone's menu) no longer shows Preview, Code, Copy, Save to project and Allow web access with nothing to act on. It says one sentence: "No artifact is open. When a reply holds a page, a drawing, a Markdown document or a long block of code, press Open on that block to see it here." When the chat holds artifacts, the sentence is "No artifact is open. Pick one from the list, or press Open on a block of code in the chat." with the list ("Pick an artifact"). The bar and the web switch come back with the first artifact.
- **The narrow sidebar (the 56 px rail)** with no project shows only the + button, not a sentence of one word per line. The add-a-folder form, which used to open inside the rail and was 1552 px tall, is a 320 px card over the page on windows wider than 860 px (under the + of the sidebar, or beside the rail). Its folder box and buttons come first and the notice after them; the form no longer pushes the foot of the sidebar out of the window. On a narrow window the sidebar is a menu, the form stays in it, the menu's close button sits at the end of the brand row and the project list keeps its height.
- **The line under the message box** leaves out whole phrases when it is too short for all of them, so it never ends in a cut word; while a run goes it reads "Esc stops · Enter queues what you type" (the status items beside it already say "Working..."). Under 560 px the box's own hint is the short sentence "Ask, review or plan. Type / for commands."
- **The title bar on two rows** (the window is narrow) shows the project's name alone; the thread tabs under the bar name the thread, so "atlas-web" is no longer cut to "atlas-web...".
- **The side pane's tabs:** when the strip is too narrow for every name, the tabs that are not selected show their icon alone, with the name as tooltip and for screen readers; nothing is cut, a count badge stays whole and the selected tab is brought into view.
- **Quick compare** opens at its top (it used to open scrolled down by 836 px, because the prompt box took the focus). The **setup guide's** footer rests at the foot of the drawer. In **Running now** the status pill starts at the row's left edge.
- **A model that Muse Code no longer offers** (`lib/muse-models.mjs`): the message reads "Muse Code does not offer <name>, model id <id>, to this account any more.", not "<name> (<id>)", which came out as "M 1 (m-1)" when the name already ended in a bracket. The start of the sentence, which the page looks for, is unchanged.

*Settings* (the morning-s, morning-s2, morning-g and morning-h engineers):
- **Plenum Hub card** starts as "Checking" with its boxes hidden and draws again once it is in the page, so it never claims a waiting list or agents before it has read them.
- **Agent types and limits** is a card with its own title; its type cards are 12 px apart; the "Build only" pill is gone and the Planner's line says "A model cannot hand work to it." (it is used by /build alone).
- **API keys:** the three routes (Claude, Gemini, Codex) are blocks 20 px apart and the terms links line up with the buttons. A switch that is on with no key saved says "The switch is on, but no Google AI key is saved, so Gemini uses its Google sign-in for now. A saved key will be used instead." (the same for Codex and the OpenAI key). Claude on the API with no Anthropic key saved says "No Anthropic API key is saved, so Claude cannot run through it yet." with what to do (`GET /api/keys` now gives `routes.claude.saved`); it no longer says that a key is in use.
- **The keys note under plain Node** names the variable that is true for the card (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY for Claude, OPENAI_API_KEY for Codex) and says to start Plenum from its installer to save keys; the error of a Save names the one variable of the key being saved (`lib/keys.mjs`, `assertCanSave`).
- **Team:** the suggestion's intro says "the models that can run now", not "signed in to"; a fact such as "Claude asks before every write when it runs through Plenum, so it leads Code and Test." is shown only while the suggestion follows it.
- **Background services:** the Sign-ins row says **Ready** and "N of M providers are ready."; the hub's helper line is "The hub's helper is [not] set to start with Windows."
- **Roles:** the Advisor menu shows "Off" or the model's name, not "Off No second opinion" (a classic select flattens the option's two spans; `menuOption` now gives the option a plain label). The Auto-compact "tokens" unit stays beside its box at 480 px.

With no project, Rules and This project show **Add a project folder** (it leaves Settings, opens the sidebar menu on a narrow window and opens the form); Safety says "No project." and the This project choice says "Needs a project. Add a project folder first."

**Reason.** The owner's words of 12 Nov 2026 at 10:15 PM (BRIEF section 7): "i want it looking and functioning as well as an apple product tomorrow so i can utilize it." The first pictures of the new look (item 92) showed small faults a person meets in the first minutes: a pane of dead buttons, sentences cut in the middle of a word, a switch that looks broken, a menu that says two things at once. Each was checked in the live page first; the ones the engineers found to be harness artefacts were left alone.

The pins of old sentences were corrected where a sentence changed on purpose (for example `legal-page.test.mjs` "Will show", "Nothing is hidden." and the new classes, `gw-05-page-registries/page-static.test.mjs`, `ultra-f2-store-backups/page-backups.test.mjs`, the tests of the Muse message). The engineers' sweeps (their numbers, 13 Nov 2026): morning-f 65 files, 1044 pass, 0 fail, and 70 of 75 further files exit 0 (the five that fail need the set-aside business and programs panels, or are stale staged copies of the phone app); morning-h 65 files, 1023 pass and one file fixed after it (`gw-05` page-static, 21 of 21); morning-s 65 files, 0 failing. **What no test proves:** the Artifacts empty state, the rail and the add-form card, the hint pieces, the pane tabs, the title bar, Quick compare's scroll, the setup guide's foot, the Running now pill, the Hub card's first paint as a picture, the Music card and the Rules tab row. For those the engineer looked at pictures in a hidden window (`ultra/work/morning-*/shots`, 1280 and 480 px; light and dark for Settings) and read the CSS; no test of its own was written (there is no `build/v06/morning-f`). The Settings pictures were checked a second time by the checker, who did not open the sixty scenes whose cards were not touched.

## 122. A new profile starts with the Advisor off

**Decision.** `defaults()` in `lib/store.mjs` has `advisor: null`. A person who starts Plenum for the first time (no state file) has no second-opinion model until they pick one in Roles or with `/advisor`. A state file that has no `advisor` key at all is one that an older build wrote when its default was Claude Opus 5.5, so it keeps that value (`LEGACY_ADVISOR`): nobody's advisor changes on its own. A saved choice, a model or Off (`null`), is read as saved. A value that is neither text nor `null` (a file an older build damaged) heals to Off.

**Reason.** The old default, `anthropic:claude-opus-5-5`, needs an Anthropic API key (the engineer's finding, `ultra/work/morning-h/NOTES.md` item 6), which a first-time person has not added: the Advisor was set to a model that the person could not use yet. Off asks nothing of a new person and costs nothing; turning it on is one pick in Roles or `/advisor`. What a review does with no key was not tried for this text.

**Source.** `ultra/work/morning-h/NOTES.md` (item 6). Code: `lib/store.mjs` (`LEGACY_ADVISOR`, `defaults`, `asState`). Tests: `build/v06/morning-h/advisor-default.test.mjs`; `build/v06/fix06-agents-tools-providers/models.test.mjs` was corrected to expect Off (the engineer: 6 of 6).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `advisor-default.test.mjs`, run sealed on 13 Nov 2026: 3 of 3 pass (a new profile starts Off; a person's own advisor and an explicit Off stay; a state file written before the advisor existed keeps the old default). The "heals to Off" line is read from `asState`; no test of its own. A profile that already had a state file written by the build before this one has the `advisor` key and keeps it, whatever it says. Not known: how many of the owner's own profiles have no `advisor` key (none was read; the owner's state file is not read for this work).

## 123. The shell keeps a real log, no longer waits for the registry, reads its switches from the server, opens again after a second launch during a quit, copies details from its boxes and leaves a starting hub alone at quit

**Decision.** Found by the overhaul audit on 12 Nov 2026 (ops-1, shell-5, shell-1, shell-3, shell-4, the log line of ops-6 and the "Copy details" button of ops-4) and left out of the first night for lack of budget (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P4 and P5), together with the open item of item 118: a quit of Plenum ended a hub launcher that was still starting the hub. Done on 13 Nov 2026 in `desktop/main.cjs` and four new files that import no Electron, so that a test can hand each one its file system, clock, `reg`, dialogs or process table: `desktop/log.cjs`, `desktop/registry-env.cjs`, `desktop/error-box.cjs` and `desktop/process-tree.cjs`.
- **The log (`server.log`).** Every line the shell writes starts with its time (ISO, UTC); what the server prints is written as it comes, with nothing added. The file has a cap of 5 MB: at the cap `server.log` is renamed to `server.log.1` (the one before is replaced) and a new file starts; a rename that fails (a locked file on Windows) is skipped, tried again only after another megabyte, and never thrown. A write that fails (a full disk, a locked or removed file, a path that is a folder) is an error event of the stream, which is caught: the log stops writing, counts the lines it drops, opens the file again after 30 seconds and then writes one line saying how many were lost; a stream that holds more than 1 MB waiting to be written drops new lines. The shell and the server's output pipes go on. The user's name in a path becomes `~`, and a key shape, a bearer value and the launch token never reach the file. The last 40 lines are also kept in memory. Every end of the server process writes one line: what kind it was (during a quit, asked for a restart, an older server, after it had answered, before it answered), the exit code and the last two lines the server had printed on stderr.
- **The start no longer waits for `reg`.** The environment variables of the registry (`desktop/registry-env.cjs`) are asked for when the app starts, both keys at once, each with a limit of 1.5 seconds; the answer never fails. Only the fork of the server waits for it. A complete reading is kept and given again when the supervisor starts the server again; an incomplete one is read again then. When a query fails or runs out of time, the server is started with the variables of the shell's own process, and its own look at the registry for a new CLI (item 113) still applies. Smoke mode and other systems read no registry.
- **The four switches are cached.** "Keep in tray on close", the finished alerts, keep-awake and the approval alerts used to be read by parsing the whole state file (4.8 MB on the owner's PC) at every state event and at every window close. The state file is now read once, at the first question (so a window closed early still honours the tray switch); after that `GET /api/desktop-settings` is asked once for a burst of state events (a wait of 250 ms, and at most one request a second). An answer that is not booleans, an error, a 404 and a slow server change nothing, and nothing is asked during a quit. The defaults are unchanged: the first three are on, the tray is off, and only an explicit `false` (or, for the tray, an explicit `true`) changes one.
- **A second launch during a quit.** A second launch used to find the lock taken and end, while the first copy was still quitting (it waits up to the shutdown wait for the server), so a double click on Plenum opened nothing. Now the first copy remembers it and, once the server has stopped, starts Plenum again once (without `--background`, so the window shows). Nothing is started again in smoke mode and when Windows is ending the session. A second launch while Plenum runs shows the window, and when there is no window it makes one and loads the page.
- **Copy details.** The question boxes of the shell (Try again / Open log / Quit, Reload / Quit, Wait / Reload / Quit) get a **Copy details** button before the last one, which is always Quit. It puts the box's words, the build line, the time and the last log lines on the clipboard (the user's name hidden), and asks the same question again. The numbers of the buttons the callers see do not move.
- **A hub launcher is left alone at quit.** `lib/hub-starter.mjs` tells the shell, over the process's own message channel, `{ type: 'hub-launcher', pid, state: 'started' | 'ended' }` for the launcher of the command `up` only: `started` when it has been started, `ended` when that process ends or fails (also after the call stopped waiting for its answer, item 118). The shell keeps the pids in a short list (at most 8, a plain process number only), asks no handler about the message, and clears the list when the server process ends. At a quit on Windows, with a launcher on the list, one hidden PowerShell call reads the table of processes (4 seconds at most) and `taskkill` ends the server and what it started except that launcher and what the launcher started. When the table cannot be read or does not list the server, the whole tree is ended as before, because a quit that leaves an agent's run behind is worse than one that ends a launcher. With no launcher on the list the table is not read and nothing changes. On other systems nothing changes.

**Reason.** A log that can run the disk full, has no time on a line and no end, and a shell that can fall over on a failed write, are what a person cannot recover from or report; the audit measured `reg query` taking 12.8, 12.9, 2.3 and 8.0 seconds before the server was forked (and again at every restart), and the 4.8 MB state parse at every event. A person who double-clicked Plenum while it was quitting saw nothing happen. A quit that ended the hub's launcher half way left the hub half started, which item 118 had named as open. The file helpers carry no Electron so that each rule is tested with a full disk, a hung `reg` or a made-up process table, not with a real one.

**Source.** Audit findings ops-1, shell-5, shell-1, shell-3, shell-4, ops-6 and ops-4 (`ultra/audit/findings/`) and open item O8 of `ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md` (sections P4, P5 and O8); the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-shell/NOTES.md`; item 118 (the open item) and item 113 (the server's own look at the registry). Code: `desktop/log.cjs`, `desktop/registry-env.cjs`, `desktop/error-box.cjs`, `desktop/process-tree.cjs`, `desktop/main.cjs` (`log`, `registry`, `settingsNow`, `fetchSettings`, `onSecondInstance`, `startAgainAfterQuit`, `reopenWindow`, `choose`, `noteHubLauncher`, `endServerTreeSparing`, `stopServer`), `lib/hub-starter.mjs` (the `leave` branch of `run`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 with tests in `build/v06/d-shell` (`log`, `registry`, `settings`, `shell` and `app`; the notes give no count). They test with stand-ins: a made-up file system and clock for the log, a `reg` that hangs, stand-in dialogs and clipboard, a made-up process table, and a hidden start of the shell on port 9160 (`app.test.mjs`: the shell runs with nothing on screen, exits 0, writes a dated log with a start block and no user name, and holds the server's output). The old shell is kept for the proof that these tests fail on it (`ultra/work/d-shell/old/desktop`, run with `D_SHELL_DESKTOP`). Pins in older suites were corrected on purpose (every log line now starts with a time, the launch token has one more use in the log's redactor, the log's flush wait); the originals are in `build/v06-before/d-shell/tests`. **Nothing here has run in the installed app on a real sign-in to Windows, with a real slow `reg`, with the real hub's launcher or with a real double click during a quit.** The 4.8 MB state read was not timed again. Open: the cap and the rotation were tested with small caps, not with a 5 MB file on the owner's disk; a job that the hub's launcher had already detached survives a quit as it always did. The owner's real check: sign in to Windows, quit Plenum while the hub is still starting, and see that the hub comes up; then double-click Plenum during a quit and see that a window opens.

## 124. A business is a folder with the owner's instructions and a record Plenum keeps

**Decision.** Plenum can hold businesses. A business is a record plus a folder: the folder (one of the owner's projects) holds his instructions (`BUSINESS.md`), a small state file (`STATE.md`), a plan (`PLAN.md`), playbooks and an outbox, so that any model can pick the work up cold from a short brief. The logic is `lib/business.mjs`, the routes `lib/routes/business.mjs` (all under `/api/business`), the hook `lib/hooks/70-business.mjs`, the prompt part `lib/prompt-parts/60-business.mjs` and the templates `lib/business-templates/*.json`. A project that belongs to no business behaves exactly as before: the chat prompt of such a project is byte for byte what it was.
- **The record is data in the owner's data folder**, never in the program folder: `businesses.json` (version 1, with a migration: an older and a damaged file load, a file from a newer Plenum is read and never written), `business-log/<id>.jsonl` (one line for each turn, run, skip, status change, answer of the owner, hand-off and flag; append-only; a full file of 4 MB is moved aside and kept) and `business-charter.json` (the sha256 of the `BUSINESS.md` text the owner last confirmed). Nothing is written until there is something to save.
- **Create never overwrites.** It writes only the files that are missing, only inside the home folder, by temp file and rename, and creates the template's routines switched off. A folder that is, contains or lies inside another business's folder is refused with a sentence that names that business. A template holds no model name.
- **What a lead is told** (the prompt part, chat only, order 60): the text of `BUSINESS.md` and `STATE.md` (indented, closed by "(End of BUSINESS.md.)" and "(End of STATE.md.)"), the last three finished turns, how to ask the owner (the `OWNER:` line of item 125), and a few rules (never type a password or pay, hand routine work to cheaper models, keep the plan and the state file current, stay inside this business's folders). A paused business still tells its lead; a closed one tells nobody.
- **Files read into a prompt are plain files inside the folder only.** A file is opened first and every check is made on the open handle and on the path as it resolves after the open (`openPlain`): a symbolic link, a junction on the way, a file with more than one name (a hard link), a secret file name, a guarded place and a file swapped between the check and the read are refused, and the page says which file and why. A read that failed for another reason is "unreadable", never "missing".
- **`BUSINESS.md` is the owner's.** Its hash is compared with the one he last confirmed before every turn, in the prompt and on the page. A change puts one flag line on the record (once per text), the lead is told once per text that what is new is unconfirmed, and Setup shows the text with one button, "I made this change". The confirmation is tied to the hash he saw (`GET /api/business/charter` gives the text and its hash from one read; `POST /api/business/charter-seen { id, seen }` answers 409 when the file is another text now). A business with no seal is never adopted silently: only Create seals, from the owner's own form.
- **Limits and pauses are the business's**: runs a day (12) and minutes of model time a day (180), counted from the record; the status (active, paused, closed); and Pause all. `mayRun(id)` answers for the routines.
- **The brief** (`GET /api/business/brief?range=day|week`) is worked out from the record and from memory: it starts no CLI and reaches no network. It counts as outside steps only what the owner approved (the five outside types, not refused); his refusals are counted apart. It carries `charterChanged`: what holds now, not what was flagged earlier.
- **Neighbours are loaded on first use** (the team, its log, the list, the routines, the hub); each part of the brief that comes from one is left out when it is not there. The module awaits no import at its top.
- **A stopped turn writes no turn line.** `server.mjs` runs the hooks' afterTurn only for a turn that was not stopped, and that stays so: the other hooks (snapshots, checks, the list) are written for it. A changed `BUSINESS.md` found at a stop is flagged at the next turn, the next prompt and the next read of the page.

**Reason.** The owner: "The idea is basically business manager/operator in a box, for numerous businesses simultaneously, with an owner that works another full-time job and does a lot of overtime." His attention is the scarce thing, so the state of each business is in files a cheap model can read cold, the limits are his, and his instructions cannot be changed under him by a model, a cloned repository or a hand-off program without his being told. The file checks close a real way out: a link where `STATE.md` should be would put any file of this PC into a model's system text.

**Source.** The owner's words of 13 Nov 2026 (BRIEF.md section 8; `ultra/biz/OWNER-ADDENDUM.md`). Design: `ultra/biz/BUSINESS-DESIGN.md` sections 2, 2.5, 2.6 and 5.4. Reviews and two fix rounds (`ultra/work/fix-business/NOTES.md`), the joint round (`ultra/work/joint/NOTES.md`, items 3, 4, 5, 10, 12, 13) and the integration (`ultra/work/integ-w3/NOTES.md`). Code: `lib/business.mjs`, `lib/routes/business.mjs`, `lib/hooks/70-business.mjs`, `lib/prompt-parts/60-business.mjs`, `lib/business-templates/`. Tests: `build/v06/ultra-w3-business/`, `build/v06/joint-w3/`, `build/v06/gw-03-prompt-parts/` (the list of prompt parts now has `60-business.mjs` and the part `business`).

**Status.** Built, reviewed, fixed twice and integrated on 13 Nov 2026. Proven by test against the live project: 82 of 83 pass in `ultra-w3-business` (one more is skipped: making a symbolic link needs a right this PC does not give the test, so the symbolic-link refusal is proven by the code path and the hard-link and junction cases only), the real routes of one sealed server with the five packages together in `joint-w3` (46 of 46), and `gw-03-prompt-parts` 39 of 39. Proven by stand-in only: every model turn is the offline echo model, which calls nothing; the team, the hub and plan use are stand-ins. Only a real run can prove: that a real lead keeps to `BUSINESS.md`, keeps `STATE.md` short and current and writes DONE and NEXT lines the brief can use; that the brief reads well after a week of real runs; the 4 MB turn-over of a record on a disk that is nearly full. The owner's real check: make one business from a template in his own folder, edit `BUSINESS.md` by hand, and see the page call it unconfirmed until he presses **I made this change**.

## 125. One list of what waits for the owner; silence is never a yes, and a yes is tied to the text he was shown

**Decision.** A lead that works for a business ends its reply with `OWNER:` lines; `lib/hooks/72-decisions.mjs` turns them into items of one list across all businesses (`lib/decisions.mjs`, `decisions.json` in the data folder); the owner answers each with one tap (`lib/routes/decisions.mjs`, under `/api/decisions`); the answer goes to the lead with its next message in the thread that asked and on the business's record.
- **The line**: `OWNER: <type> | <title> | recommend: <choice> | options: A / B / C | draft: outbox/<file>`. Only the title is needed. The type is question (the default), plan, send, publish, spend, account or delete. Only the trailing lines of a reply are read (an `OWNER:` line in quoted text in the middle is not); at most five lines of a reply become items; at most 20 wait per business; a title that already waits is not added twice.
- **Silence is never a yes.** An item waits until the owner answers it or says it is not needed. There is no default answer, no timer and no "answer all".
- **An answer is a refusal only when it is the No the program adds.** Every item ends with the server's own "No" (`item.refuse`). Whether an answer is a refusal is never read from the words of an option: a model's option "No problem, send it" is a yes. The record line carries `refused: true`.
- **A yes is tied to the draft he was shown.** The hash is taken when the draft is shown (`GET /api/decisions/draft` gives the text and its sha256 from the very bytes read) and checked when he taps (`seen`); a 409 says the draft changed and nothing is recorded. The hash is saved in the same atomic write as the answer.
- **The draft is opened first and checked on the open handle** (`openDraft`, added at the integration): the path is resolved and checked, the file is opened, and it must then still be a plain file with one name, the very file the path names after the open, with no link on the way. `readDraft` and `hashDraft` read from that handle, never from the path again. So a file or a link put in the draft's place between the check and the read is refused, not shown.
- **A send or a publish with no draft at all cannot be approved** (only the No, with "There is no draft to read, so this cannot be approved here."); a spend, an account and a delete may have none. A draft that is a link, a second name of a file, outside the folder or missing keeps the item, but it can only be refused. A later `OWNER:` line of the same title that brings a draft mends the waiting item instead of adding a twin.
- **A yes never pays and never sends by itself.** Every answer block to the lead carries that sentence as its second line, and every item carries it for the page. The owner pays himself; a send happens only through the step that shows its own card. The model's type cannot avoid the record: any item with a draft, and any item that talks of money, is written as an outside step (a money item as a spend) whatever type the model gave it.
- **Nothing is lost.** An answer whose line could not be written to the business's record is tried again with the next change of the list and found by its key, so it is written once. A list that cannot be read (locked, no access) is not an empty list: nothing is put in force and the file is never replaced by an empty one. A damaged list is kept (`decisions.damaged.json`, a second one as `decisions.damaged-<ms>.json`). An answered item is kept 30 days, and for as long as it is owed to the lead or to the record.
- **Two windows cannot both answer one item**: the second is a 409.

**Reason.** Coordinator's guidance 1, which the owner did not strike: one queue of decisions across all businesses, each a yes/no or a pick with the agent's recommendation, answerable in seconds. Because a tap can let text leave the PC in his name, the tap has to mean "this text", not "whatever is in that file by then", and a model must not be able to word its way into a yes or out of the record.

**Source.** BRIEF.md section 8 (guidance 1, 2 and 9). Design: `ultra/biz/BUSINESS-DESIGN.md` section 4. Two fix rounds (`ultra/work/fix-decisions/NOTES.md`), the coordinator's decisions of 13 Nov about 11:55 (`ultra/plan/cross-edits.md`), the joint round (items 1, 2, 3, 8, 12, 14) and the integration. Code: `lib/decisions.mjs`, `lib/hooks/72-decisions.mjs`, `lib/routes/decisions.mjs`. Tests: `build/v06/ultra-w3-decisions/`, `build/v06/joint-w3/`, `build/v06/integ-w3/draft-open.test.mjs`. `ultra/plan/w3-decisions.report.md` still describes the old answer block and the plain 30 days rule; the contract comment at the top of `lib/decisions.mjs` is what holds.

**Status.** Built, fixed twice, joined and integrated on 13 Nov 2026. Proven by test against the live project: 101 of 101 in `ultra-w3-decisions`; the flow through the real routes in `joint-w3`; the open-handle rule in `integ-w3/draft-open.test.mjs` (5 tests: a swapped file, a hard link to a file outside put there after the check, a draft removed after the check; all refused on Windows). Proven by stand-in only: the lines are written by the offline echo model, which repeats what it was sent. Only a real run can prove: that real leads (Claude, Codex, Gemini, Muse Code) write the `OWNER:` line at the end of a reply as told, pick true types, and act on the answer block in their next turn; how the list feels with a day's real items. Not built: answering from the phone's own screen (the Businesses pane is in the phone's bundle after the next phone build, untested there), a morning and an evening brief as a notification, and standing permissions ("this kind of message may go without asking"). The owner's real check: let a real lead end a reply with an `OWNER:` line, answer it with one tap, and see the lead act on the answer in its next message.

## 126. Routines: a lead started on a schedule as an ordinary chat turn, switched off until the owner turns one on

**Decision.** A routine starts a lead model at its time as an ordinary chat turn in the folder's "Routines" thread, on Plenum's own server, so it has the approvals, limits, Stop, checkpoints and saved history of any chat. The logic is `lib/routines.mjs`, the routes `lib/routes/routines.mjs` (under `/api/routines`), the file `routines.json` in the data folder (version 1, with a migration).
- **No Plenum tool or route lets a model create, change or switch on a routine.** There is no tool for it, the routes need the page's token, and the file is in the data folder every tool refuses. Routines arrive switched off, a template's too.
- **No timer exists until a routine is switched on.** The scheduler's only way in is `start()`, called by the route module when the server loads it; importing the module starts nothing. With `PLENUM_NO_SCHEDULER=1` or `PLENUM_SMOKE=1` there is never a timer. Before the License Agreement is accepted a tick does nothing.
- **A run is a request to Plenum's own server** (127.0.0.1 on its port, with the launch token). The scheduler first asks `GET /api/health` and goes on only when the answer names this very process, so a process that is not the server on that port is never sent anything else.
- **Inside the business's limits.** Without the business module nothing runs. A run is skipped, with a line on the record, when the business or everything is paused or a daily limit is reached; with no lead that can run it is tried once more after 30 minutes. In a folder where the Routines thread may not write, a routine can only read and report, and the row says so before it is switched on.
- **Plan use.** An unattended run is refused for plan use only when the lead is at or past the owner's stop line, or its provider can be read without a prompt and has no reading. Which providers can be read so comes from the reader table of `lib/quota.mjs` through `team.canReadPlanUse(provider)` (today Codex), not from a list in routines. The last reading that was a number counts until that plan's window resets (five hours when the reader gave no reset time), and the reason says its age ("was at 93% of its plan 40 minutes ago ..."). For a provider that cannot be read without a prompt the run starts and its line says "Plan use is not known for <model>, so the stop line could not be checked." A run that goes on is looked at against the line every 10 minutes. This needed 12 edits in `lib/team.mjs` (`canReadPlanUse`, `availability(ref, { lines, last: true })`); without `last` nothing changes for the delegate tool or the Team page.
- **Ask first while nobody is there**: each change waits five minutes for the owner and is then skipped; the run's line says how many cards were not answered. The row says this, and whether the Routines thread may write, before a routine is switched on.
- **A changed playbook waits for the owner.** A routine that runs a playbook file is saved with the file's hash; when the file is another text, the routine is held and the owner is asked; Keep (or a yes to Plenum's own question, read as anything but the refusal) takes the new text.
- **Missed times, restarts.** A time that passed is caught up within the catch-up window (six hours by default, never less than five minutes), else skipped with a line. A run cut off by a restart is marked "interrupted". After a failure the routine is tried once more after 30 minutes while that is still inside the catch-up window; after three failures in a row it is switched off and the owner gets an item that says why.
- **Stop** ends the run's turn; a routine that had not started yet then never starts, and the answer says what happened. Pausing or closing a business stops its running routine.
- **A follow-up run** lets the lead act on the owner's answers: only where a routine is on and the Routines thread asked.

**Reason.** Coordinator's guidance 4 (unattended for days: routines on a schedule with a record of each run; work that survives restarts, sleep and usage limits) for an owner who is at another job all day. A routine that is "just a chat turn" gets every guard the chat already has and adds none of its own to keep in step. Off by default, and out of every model's reach, because a schedule is a standing permission to spend his subscriptions.

**Source.** BRIEF.md section 8 (guidance 3 and 4). Design: `ultra/biz/BUSINESS-DESIGN.md` section 5. Two fix rounds (`ultra/work/fix-routines/NOTES.md`; `check-routines.md` N1), the coordinator's decision on the last reading, the joint round (items 6, 7, 8) and the live patch (`ultra/work/joint/live-edits/lib-team.mjs.patch.txt`). Code: `lib/routines.mjs`, `lib/routes/routines.mjs`, `lib/team.mjs`. Tests: `build/v06/ultra-w3-routines/`, `build/v06/fix-routines/`, `build/v06/joint-w3/schedule.test.mjs` and `live-team.test.mjs`, `build/v06/ultra-w3-team/`, `build/v06/integ-w3/smoke.test.mjs`.

**Status.** Built, fixed twice, joined and integrated on 13 Nov 2026. Proven by test against the live project: `ultra-w3-routines` 62 of 62, `fix-routines` 32 of 32, `joint-w3` schedule and live-team, `ultra-w3-team` 65 of 65 on the patched `lib/team.mjs`; smoke mode in `integ-w3/smoke.test.mjs` (loading every module of the five packages sets no timer; a sealed server in smoke mode with a routine switched on runs nothing across more than one tick and starts no program) and the hidden shell's own smoke start (`integ-core/app-start.test.mjs`, 3 of 3). Proven by stand-in only: every scheduled start in the tests runs with a clock moved by hand and stand-in timers, and every run is the offline echo model. Every test server runs with `PLENUM_NO_SCHEDULER=1`, so **the real timer in a real server has never fired in a test**. Only a real run can prove: a routine that starts by itself at its time in the installed app, after sleep, after a restart and after an update; the catch-up after the PC was off; the five-minute wait of a card with nobody there; the stop line with a real Codex reading; what a real lead does with a playbook over several days. The owner's real check: switch on one routine for a time two minutes ahead in the installed app and see it start by itself.

## 127. A hand-off can go to a program: a CLI on this PC or an agent on Plenum Hub

**Decision.** A lead can hand a task to a program, not only to a model: Claude Code, Codex, Muse Code, Gemini CLI, Antigravity, Cursor or Grok Build on this PC, or an agent the owner added by its name on Plenum Hub. The task runs inside that program with that program's own abilities; Plenum starts it hidden, follows it, can stop it and takes its result as a hand-off result. The record is `lib/programs.mjs` (`programs.json`, version 1), the runner `lib/program-run.mjs`, the routes `lib/routes/programs.mjs` (`GET` and `POST /api/programs`, `POST /api/programs/check`), the page `public/ext-programs.js` and `.css` (Settings > Agents > Programs).
- **Who decides.** The delegate tool's description carries one line per program, "best at", which the owner edits; the lead names the program in `program`, and the owner can name it himself. With no file the defaults apply and nothing is written until he saves. The first lines say only what the code makes true.
- **Never a silent switch.** A program that is switched off, not installed, signed out, at its plan limit, on another route or without a place on the hub is not replaced: the lead gets one sentence that says so and names the programs that can run now, in the owner's order.
- **Never more rights than the lead.** The same folders and the same approval mode. Programs that can ask send each step to Plenum; Codex and Gemini CLI cannot ask before they write, so Plenum lists what they changed.
- **A held Claude Code run has none of the owner's skills, servers or settings**: it is started with `--setting-sources=` and the tools of the task's agent type only (item 119). An allow rule in his own Claude settings therefore cannot apply to it. The page says this once (the server's sentence), not twice.
- **An agent on the hub**: Plenum creates a task for it, reads it back by its id only (never from a list), waits up to the entry's minutes (10 by default, 60 at most; at most three hub hand-offs at once) and takes the answer as the result, marked as another agent's words, ended at a line of Plenum's so a forged label is bent. Plenum opens nothing on this PC for it.
- **Starting the agent.** The agent states on the page come from the hub's own list (`hub_list_agents`, asked when the section is drawn and by "Check agents", never by a timer, with an agent Plenum already holds a key for): status, online, `presence.wakeable` and, since the integration, `presence.helper_can_start`. With hands-off mode on in the hub, Plenum calls no wake: the hub's helper starts the agent by itself (`wake(agent, { by: 'handoff' })` answers `skipped: 'hands_off'`, worded as the helper's start, never as "woken"). With hands-off off, Plenum asks the helper once; the sentence on the page follows `helper_can_start` and makes no promise (the hub can still answer that everything is paused or that the hourly limit is reached: `too_many`). A hub that does not send `helper_can_start` gets the sentences from before it.
- **Stop** on a hub hand-off cancels the task at once; a run the helper had already started ends within 10 minutes; pausing the agent in the hub stops it at once. The page and the result say so.
- **The task goes to a business room only with the owner's switch.** "Results in the business room" is off for every business until he turns it on (`shareRooms`). On: a short result line after a task on this PC, and the title and instructions of a hub task, go into that business's room. Off: none of that goes to a room. The words are "acknowledged", never "read".
- **The result on the record.** In a business's folder a hand-off writes `{ type: 'handoff', program, label, title, ok, text (300 characters), changed }`; the brief counts hand-offs by program, in words.
- **Everything the record answers is read from memory**: no CLI is started and the network is not reached by reading the page, except the one look at the hub's list named above.

**Reason.** The owner, 13 Nov 2026, 1:08 AM: "The tool should also be able to invoke the capabilities of codex/Claude code/Muse/grok bot as well, running in those respective programs. For example, if it's determined that a task is best run in Claude code, it should be started and run in Claude code with the output being utilized by the network". The lines the lead reads are his to edit because he knows what each of his subscriptions is good at; the switch per business is his because a room reaches agents on other computers.

**Source.** BRIEF.md section 8 (1:08 AM). Design: `ultra/biz/DELEGATION-DESIGN.md` section 15. Two fix rounds (`ultra/work/fix-programs/NOTES.md`), the hub chat's answers (`ultra/plan/hub-answers.md` sections 6 and 7; `ultra/plan/cross-edits.md`), the joint round (items 9, 10) and the integration. Code: `lib/programs.mjs`, `lib/program-run.mjs`, `lib/routes/programs.mjs`, `public/ext-programs.js`, `public/ext-programs.css`; live neighbours unchanged: `lib/claude-cli.mjs`, `lib/hub-starter.mjs`, `lib/hub.mjs`. Tests: `build/v06/ultra-w3-programs/`, `build/v06/joint-w3/handoff.test.mjs`, `build/v06/held-hole/`, `build/v06/hub-contract/`, `build/v06/integ-w3/hub-can-start.test.mjs`.

**Status.** Built, fixed twice, joined and integrated on 13 Nov 2026. Proven by test against the live project: `ultra-w3-programs` 156 of 156 (among them 17 that pin the live `lib/claude-cli.mjs`: `--setting-sources=` and no Skill for a hand-off), `held-hole` 35 of 35, `hub-contract` 30 of 30, `joint-w3` hand-off (a program on this PC and a hub agent, with the real business record), `integ-w3/hub-can-start.test.mjs` (4). The page was walked hidden at 1280 and 480 px in both themes on stand-in routes and on the real routes of a sealed server, by keyboard. Proven by stand-in only: every program is a stand-in CLI, the hub is a stand-in, the wake and the hub's state are injected. **No prompt has been sent to a real program and the real hub has not been called.** Only a real run can prove: a task that really runs in Claude Code, Codex, Muse Code, Gemini CLI, Antigravity, Cursor and Grok Build and comes back with its result and its changed files; that Stop ends each program's whole process tree; a hand-off to the owner's real Muse and Grok Bot on the real hub, with hands-off on and off, the wake, `too_many`, the cancel, and the room with the switch on; what `presence.helper_can_start` really says for his agents. Left out on purpose: a cached `listAgents()` in `lib/hub.mjs` and a `handsOff()` answered from memory in `lib/hub-starter.mjs` (each would only save a call). The owner's real check: hand one small task to Claude Code from a lead in the installed app and see its result and its changed files come back.

## 128. The Businesses pane: Today, Routines and Setup, and the page only shows what the server holds

**Decision.** One side pane, Businesses (`public/ext-business.js` and `.css`, found by the panel discovery; a title-bar button with the count of what waits), with three tabs. Today: the list of what waits and the brief of each business. Routines: each routine with its switch, when it runs, the last run, Run now, Stop, Edit, Delete and Keep. Setup: the first-business form, and for one business its name, status, leads, limits, hub room, files and `BUSINESS.md` with "I made this change".
- **The page decides nothing.** Options come from the server with the program's No last; a no is `choice === item.refuse` only; the first press on a yes to an item with a draft opens the draft and the second sends the hash that came with the text; a 409 is shown in the server's words and the draft is read again. While an opened draft cannot be read, only the No is offered.
- **The sentences are the server's**, said once above the list (whose words the options are, who chose the type, what a yes never does). One phrase is turned for the reader: the server writes "the owner pays himself" for the lead, and the page says "you pay yourself".
- **No promise the scheduler will not keep** (integration): while everything is paused or the scheduler is off, a routine's row says "It does not start by itself for now." instead of a next time, and the brief lists no next run.
- **What just happened is one line in a band** that rests against the pane's top edge while the pane scrolls (integration: it was a floating pill that lay across a row or a button). Pause all is said by its banner only.
- **Before a routine is switched on** the page shows the server's two facts for that folder (may the Routines thread write; what the approval mode means with nobody there) and the app's own notice about agents that act without asking.
- **Strict CSP holds**: no inline style, text only through `textContent`, every colour and size a token, no viewport query (the pane is laid out for its own width), everything reachable by keyboard, every control at least 24 px high, nothing wider than the pane at 1280 and at 480 px.
- **Not kept apart yet, and the page says so**: sign-ins and the browser profile are shared by all businesses.

**Reason.** "Looks now matter as much as function" (BRIEF.md section 7), and the pane is where the owner spends his seconds: one place for what waits, with nothing on it that the server would refuse and no sentence that is not true at that moment.

**Source.** Design: `ultra/biz/BUSINESS-DESIGN.md` sections 4.4 and 6; `ultra/ui/DESIGN-SPEC.md`. The joint round (items 1, 4, 10, 11, 13, 14 and "Still open") and the integration's walk (`ultra/work/integ-w3/shots/joint/`, 76 pictures: 19 states at 1280 and 480 px, light and dark; `shots/programs/` for the Programs section). Code: `public/ext-business.js`, `public/ext-business.css`. Tests: `build/v06/ultra-w3-business-page/`, `build/v06/joint-w3/page.test.mjs`.

**Status.** Built, joined and integrated on 13 Nov 2026. Proven by test against the live project: `ultra-w3-business-page` 60 of 60 (stand-in routes for every state, and the real routes of a sealed server), `joint-w3/page.test.mjs` 11 (the hidden live page on the real server with the five packages, walked by keyboard), and the stylesheet tests (64 of the 65 files of `ultra/ui/css-tests.txt` pass; the one that fails, `build/imp-12/page-and-wiring.test.mjs`, pins a line of `server.mjs` that another engineer was changing at the time). Looked at by the integrator in pictures of the live page: each of the 19 states in at least one picture at 1280 px (light) or 480 px (dark), 26 of the 76 pictures in all, and the states the fixes touched again afterwards; the other pictures of each state (the second width, the second theme) were taken and not all looked at. Proven by stand-in only: the items, runs and briefs on the page come from the offline echo model and from stand-in routes. Only a real run can prove: how the pane reads with a real business after a week (long titles, many items, a long brief), on the owner's screen and scaling, and in the phone client (the pane is in the phone's bundle after the next phone build and has not been looked at there). Not built: a view of the record line by line (the brief sums it up; `GET /api/business/log` exists). The owner's real check: open the pane with a real business after a day of work and look at it on his own screen.

## 129. A council or relay run that is cut off keeps what it had, and a failed run leaves one line in the log

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (orchestration-2, which is j-council-5 and rated high; orchestration-6, ops-6, ops-2, and w1-store-save-1) and built on 13 Nov 2026 in `server.mjs` (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P1). A council question was stored, but the seats' answers lived only in a local object and the one assistant row was added at the very end, after the moderator; a crash or a kill in between kept nothing, and the page said "no reply was saved". A relay hop that failed or was stopped left no row at all.
- **The council's row is a draft from the start.** Once nothing before the stream can throw, the server adds the row (`kind: 'council'`, the words "The council was cut off before it reached a verdict. The answers the seats had given are kept above.", the council's own record, `draft: true`) and saves quietly. The row is brought up to date, and saved quietly, at the start of the council, at each seat's answer, at the verdict and at an error. When the run ends, the same row is finished in place (`store.finishMessage`), so the chat has one council row, not two; a run with nothing to keep takes the draft out again. A start that is refused leaves no draft behind. The page does not draw a draft row while the run is live; it draws it from the state file after a restart.
- **A kill keeps the answers.** A draft that is still a draft when the state is read again is marked interrupted (the store's rule for every reply, B-29), so the page shows the seats' answers and says the council was cut off.
- **A failed or stopped relay hop is saved.** `saveRelayHop` now saves every hop that ends, with the text it had: a failed hop with its reason as the row's error (or "The hop failed."), a stopped hop marked `aborted` (the page draws "Stopped."). A hop that worked and has no text is not saved, as before. A failed or stopped hop's text is never taken as a seat's reply for a later relay (`relaySeats` skips it, and skips draft council rows), and its session is not forgotten: only a hop that worked makes the seat start a new session.
- **One line in the log for a failed run.** A council that ends with an error and no verdict, and a failed relay hop, write one line through the server's fault log: the kind of run and the models (at most eight, each cut to 80 characters) and the reason, cut to 300 characters. Never the question, a reply, a key or the launch token (the line goes through the same redaction as every other fault, and the same line is written once in ten seconds at most).
- **The state is saved on the way out.** `stopRunsAndSave` (the clean stop of a quit and of the fault handler) now also waits for `store.flush()`, so a draft save that still waits is written before the process ends. After `listen()`, the server also starts the child sweep of item 138 in the background (off in this build).

**Reason.** The bar is that a person never loses work a model already did. A council can run for many minutes and cost real money; two seats' answers that were on the screen were gone after a crash, with a page that said nothing was saved. A relay that ended in the middle left a gap in the chat. A failure that the page showed left nothing in the log to find it by.

**Source.** Audit findings orchestration-2 (= j-council-5), orchestration-6, ops-6, ops-2 and w1-store-save-1; plan P1 in `ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-run-records/NOTES.md`. Code: `server.mjs` (`COUNCIL_CUT_OFF`, `councilRow`, `handleCouncil`, `logRunFailure`, `saveRelayHop`, `relaySeats`, `stopRunsAndSave`), `lib/store.mjs` (`addMessage`, `finishMessage`, `removeMessage`, `markInterrupted`, all older). Tests: `build/v06/d-run-records/server.test.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `server.test.mjs`: 6 tests (a finished council leaves one row, finished in place; a kill after the seats answered keeps their answers and the restarted server shows an interrupted row, not "no reply"; a failed council writes one log line with the models and the reason and never a key, the token or the question; a failed hop and a stopped hop are saved; a hop that works is saved as before), all with stand-in models; the same file fails on the old `server.mjs` (the engineer's copy, selected with `RECORDS_PROJECT`). That a state refresh in the middle of a council does not draw the council twice is proved in the page test of item 131's package (`build/v06/d-status-truth/draft.test.mjs`, 4 tests), not here. **No real council, relay or provider was run.** Not done: the "Verdict based on A of N seats" line and Retry seat are still open (item 98). The owner's real check: start a council in the installed app, end Plenum from Task Manager after two seats have answered, start it again and see the two answers and the cut-off line.

## 130. A council seat whose model is gone says so before Send, a refused question comes back, and the example in a refusal is a model that exists

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (j-council-7, rated high) and built on 13 Nov 2026 in `public/app.js`, `lib/council.mjs` and `public/style.css` (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P2). A saved council whose seat names a model that is no longer in the catalog showed the raw id on the board with no warning, and Send was then refused with a 400 whose example was a retired id; the page had already pushed the person's message into the chat and put the refused question back only when files were attached.
- **A seat or the moderator whose model is not in the catalog** (a saved council from before a model was renamed or removed) shows the same line as a model its CLI no longer offers, in the same place on the board, before Send: "<Provider> does not offer this model any more. Pick another." An OpenRouter id is free text and is never in the catalog, so it never gets this line; nothing is said while the catalog has not loaded.
- **A question the server refuses before it starts** (a seat's model it cannot use, a file it cannot take, the spend limit) was never saved, so the row the page drew for it is taken out of the chat and the question goes back into the message box when the box is empty (as `/council <question>` in plain chat mode); files go back to the composer too. Before, this was done only when files were attached.
- **The example in a refusal is a model that exists today.** `refExamples()` in `lib/council.mjs` builds the example from the live catalog (Anthropic, Muse Code, Codex and Gemini, the first model of each that is not marked `offered: false`) at the time of the refusal, so a retired id is never the one the person is told to type; with none found it falls back to `anthropic:claude-opus-5-5`.
- **`/model`, `/advisor` and `/effort` wait for the saved state** with the toast "Plenum is still loading your settings. Try again in a moment." (as the menus and Send do since item 120), so none of them reports or changes a built-in default as if it were the person's choice.
- **The add-a-folder card floats over the page only on the desktop surface.** On the phone's own surface (`data-surface="phone"`) the form stays in the menu at any width (`public/style.css`).

**Reason.** A person must never be told a model is theirs when it is a default, never lose a typed question to a refusal, and never be sent to type an id that does not exist. The card rule keeps the add-a-folder form where the phone's own page needs it.

**Source.** Audit finding j-council-7 with its verdict; plan P2; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-page-truth/NOTES.md`. Code: `public/app.js` (`seatGoneWords`, `showGone`, `askCouncil`, `settingsNotLoaded`, `runCommand`), `lib/council.mjs` (`refExamples`, `checkRef`), `public/style.css` (`.add-project`). Tests: `build/v06/d-page-truth/page.test.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `page.test.mjs`: 5 tests (a seat with no catalog model says so in one sentence and the others stay quiet; a refused question comes back and leaves no user row; the refusal names the seat and its example is offered today; the card floats on the desktop surface only; the three commands wait for the saved state), run in a hidden window against a sealed server; the engineer's run: the old `app.js` fails 2 of the page tests and the old CSS and JavaScript fail the 2 static ones. Not done: a test that a council refresh draws twice (the page already skipped draft council rows while a live one is drawn; that test is `draft.test.mjs` of the package of item 131). No real provider was asked. The owner's real check: open a saved council that names a model Muse Code has dropped and see the line on its seat before pressing Send.

## 131. The Gemini card says what is true, and Accounts paints each card as its own answer arrives

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (page-setup-3, rated high) and open item 107 (Accounts waits for all its requests before it paints); built on 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P6 and O3). The Gemini card told everyone "Sign in to your Google account ... Sign in with Google" and never said that Gemini CLI no longer serves Google AI Pro, Ultra or free accounts (item 89), a reason that was only on the Antigravity card; and `signedIn` was true for a saved Google AI key and for a Google sign-in alike, so a person who had only saved a key was told "Gemini has a saved sign-in".
- **The server says which.** `geminiStatus()` (`lib/gemini.mjs`) adds `google` (a Google sign-in is on this computer) and `key` (a run goes through the saved Google AI key); `providerStatus` passes both on, and `GET /api/accounts` gives the Gemini card `{ installed, signedIn, email, google, key }`. With a saved key and a Google sign-in and the key route off, the run uses the sign-in, so `google` is true and `key` is false. The status route stays as fast as item 91 made it: no new blocking call, no CLI started.
- **The card says what it knows.** With `key` true: "Using your key", "Gemini runs on the Google AI key saved in Accounts, and its requests are billed per token. It needs no Google sign-in." With `google` true: "Signed in", "Gemini has a saved Google sign-in for <email>." With neither: "Not signed in" and the sign-in sentence. With the older shape (no `google` and no `key`) the card claims neither and says "Gemini is ready on this computer". In every state except the key route, a CLI that is not installed and a status that is not known yet, a quiet line says "Gemini CLI does not serve Google AI Pro, Ultra or free plans any more. Use the Antigravity card for those, or save a Google AI key.", and when Antigravity has reported the person's plan, "Antigravity reports your Google plan as <plan>. Gemini CLI does not serve that plan any more, so use the Antigravity card for it, or save a Google AI key." The button reads "Sign in again" when there is a Google sign-in (or, with the older shape, when `signedIn` is true), and "Sign in to Gemini CLI" otherwise.
- **Accounts paints as each request returns.** `GET /api/accounts` and `GET /api/accounts/cli` are asked together and each paints when it answers: the cards of Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI and Muse Code appear with the first, and the cards of Cursor, Grok Build and Antigravity say "Checking…" and offer nothing until the second answers, when `MuseUI.redrawAgentCards` puts the real cards in place (and the Gemini card is drawn again when Antigravity reported a plan). A CLI that is slow holds up its own cards and no other. The setup guide does the same (item 132).
- **A flaky test was a race of the test.** The harness of `build/v06/council-folder/page.test.mjs` waited for the council's folder menu to read "read", which is also the page's built-in default, so it did not prove that the saved state had arrived, and a change made earlier was refused by the page (item 120). It now also waits for the model menu to be enabled.

**Reason.** A card that says "saved sign-in" to a person who saved a key, or that sends a Google AI Pro owner to sign in to a CLI that will not serve them, wastes the first minutes of the product. One slow CLI held up a whole page of cards.

**Source.** Audit finding page-setup-3; open item 107 (Accounts and the setup guide waiting for all requests); plan P6 and O3; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-status-truth/NOTES.md`. Code: `lib/gemini.mjs` (`geminiStatus`), `lib/providers.mjs`, `server.mjs` (the `gemini` object of `GET /api/accounts`), `public/app.js` (`geminiWords`, `geminiCard`, `renderAccounts`), `public/ext-accounts-cli.js` (`pendingCard`, `redrawAgentCards`). Tests: `build/v06/d-status-truth` (`status.test.mjs`, `accounts.test.mjs`, `draft.test.mjs`); `build/gemini/test-gemini.mjs` was corrected for the two new fields.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `status.test.mjs` 5 tests (key only; Google sign-in only; both with the key route off; neither; the status route passes both on, stays fast and runs no CLI) and `accounts.test.mjs` 6 tests (Accounts paints the first read while the second is held back; the plan sentence by plan once Antigravity reported one; a key-only Gemini does not claim a saved sign-in; the older shape is not guessed at; the not-signed-in sentence; and an honest probe of the old page) in a hidden window. `draft.test.mjs` (4 tests) is the package's test that a council refresh in the middle of a run draws one card (item 129), with a probe that shows two cards on a page without the skips. The council-folder test passes 6 of 6 after the fix (the engineer's run; 4 of 5 before it, and the same on his backups, so the race was the test's). **No real Gemini CLI, Antigravity or Google account was involved**; the Antigravity plan text is tested with a stand-in answer. Not done: a 503 "still checking" answer of Accounts has no automatic second try (item 107); it was not looked at again. The owner's real check: open Accounts with a Google AI Pro account signed in to Antigravity and read the Gemini card.

## 132. When the chosen model cannot answer, one press uses one that can, and the setup guide says which model will answer

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (j-firstrun-3, rated medium) and built on 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P7). With only a Gemini CLI signed in and no Muse Code, the model stayed Muse Spark 1.2 and the banner said "Muse Code not installed, so Muse Spark 1.2 cannot answer yet" with only **Open Accounts**: nothing offered a model that works, and the setup guide never said which model would answer.
- **The banner's Use button.** When the chosen model's provider is not ready and another provider is, the banner above the message box gains **Use <name>** beside Open Accounts. The name is the first ready provider in the order Claude, Codex, Gemini, Muse Code, Cursor, Grok Build, Antigravity and then any other ready provider, with the first model of it that its CLI still offers. One press sets the model through the page's own path for a thread's, a project's or the app's model (`MuseUI.setThreadModel`), says "Now using <name>. Change it any time from the model menu." and turns the banner into a calm line, "<name> answers your next message. Change it any time from the model menu.", until the model is changed. A reply that is still running is left alone ("A reply is still running. Use this model when it ends.").
- **Nothing is switched by itself.** The button is a press. No code in this package changes a person's model by itself; picking the default model by availability, without a press, is not built and is the owner's decision (BRIEF, bar 5).
- **The setup guide's last step names the model.** After the sign-ins it says "Plenum will answer with <name>.", or "<name> is the chosen model, but it cannot answer yet: <reason>." with **Use <name>** under it. It adds nothing when no sign-in or key is set up, when the model panel did not load or when nothing has been read yet. The step asks the model panel to read again when it is opened, so a sign-in made a moment ago counts.
- **The setup guide paints as its requests return.** Its three status requests (the keys, the accounts, the added CLIs) each paint their own part; a row waits only for its own part, and Cursor, Grok Build and Antigravity say "Checking" until their read answers. A request that fails says so and keeps the parts that came; an answer of an older read (after **Check again**) is dropped. The summary waits for all three.

**Reason.** A person's first minutes: they sign in to one thing and the product tells them it cannot answer, offering nothing. A press that fixes it, and a guide that says what will answer, keep the choice theirs.

**Source.** Audit finding j-firstrun-3; plan P7 (and O3 for the setup guide); the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-first-model/NOTES.md`. Code: `public/ext-model-info.js` and `.css` (`readyChoice`, `answering`, `useReadyModel`, `refreshModelInfo`), `public/ext-setup.js` (`merge`, `rowReady`, `keyReady`, `modelLine`, `readModels`, `loadStatus`), `public/ext-accounts-cli.js`. Tests: `build/v06/d-first-model` (`model.test.mjs`, `setup.test.mjs`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. 15 tests (7 in `model.test.mjs`: the Use button with only a Gemini CLI signed in, the order, no button when the model is ready, a model the catalog no longer offers is never chosen, a run in progress is left alone, the helpers on `MuseUI`, textContent only; 8 in `setup.test.mjs`: the summary names the model, the Use line, no sentence without the panel, each request paints its part, a failed request, an older answer dropped, the Checking cards, a card built with its answer), in a hidden window against stand-in provider status; pictures of the banner and the guide are in `ultra/work/d-first-model/shots`. **No real provider was signed in.** The owner's real check: on a profile with only the Gemini CLI signed in, press Use and send one message.

## 133. A failed pipeline step or compare column can be tried again alone, and the steps that worked are not paid for twice

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (j-council-8, rated medium; w1-agent-snapshot-1) and built on 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P8). A pipeline stopped at its first failed step and the panel offered only Run, Edit and Delete; Run started again from step 1, so a long pipeline paid again for the steps that had worked. A failed Quick compare column showed its error and could not be retried alone.
- **Pipelines.** `POST /api/pipelines/run` takes `fromStep` (a whole number from 0 to the last step), `previousText` (the kept reply of the step before; not empty, at most the prompt limit), `skipFailed` (true or false) and `previousStep` (which earlier step that reply is); `checkResume` in `lib/pipelines.mjs` refuses a bad one with a 400 sentence before anything starts. The steps before `fromStep` are not run and send no events; `previousText` is quoted to the step as `{previous}`; with `skipFailed` the step at `fromStep` is not run (it sends a `step-skipped` event) and the next step gets the last reply that worked, named for its real step. The first and the last step cannot be skipped (nothing to start from; the last makes the final reply).
- **The panel.** A step that failed shows **Retry step** and **Skip step** once the run has ended (a stopped step offers neither; the first and the last step offer Retry only). The steps before it keep their replies. A retry sends the first run's message, project and thread. The buttons say that the steps from there on are billed again, and the line under the run says "Retry it to go on from there; the steps that worked are kept."
- **Quick compare.** `POST /api/compare` takes `only` (one column's letter, with the same seats, prompt, effort and pictures as the first run); that column alone is asked again and the others are neither asked nor billed. A failed column shows **Retry this column** (not Keep), asks again with the request the page kept, and can be retried again if it fails again. The button says that it is billed again.
