- **Not done:** a failed relay hop or a failed council seat cannot be retried alone; that needs `server.mjs` and `public/app.js` (the council's Retry seat is still open, item 98).

**Reason.** A step that fails at 3 of 5 should cost the person step 3 again, not steps 1 to 3. The retry is a press and says what it costs.

**Source.** Audit findings j-council-8 and w1-agent-snapshot-1; plan P8; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-step-retry/NOTES.md`. Code: `lib/pipelines.mjs` (`checkResume`, `stepMessage`, `runPipeline`), `lib/routes/pipelines.mjs`, `lib/routes/compare.mjs` (`runCompare`, `compareRoute`), `public/ext-pipelines.js` (`resumeRun`, `startRun`), `public/ext-compare.js` (`retryColumn`, `execute`). Tests: `build/v06/d-step-retry` (`retry.test.mjs`, `page.test.mjs`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. 15 tests (8 in `retry.test.mjs`: resume starts at the failed step, skip, a retry that fails again, no resume changes nothing, `checkResume`, the route refuses bad input, the route passes a resume on, compare runs one seat; 7 in `page.test.mjs`: Retry and Skip on a failed step with the earlier replies kept, what Retry step sends, Skip step, the first and last step, Retry this column, what it sends, a column that fails again), all with a failing stand-in step and stand-in models; the engineer also ran them against the old code (a private copy, `LIB=ultra/work/d-step-retry/oldproj/lib`). Pictures: `ultra/work/d-step-retry/shots`. **No real model ran a pipeline or a compare.** The owner's real check: run a three-step pipeline with the second step's model signed out, sign it in, press Retry step and see that step 1 is not asked again.

## 134. The phone link notices a silent relay, never replaces another program's Tailscale share, and asks before it starts a new phones file

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (mobile-1 and remote-4, rated high; remote-5; w1-phones-stop-1 and -2) and by open items of items 109 and 100; built on 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P12, O4 and O5). After the PC slept or the Wi-Fi changed, the relay carrier kept `connected = true` on a half-open socket for good, while the relay had dropped the phone, so Stop from the phone never arrived. The Tailscale carrier did not look at what Tailscale already served before it served, and was not looked at again while Plenum ran. A phones file that could not be read for more than a minute was started fresh by itself.
- **The relay carrier pings.** While its socket is open the carrier sends the text `ping` every 20 seconds (the relay's room answers `pong` without waking) and counts any frame that arrives as life. A ping that gets no frame within 10 seconds is a miss; the next ping goes at once; two misses in a row end the socket here, without waiting for a close event: the phones' handshakes end, the link shows as dropped with the reason "silent", and the carrier connects again with the usual back-off. The timers are `unref`'d and cleared with the socket. Every change of the link is published (`{ type: 'phone', kind: 'link', ... }`) and Settings > Phones reads its state again; for a silent relay it says "Your relay stopped answering, so Plenum is connecting again."
- **Tailscale: read first.** Before it serves and before it takes anything down, the carrier reads `tailscale serve status --json`. The handler on https 443 is Plenum's own only when it is one web entry whose only path is `/` and that proxies to `http://127.0.0.1:<this Bridge's port>`, with no other forwarding on 443. Free 443: Plenum sets its one handler. Its own handler already there: kept, no second serve. Anything else on 443: Plenum does not replace it, sets nothing, and says "Tailscale already serves something else on https port 443, so Plenum left it alone. Turn that off in Tailscale (the command tailscale serve status shows what it is), then turn this on again." Tailscale that will not say what it serves: Plenum sets nothing ("Tailscale would not say what it already serves, so Plenum set nothing. Open Tailscale and try again.").
- **Tailscale: off removes only its own.** Switching the link off runs `tailscale serve --https=443 off` only when the handler on 443 is Plenum's; a foreign handler stays and Plenum lets go of its claim; when Tailscale cannot say, nothing is removed and the claim is kept for the next try. Plenum never runs `serve reset` (item 109). No command names the hub's handler on port 8790, and none changes or removes it; Plenum does read the whole serve list, so the hub's entry is in what it reads, and no decision depends on it.
- **Tailscale: looked at again.** While Plenum runs and the switch is on, the Bridge asks the carrier every 2 minutes (`check()`): a handler that went away (another program ran a reset) is set again, one that another program put on 443 is reported and left alone, and a link that was refused is tried again when 443 is free. A change is published to the windows; an unchanged link causes no event and no command beyond the read. With the switch off `check()` does nothing, and with `PLENUM_TAILSCALE_CLI=off` nothing is found and nothing runs.
- **A phones file that cannot be read asks first.** A file that cannot be decrypted, parsed or is of another version is no longer set aside by itself. The registry says "Plenum cannot read the saved phones. Nothing has been replaced. Start fresh to set that file aside and begin again; every phone must then pair again.", saves nothing, does not start the bridge and does not read the file again by a timer (a file that is only locked or not ready is still read again after 2, 5, 15 and then every 60 seconds). Settings > Phones shows **Start fresh** with a question ("Plenum cannot read the saved phones. The old file is kept under another name next to it, and a new one is started. Your phones must pair again."). Yes posts `/api/phones/remove-all`, which in this state first sets the old file aside (`phones.json.damaged`, then `-2`, `-3`, never deleted) and then starts an empty registry with new keys.
- **On the Phones page:** **Any address** says in a sentence why it is grey ("Turn on Same network first. Any address is a setting of that way in."), and the relay address box is 34 px high like the other boxes.

**Reason.** Stop from a phone is part of the floor, and a link that says it is up when it is not is worse than one that says it is down. A product must never undo or replace what it did not set up (item 109 took out `serve reset`; this finishes the rule). A person's phones are re-paired by hand, so starting a new file is theirs to say.

**Source.** Audit findings mobile-1, remote-4, remote-5, w1-phones-stop-1 and w1-phones-stop-2; open items of item 109 (read before serving) and item 100 (ask first); plan P12, O4 and O5; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-phone-link/NOTES.md`. Code: `lib/remote/carriers/relay.mjs` (`planPing`, `silent`, `setLink`), `lib/remote/carriers/tailscale.mjs` (`parseServeStatus`, `inspectServe`, `readServe`, `check`, `stop`), `lib/remote/index.mjs` (`checkCarriers`, `removeAll`, `planRetry`), `lib/remote/registry.mjs` (`startFresh`, `ready`), `public/ext-remote.js`, `public/ext-remote.css`. Tests: `build/v06/d-phone-link` (`tailscale-serve.test.mjs`, `relay-silent.test.mjs`, `registry-ask.test.mjs`) and the mobile tests `mobile/test/carriers.test.mjs`, `pairing.test.mjs`, `plugins.test.mjs` and `mobile/test/helpers/fake-tailscale/fake.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. 37 tests in `build/v06/d-phone-link`: `tailscale-serve` 18, `relay-silent` 7 and `registry-ask` 12, all with a stand-in Tailscale (a fake program that keeps a serve list, with the hub's handler on 8790 in it) and a stand-in socket that goes silent without a close; no test reaches the real Tailscale or a real relay (`PLENUM_TAILSCALE_CLI=off` stays in every runner). The mobile tests and the fake Tailscale were edited for the new reads (the edits are in the working tree: `mobile/test/helpers/fake-tailscale/fake.mjs`, `carriers.test.mjs`, `pairing.test.mjs`, `plugins.test.mjs`), and the tests that pinned the older behaviour were corrected: `build/v06/ultra-f1-phones-stop/f1-review` (4 tests) and `build/v06/integ-phone/tailscale-no-reset`. Pictures of the Phones page: `ultra/work/d-phone-link/shots`. **Not tried on a real phone, over a real relay, with the real Tailscale or beside the real hub's share.** The phone's own copy of the Phones page (`mobile/www/ui`) is build output; whether it was rebuilt after this item was not checked for this text. The owner's real check: turn the phone link on with the hub's Tailscale share present and see that `tailscale serve status` still lists the hub afterwards; let the PC sleep with a phone paired over the relay and see that Stop from the phone works after it wakes.

## 135. Help: topics, the build, and a report the person can read and copy when something is wrong

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (docs-2 and ops-4, rated medium; ops-3 partly) and built on 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P11). The Help button was labelled "Commands" and opened only the Commands drawer; nothing in the app opened the log folder, copied a report, or showed the version and build, so a person with a fault could tell nobody anything.
- **The page** (`public/ext-help.js` and `.css`). A **Help** link in the sidebar above Commands, and F1 anywhere (also in a text field; a dialog that is open keeps the key). The drawer holds short topics (start a chat, stop a run, ask first and approvals, models and sign-in, settings, keyboard shortcuts), **About** (the build line from `lib/build-info.mjs`) and **Diagnostics**: the whole report as plain text, shown before anything is copied, with **Copy diagnostics**, **Save as a file** and **Open log folder**. Nothing is sent anywhere. A page error that the page caught is reported to the server (`POST /api/diag/client-error`: its message, file and line only, cut to 240 characters, at most 20 a minute) and goes to the log and to the report.
- **The report** (`lib/diag.mjs`, `lib/routes/diag.mjs`). Built from a short list of facts picked one by one, never from a state, a settings object or a log read whole: the build line, Node, Electron, Chrome, the operating system; which CLIs are found, each with the version it prints for `--version` (never a prompt; the tools are asked at once, waited for 600 ms at most and kept for a minute); for each tool and each saved key only yes or no; counts and sizes; the data folder and the app folder as paths with the user's name taken out; a project only as its last folder's name; and the last 50 error lines of the log, each cut short. It never holds a key, the launch token, a chat, a prompt, a file's content, a project's path beyond its last name, the user's name, an e-mail address, a phone's name or pairing data or a hub key. One redaction cleans every piece of text: keys by value and shape, the launch token, text after bearer, token or password, the query of a web address, e-mail addresses, any path but Windows' own folders and the data and app folders (cut to its last name), and a long quoted run in a log line (40 characters or more).
- **Routes.** `GET /api/diag` (gated: it starts each tool with `--version`; answers in well under a second, with `pending` for tools that had not answered), `GET /api/diag/basic` (looks for and starts no tool, and is not gated, so a report can be copied before the License Agreement is accepted), `POST /api/diag/client-error` and `POST /api/diag/open-log`.
- **The desktop side** (`desktop/handlers/diag.cjs`). `diag-log` answers the last lines of `server.log` (at most 1,500 lines of the last 96 KB; the shell writes the file through its own redactor, item 123) and `open-log-folder` shows `server.log` in the file manager; neither carries a path or text from the server, and the answer holds no path. Without the handler (plain `npm start`, or an older desktop app that never answers, not asked again for 5 minutes) the report uses this run's own errors, says so, and Open log folder says in one sentence where the log is.
- **The page tells the limit:** a line of error text can quote anything a program printed; the rules take out what has a shape, and a short word of the person's own inside an error line cannot be known, so the report is shown whole before it is copied.

**Reason.** Bar item 3 (every failure is explained, with a next step): a person with a fault must be able to say what version they run and what failed without hunting for a folder, and what they paste to a stranger must hold nothing of theirs.

**Source.** Audit findings docs-2, ops-4 and ops-3; plan P11; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-help-diag/NOTES.md`. Code: `lib/diag.mjs` (`makeRedactor`, `makeReport`, `noteError`, `clientErrorLine`), `lib/routes/diag.mjs`, `public/ext-help.js`, `public/ext-help.css`, `desktop/handlers/diag.cjs`. Tests: `build/v06/d-help-diag` (`unit.test.mjs`, `server.test.mjs`, `page.test.mjs`, `mutants.mjs`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `unit.test.mjs` 32 tests (it also tests the desktop handler file), `server.test.mjs` 8 tests (ports 9280 and 9281; a planted key and token that must not appear in any answer), `page.test.mjs` 11 tests in a hidden window (port 9282), and `mutants.mjs`: 22 one-line changes of the redaction, all caught by a test. Pictures: `ultra/work/d-help-diag/shots` (help and report, 1280 and 480 px, light and dark). **Open log folder was never run in the installed app** (the handler file is tested with a stand-in shell). No test sent a prompt to a CLI. The owner's real check: press F1 in the installed app, copy the report and look in it for his name, a project path or a key.

## 136. What a person has typed and not sent comes back after a reload

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (page-core-6, rated medium) and built on 13 Nov 2026 in two new files, `public/ext-drafts.js` and `.css` (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P9). The page reloads itself on a new build (and at the token card), and a half-written message was lost.
- **What is kept.** Only the text of the message box, for each thread (the scope of `MuseUI.threads.scope()`), in this browser's `localStorage` under one key. Never a picture or file, never the follow-up queue (it stays in memory), never text that starts with `/`, never text with the launch token or the shape of a key, never more than 400,000 characters for one draft or 1,000,000 for all; it is never sent to the server, so no route and no log sees it. Saved 300 ms after typing stops, and when the page is closed.
- **When it comes back.** When its chat is shown again and the box is empty: the note "Your unsent message from this chat is back in the box." with **Discard**. Text typed since is never replaced; a draft that this page did not read from or write to is never overwritten by text from another window; a thread switch while typing keeps what is in the box and saves it under the thread it was typed in.
- **When it goes.** Sending clears it; a send the server refuses puts the text back into the box and it is saved again; drafts older than 30 days and drafts of projects or threads that no longer exist are dropped, and the oldest go first beyond the size limit.
- **Storage blocked.** Every access is in a `try`; with storage blocked the page works and keeps drafts in memory while it lives. The split view's own message box is not saved, and a draft typed in the first second, before the page knew its chat, does not replace a saved one.

**Reason.** A reload that eats a long half-written message is the kind of loss a person does not forgive.

**Source.** Audit finding page-core-6; plan P9; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-drafts-browser/NOTES.md`. Code: `public/ext-drafts.js`, `public/ext-drafts.css`; no existing file was edited. Tests: `build/v06/d-drafts-browser/page.test.mjs` with `page.cjs` and `block-storage.cjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `page.test.mjs`: 12 tests in a hidden window against a sealed server (a draft is back after a reload with its note and Discard; no request carries it and the state file never holds it; another thread's text is never mixed in; a thread switch in mid-typing; two windows; sending clears; a refused council question is kept again; a key, the token, a command and a huge paste are never written; old drafts and drafts of gone chats; storage blocked; no console error); the same file fails 11 of 12 without the panel (`PAGE_NO_DRAFTS=1`). Picture: `ultra/work/d-drafts-browser/shots` (looked at light 1280 and dark 480). A draft is plain text in the browser's own storage on this PC. The owner's real check: type half a message, let the installed app update itself or reload, and see the text again.

## 137. A click that opens a new tab or a blocked pop-up says so to the model

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (browser-computer-3, rated medium) and built on 13 Nov 2026 in `desktop/browser.cjs` and `lib/browser-tools.mjs` (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P13). A click on a link with `target=_blank`, or a pop-up, became a background tab and the call returned a reply with the unchanged address; the model thought the click had done nothing and clicked again.
- **The browser records it.** The window-open handler notes the new tab (its id and address, at most 5) on the tab that opened it, and counts a pop-up that was refused (the tab limit, an address that is not http). Before a click, a typed Enter (`type`, `type-secret`) or a key press the record starts empty, so only what that action did is reported; the reply of the action (`where()`) carries `opened` and `blocked` and resets them.
- **The model is told.** `browser-tools.mjs` adds to the reply: "That opened a new tab: <id> <address>. Your own tab did not change. To read the new one, call browser_tabs with action select and tab <id>, then browser_snapshot." and, for a refused pop-up, "A pop-up was blocked, so it did not open. The page you are on is unchanged." A tab id or address that does not have the expected shape is not repeated to the model. An action that did neither says nothing about tabs. The floor rules of the browser pane are unchanged.

**Reason.** A model that is told a click did nothing clicks again, and may submit twice.

**Source.** Audit finding browser-computer-3; plan P13; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-drafts-browser/NOTES.md`. Code: `desktop/browser.cjs` (`wire`, `where`, `actOnPage`), `lib/browser-tools.mjs` (`afterAct`). Tests: `build/v06/d-drafts-browser/tools.test.mjs` and `browser.test.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. `tools.test.mjs`: 5 tests in plain Node (a new tab is named with its address and how to read it; a blocked pop-up; an action that did neither; a bad tab id or address is not repeated; a typed Enter and a key say it too); `browser.test.mjs`: 1 test of 10 checks in a hidden Electron window against a page served from the package's own port (a click, a typed Enter and a key say what the page opened or blocked). The same files fail on the old code (3 of 5 and the 5 checks). The browser window is a hidden stand-in page, not a real site. The owner's real check: ask a model to click a "open in new tab" link on a real page and see that it reads the new tab.

## 138. Programs left running by a crash: Plenum keeps a list of its children and Stop waits for the tree to end; the clean-up at the next start is built and switched off

**Decision.** Found by the audit of 12 Nov 2026 (agents-3, rated high; j-council-2; cli-4) and built on 13 Nov 2026 in `lib/child-ledger.mjs`, `lib/scratch.mjs` and `lib/proc.mjs` (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`, P3). If the Electron shell or the server crashes or is ended in Task Manager, `taskkill /t` never runs, and the process trees of the CLIs (`cmd`, `node`, their children) keep running with their `plenum-readonly-*`, `muse-council-*` and `plenum-agent-*` temp folders; and Stop started `taskkill` and did not wait for it, so a CLI behind a `.cmd` shim was alive for about two seconds after Stop. Two parts are on, one part is **off**.

*On: the list of children, and Stop.*
- **The list.** `startChild` (`lib/proc.mjs`) writes one line for each real child to `children.json` in Plenum's data folder (`MUSE_CONSOLE_HOME`, else `~/.muse-console`): the process id, the time it started, the program's name, a few hashes of the flags and paths in its command line, and the process that started it. It never holds an environment, a prompt, a key or any text a person typed. The line is taken out when the child ends or fails. At most 500 lines are kept; the file is written by temp file and rename, and two Plenum processes that share one folder take turns through a lock file (`children.json.lock`) so that no line is lost. A damaged file is set aside. The list is not used (nothing is written) with `PLENUM_NO_SWEEP=1`, with `PLENUM_SMOKE=1` and in a `node --test` process that has no data folder of its own; a test's stand-in spawner never reaches it; the hub's launcher never goes through `startChild`, so it has no line.
- **Stop waits.** `killTree` still starts `taskkill /t` at once, and now returns a promise that is true when the tree is gone and false when it is not gone after 2.5 seconds. When the first `taskkill` answers with an error while the child is still running, it is run once more after 400 ms (never a second one beside a first that is still working). A reply that was stopped ends only once the tree is gone or the limit has passed; one line goes to the log when a program did not end. Stop cannot hang: the limit timer is set before the first attempt.

*Built and OFF: the clean-up at the next start.* The sweep (`sweepOnce` and `sweepAtStart` in `lib/child-ledger.mjs`) reads the list at the next start, ends the programs of an earlier Plenum that are still running, and clears the temp folders of runs that were cut off. **It runs only when the environment has `PLENUM_SWEEP=1`; off is the default, and nothing sets it.** With it off, no PowerShell is started, no table of processes is read, no process is ended, no folder is looked at or removed, and the one thing logged is "The clean-up of programs left by an earlier run is switched off in this build." The server (after `listen()`), a timer 1.5 seconds after the server starts, and the first child each ask for the same one sweep; with the switch off each of them answers at once. The reason it is off is below.

*The guards that exist in today's code, for the day it is switched on.* A process is ended only when all of these hold:
1. its line is in this data folder's list, and the line is not older than the last start of the computer less a minute (G3: such a line is dropped without reading anything, because after a restart nothing of ours is alive);
2. its owner (the Plenum process that started it) is gone: the owner counts as alive when a live process with the owner's id was created at or before the line's start time plus 2 seconds, or at or before the child's own creation plus 2 seconds, or within a minute of the owner's recorded start; a time that cannot be read counts as alive, so a second Plenum on the same folder keeps its children, and when anything is in doubt nothing is ended;
3. a process with the child's id is running, was created between 5 seconds before and 2 seconds after the recorded start, has the recorded program name and its command line holds every recorded hash; when the name and the time fit but the flags do not (or the command line cannot be read), the line is kept and nothing is ended; just before the end that one id is looked at again alone and compared again;
4. it is not this process, not the hub's launcher (a program name that starts with `tabletalk` or `hub-starter`), and not an ancestor of the sweeping process;
5. the tree is walked from a fresh table of all processes (a child counts only when it was created after its parent, so a reused parent id never adds a stranger), and each id is ended only while it still has the creation date the table showed (G1): one hidden PowerShell call compares the date and ends the process in the same command, the root is compared first, and when it has changed nothing at all is ended. `taskkill /t` is never used by the sweep, and no process is ended by a bare id.
The table comes from one hidden PowerShell call (`Get-WmiObject Win32_Process`; `ps` on other systems); an answer that is not one JSON array is a failed read, and a failed read ends nothing. The temp folders (G2): only a folder directly in the temp folder whose name is one of fourteen Plenum prefixes and six characters that include a capital letter or a digit, that is a real folder (no link), that holds the marker file `.plenum-scratch`, and that nothing inside it has been written to for 12 hours and since before the computer started (or for a week), is removed; a folder that is the scratch folder of a line whose owner is alive is kept; a folder with more than 5,000 entries or one that cannot be read is kept. **No `mkdtemp` call of Plenum writes that marker yet** (`makeScratch` exists, and none of the calls in `lib/` and `desktop/` uses it), so even with the switch on a sweep would clear no folder today.

**Reason.** The bar is that Stop stops and a crash leaves no stray program working in the owner's name. The sweep is off because two independent checks found that ending a process by a recorded process id can end a stranger when Windows hands the id to another program: the first check (`ultra/plan/check-sweep.md`) found eleven faults, and the second (`ultra/plan/check-sweep2.md`), after the engineer's rewrite, found that the tree was ended by bare id 2.5 to 7 seconds after the table was read, and that on this PC Windows gave an id out again after as little as 5.3 seconds (89 ids came back in 90 seconds with 800 short processes), and that a start of any Plenum server that shared the temp folder removed another Plenum's live run folder after 12 idle hours (shown with a plain `node server.mjs` 2.2 seconds after its start). Its verdict was to ship the list and Stop and switch the sweep off. G1 and G2 above are the guards that answer those two findings, and G3 and the lock are further guards; they are in the code and tested with stand-ins, and the coordinator's decision is that the sweep stays off until they are proved on a real machine.

**Source.** Audit findings agents-3, j-council-2 and cli-4; plan P3; the engineers' notes `ultra/work/d-orphan-sweep/NOTES.md` and `d-orphan-sweep2/NOTES.md`; the two checks named above (both in `ultra/plan/`); the coordinator's decision of 13 Nov 2026 after the second check. Code: `lib/child-ledger.mjs` (`recordChild`, `forgetChild`, `sweepOnce`, `sweepAtStart`, `sweepSwitchedOn`, `ownerAlive`, `matchProcess`, `treeBelow`, `endScript`, `endConfirmed`, `mutate`), `lib/scratch.mjs` (`isScratchName`, `makeScratch`, `hasScratchMarker`, `untouchedSince`, `sweepScratch`, `removeScratchDir`), `lib/proc.mjs` (`startChild`, `killTree`, `streamProcess`), `server.mjs` (one line after `listen()`). Tests: `build/v06/d-orphan-sweep`, `d-orphan-sweep2` and `d-orphan-sweep3`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026, the sweep switched off. Counts from the second check's run on the files as they were then (sealed, one file at a time): `d-orphan-sweep/orphan-sweep.test.mjs` 24 tests, 23 pass, 1 skipped; `d-orphan-sweep2/faults.test.mjs` 35 of 35; `d-orphan-sweep2/engineer-24.test.mjs` 24 tests, 23 pass, 1 skipped. The skipped test is "REAL crash of the owner": in the shell the tests run in, a process's children die with it, so **no test here has ever swept the tree of an owner that really died**; the nearest proof is a server test with a seeded orphan. The guards G1, G2, G3 and the lock, and the switch itself, were added after that run, and their tests are in `build/v06/d-orphan-sweep3` (`guards.test.mjs` 18 tests and `server-off.test.mjs` 2; 15 of the 18 guard tests fail on the files as they were before the guards, laid over a copy of the project; the older suites `d-orphan-sweep` and `d-orphan-sweep2` were re-pinned for the switch and now run with `PLENUM_SWEEP=1`, with boot and uptime times set to 0, marker files in the test folders, and a different pin for the PowerShell end). Each guard as built: the creation-date check runs in the same hidden PowerShell command that ends the process (`endScript`, `endConfirmed`); the sweeping process and its ancestors (`ancestorsOf`) are never ended; lines older than the last start of the computer, less a minute, are dropped; a scratch folder is cleared only with the marker file `.plenum-scratch` in it (`makeScratch`, `markScratch`, `hasScratchMarker`) and only when untouched since before the computer started (`scratchCutoff`); two Plenum processes on one ledger take turns through `children.json.lock` (`withLock`); the one-line off note is the only thing the server does with the switch off (`server-off.test.mjs`). Every process in them is a stand-in sleeper; no test ends a program of the owner's. Not closed: the second check's N3 (a clock set back by more than a minute in the first seconds of a Plenum's life could make a second Plenum on the same folder judge its live owner dead; the checker put it below one in a million starts) and N6 (until every `mkdtemp` writes the marker, nothing is cleared). What must be true before the switch is turned on, as the code's own header says: the real-crash test has run on a shell that lets children outlive their parent; every `mkdtemp` call uses `makeScratch` (read in the code on 13 Nov 2026: 19 `mkdtemp` calls in `lib/` and two `mkdtempSync` in `desktop/browser.cjs`, and none uses `makeScratch`); and a third independent check has passed. The owner's real check, in one line: run a council, end Plenum from Task Manager, set `PLENUM_SWEEP=1` for the next start and see that only the leftover CLI of that run ends and nothing else.

*Update of 13 Nov 2026 (the scratch-folder calls).* 7 of the 19 temp-folder calls in `lib/` now use `makeScratch` and so write the marker: `plenum-agy`, `plenum-muse`, `muse-console`, `muse-console-agents`, `plenum-gemini-auth`, `plenum-images` and `muse-review` (test `build/v06/d-scratch-calls`, 8 of 8, 7 of them fail on the old files; stand-ins only). 12 stay without the marker (read with Grep for `mkdtemp` in `lib/` on 13 Nov 2026, as 12 plain calls: `agent-run`, `antigravity` read-only, `claude-cli` update staging, `council` twice, `cursor`, `grok`, `mcp-server`, `pipelines`, `providers` read-only, `tool-test`, `routes/compare`) because a model could list the folder, or it would be queued or judged, so a marker file in it would change what the program sees; they need a parent/child option in `makeScratch` first. **Condition 2 for `PLENUM_SWEEP=1` (every `mkdtemp` call uses `makeScratch`) is not met**, and the two `mkdtempSync` in `desktop/browser.cjs` are not part of this count.

## 140. The independent check of the business packages: a hand-off that cannot ask waits for the owner's yes, and eleven smaller holes are closed

**Decision.** The independent check of 13 Nov 2026 (`ultra/plan/check-business.md`: two high, four medium and five low findings in the code of items 124 to 128, and one more for the phone) was answered in `lib/programs.mjs`, `lib/program-run.mjs`, `lib/routines.mjs`, `lib/business.mjs`, `lib/decisions.mjs`, `lib/routes/programs.mjs`, `public/ext-programs.js` and `public/ext-business.js`, and by one call in `lib/remote/stop.mjs`.
- **H1: a hand-off to Codex, Gemini CLI or Muse Code in a folder on Ask first starts only after the owner's yes.** These three programs never send their own steps to Plenum (`programs.cannotAsk`: a program on this PC that is not in `GATED`). In Ask first, `runLocal` in `lib/program-run.mjs` now calls `programs.askStart` before anything starts. The card is at the top of Businesses > Today and shows the program, the model, the folder, the scope, the agent type, who asked (the lead, and the routine when one started it), the title and the whole task text. The yes names the sha256 of exactly the text that was shown (`seen`); a yes with no hash or another hash is refused with 409. No answer in five minutes (`START.waitMs`), a no, Stop, a task text over 60,000 characters (`START.textMax`), and a card asked while twelve already wait (`START.most`) each end in "not started", in words, and nothing is sent to the program; a no reads as denied. The route is `POST /api/programs/starts/answer` and it needs the launch token and Full access (a phone below Full cannot call it: it is not in `WATCH_POSTS`). With Auto-approve or Approve all the hand-off starts at once, as the owner set it. Codex in Ask first is also started with `--ignore-user-config` (`tools.codex.ignoreUserConfig`, set on a copy of the tools), so the MCP servers of the owner's Codex file are not loaded. Connected services are named in the program's instructions only for an editing agent type in Auto-approve or Approve all (`abilityList`); in Ask first the instructions say not to use a connected service that sends, posts, pays or publishes.
- **H2: a playbook is read only as a regular file with one name, no link, inside the business's folder.** `readPlaybook` in `lib/routines.mjs` opens the file first and checks the open handle (a file, one name on the disk, the real path inside the folder, the same file after the open, not a guarded or secret file, at most 2 MB), then reads from that handle. A hard link to a file outside the folder is refused, where the same hole was already closed for `BUSINESS.md`, `STATE.md` and drafts.
- **M1: a stopped turn holds the routines.** After Stop all, or Stop on the chat of a run that a routine started, no routine and no follow-up starts by itself for 30 minutes (`HOLD_MS`). The hold is written to `routines.json` (`stopHold`), so a restart keeps it, and is said in the Routines tab, in a toast and in the stop's own notice. **Resume routines**, or **Run now** on a routine, ends it. A Plenum that is closing does not make a hold. `lib/remote/stop.mjs` calls `routines.holdStarts()` at the end of `stopEverything` (not for a stop of one project's runs); it holds only when something could start by itself: the scheduler runs and a routine is on, or a follow-up waits. With no routine on, nothing is held, said or written.
- **M2: a follow-up run is listed as running and has its own Stop.** `describe().running` carries the key `f:<project>` of a follow-up, `describe().followUps` names it, the Routines tab draws a Running pill and a Stop for it, and `stop('f:<project>')` ends it (409 "not running any more" once it is over).
- **M3: a business that disallows models billed per token never runs a hand-off on an API-key route.** `runLocal` reads the business's `allowApi` (`business.overlayFor`) and refuses before anything starts, each time; when the billing cannot be told it refuses too. A folder that belongs to no business has no such rule. A per-token hand-off says so in a notice every time, no longer once per server run.
- **M4: text from files and programs is always in a marked block, and lines that imitate the owner's answers are marked.** In `lib/business.mjs` and `lib/routines.mjs` a line in `STATE.md` or in the playbook that starts like "OWNER'S ANSWERS" or like an item number (`#41 `) gets a backslash, and those words in a last-run line are bent to "(owner's answers)". What a program wrote reaches the lead inside "[Program] From <label> ... End of what <label> wrote." with the same bending (`fromProgram`).
- **L1:** the sentence on every item and answer block (`NO_PAY_SEND`) now says a send shows its own card only in a folder on Ask first, from a model or program that asks Plenum before it acts. **L2:** Approve all has its own text on the folder's row: the cards of the browser pane and of computer use are answered too, so a routine can click, type and post. **L3:** Keep takes only the text whose sha256 he was shown (`keep` needs `seen`; a yes on the changed-playbook card keeps only the hash in his answer). **L4:** a folder added to a business later goes through the same guarded-folder check as a new business (`update` calls `guardFolder`). **L5:** a routine whose business record is gone is switched off with "Switched off: its business is not there any more."

**Reason.** The owner's rule for hand-offs is that what leaves the PC or costs money keeps its own approval. Before this, a lead in an Ask first folder could hand a task to Codex, with the owner's own MCP servers loaded and "connected services" named in the instructions, and a message could leave the PC in his name with no card; a hard-linked playbook could put any file of the drive into a prompt; an emergency stop was followed within 30 seconds by the next waiting routine; and a forged "the owner said yes" line in a file could ride into every later run. The card puts the one real gate where Plenum can have one, before the start, and ties the yes to the text the owner saw.

**Source.** `ultra/plan/check-business.md` (findings H1, H2, M1 to M4, L1 to L5; L6 is the phone, below); the engineer's notes `ultra/work/fix-biz/NOTES.md`. Code: `lib/programs.mjs` (`askStart`, `answerStart`, `starts`, `START`, `TEXT.startAsk`), `lib/program-run.mjs` (`runLocal`, `abilityList`, `fromProgram`), `lib/routes/programs.mjs`, `lib/routines.mjs` (`readPlaybook`, `holdStarts`, `holdForStop`, `HOLD_MS`, `keep`, `stop`, `describe`), `lib/remote/stop.mjs`, `lib/business.mjs` (`guardFolder`, the quoting of `STATE.md`), `lib/decisions.mjs` (`NO_PAY_SEND`), `public/ext-programs.js`, `public/ext-business.js`. Tests: `build/v06/fix-biz` (`programs.test.mjs` 9, `routines.test.mjs` 8, `business.test.mjs` 5, `page.test.mjs` 9).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. 31 tests in `build/v06/fix-biz`; of the 22 unit tests (programs, routines, business), 21 fail on the code as it was before, and the one that passes is the guard test for hard-linked `BUSINESS.md`, `STATE.md` and `PLAN.md`, which was already sound. The 9 page tests run in a hidden window. The pins of the earlier suites that said the old sentences (`ultra-w3-routines`, `fix-routines`, `ultra-w3-programs`, `ultra-w3-business`, `ultra-w3-business-page`, `joint-w3/page`) were corrected on purpose, and those suites, `ultra-w3-decisions`, `integ`, the legal-gate route walk and the CSS run passed on the live tree after the changes (the engineer's last runs). All of it uses stand-in programs, a stand-in hub and the offline model: that `--ignore-user-config` really stops a real Codex from loading its MCP servers needs a real run (Codex's own help words the switch; no test can show what the program does), and so does a real hand-off to each of the three programs after a yes. Open: a Codex or Gemini lead, or a team coder, in Ask first is unchanged (`lib/agent-run.mjs`, "Coders that cannot ask": they edit on their own and Plenum lists what changed); with "ask before an agent starts" on, a hand-off shows the agent's start card and then the program's start card; the phone has no Businesses pane (L6: its bundle lacks `ext-business.*` and `ext-programs.*`), so the card is answered at the computer only, and an Approve-and-watch phone could not answer it in any case (closed by item 146: the card is answered at the computer, or on a phone with Full access).

## 141. The page shell: keyboard and screen readers, and plain words for a slow start

**Decision.** Found by the overhaul audit and planned as P10 (`ultra/plan/deferred-plan.md`); built on 13 Nov 2026 in `public/index.html`, `public/ext-a11y.js` and `public/ext-a11y.css`, without a change to `public/app.js`.
- **The chat is a log that is not live.** `#chat` has `role="log"`, `aria-live="off"` and a label: the transcript is redrawn many times a second while a reply streams, and a live region would read it again and again.
- **Two live regions say the short events once each.** `#a11yStatus` (polite: a reply finished, a run stopped, an approval waits) and `#a11yAlert` (assertive: a failure). They are filled from the run events on `window` (`plenum:event`: only the end of a run, an error and an approval card, never a delta) and from the toasts, whose own box is switched off once its toasts are spoken from these two. The same words twice within 1.5 seconds are said once. Other code can say a sentence with `MuseA11y.announce(text, { urgent })`.
- **The drawer is a dialog.** `#drawer` has `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-labelledby` its title and `tabindex="-1"`. When it opens the focus moves to it; Tab and Shift+Tab go round its controls and then the Stop controls that stay above the dim layer (the drive bar and pill, any button named Stop), so Stop stays reachable by keyboard; a dialog on top of it keeps its own focus; when the content is redrawn and the focused control goes with it, the focus returns to the same control in the new content; when it closes, the focus goes back to the control that opened it unless the app already moved it. Escape stays the app's own.
- **A slow start says so.** If the first answer of `GET /api/state` has not come in 3 seconds, plain words under the title bar say Plenum is still starting; after 30 seconds they say what to do; they go when the answer comes. It is read from the browser's own record of finished requests, so no request is added.

**Reason.** A keyboard person must be able to reach Stop and must not lose the place after a redraw, and a person who cannot see the page must hear that a reply is finished, that a run stopped, that a card waits or that something failed, without hearing the transcript read again for every streamed word.

**Source.** The overhaul audit's findings on the page shell and plan P10; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-page-shell/NOTES.md`. Code: `public/index.html`, `public/ext-a11y.js`, `public/ext-a11y.css`. Tests: `build/v06/d-page-shell/page.test.mjs`.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. 8 tests in a hidden Electron window, each failing on the old files (backup in `build/v06-before/d-page-shell`); the failure, stop and approval announcements were tested with injected events, not with real runs. **Not tested:** a real screen reader (NVDA, Narrator, VoiceOver), and real keyboard focus in a window that is shown (the tests ran hidden). The owner's real check: open the installed app with Narrator on, send a message, and see that the end of the reply and an approval card are read once and the transcript is not read again; open a drawer, press Tab round it and reach Stop. Open: `loadAll` in `public/app.js` still draws the page twice at start (the duplicate render); it was not touched because `app.js` was not in this package (closed by item 144).

## 142. The computer-use drivers say their failures through `lib/api-errors.mjs`

**Decision.** `lib/computer/generic.mjs` and `lib/computer/openai.mjs` no longer make up their own failure texts. Every non-2xx answer, a request that timed out, a connection that could not be made, an error inside a 200 answer and a reply that cannot be read go through `classify` and `errorFor` of `lib/api-errors.mjs`: the kind, a plain sentence with a next step (open Accounts, no credit, wait N seconds, pick another model), the provider's own words on one line and cut short, and the key replaced by `[key]`. The raw body and the request never reach the text. **No request is made again, on purpose:** a step the model asked for must not run twice on the person's screen, so the person sends again.

**Reason.** This closes the "Not done" part of item 104, which left the two drivers showing "returned an error (429)" while every other API path said what happened and what to do. A run that fails with a raw status cannot tell "wait a minute" from "add credit" from "pick another model".

**Source.** Item 104; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-computer-texts/NOTES.md`; plan O7. Code: `lib/computer/generic.mjs` (`said`, `failureOf`, `post`), `lib/computer/openai.mjs`, `lib/api-errors.mjs`. Tests: `build/v06/d-computer-texts/failures.test.mjs`; the sentences pinned by `loop.test.mjs` were corrected.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. 29 tests, 26 of which fail on the old files (backup in `build/v06-before/d-computer-texts`). They run against a stand-in server only: no real OpenAI, Google or other provider was asked, so the texts were not seen with a real rate limit or a real rejected key.

## 143. Release gates: `npm run check`, the core job, a tag that must be newer, and a hidden start of the packaged Windows app

**Decision.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 (plan P15):
- **`npm run check`** (`scripts/check.mjs`): parses every source file of the app (`server.mjs`, `plenum.mjs`, `lib/`, `desktop/`, the scripts of `public/`, `scripts/`) without running any, and checks that every relative import names a file that exists, that every named import is an export of its module (V8 links the modules without running them, which needs `--experimental-vm-modules`, so the script starts itself once with it), and that an imported package is listed in `package.json` and installed. No network, nothing started, nothing written.
- **`.github/workflows/core.yml`:** runs `npm ci` and `npm run check` on `windows-latest`, on every push to `main`, every pull request, by hand, and when `release.yml` calls it.
- **`release.yml`:** the new job `core` (calls `core.yml`) and the existing `suites` job must both pass before `prepare` creates a draft. `prepare` also refuses a tag that is already published and a tag that is not strictly higher than the newest published release (a pre-release such as `-beta.1` sorts below its release), because electron-updater offers an update only for a higher version than the installed one. On Windows, the unpacked app is built first and started once, hidden, by `scripts/smoke-packaged.mjs` (`npm run smoke:packaged`: sealed folders, a free port that is not 4317, an allow-list environment, the app's own smoke mode, the process tree ended by its pid) before the Build step attaches anything to the draft.

**Reason.** A tag whose server or shell does not even parse, a rebuild under a version that installed apps never offer, and a Windows build that does not start would each have reached the owner's draft. They are caught before the draft exists.

**Source.** Plan P15; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-release-gates/NOTES.md` and `O11.md` (the reading of GitHub's and electron-builder's pages, whose confirmed points are in the Status lines of items 6 and 7). Code: `scripts/check.mjs`, `scripts/smoke-packaged.mjs`, `.github/workflows/core.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `package.json` (the two scripts only). Tests: `build/v06/d-release-gates` (`check.test.mjs` 14, `gates.test.mjs` 18); the pins of `ota-03` (workflow, prepare) and `legal-packaging` (jobs, steps, scripts lists) were corrected.

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. Proven by those 32 tests and by a local hidden run of the check and the smoke script. Only a real tag run on GitHub proves the workflow's wiring (`core` before `prepare`, the tag rule, the smoke step on the runner): nothing was pushed. The pins on the author, copyright and `LICENSE.txt` lines are left to the test-truth work and the owner (item 139).

## 144. A failed council seat can be run again alone, the verdict says how many seats it rests on, and the title bar keeps the drive pill's words

**Decision.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 in `lib/council.mjs`, `server.mjs`, `public/app.js` and `public/style.css`.
- **Run one seat again.** A seat whose round-1 answer failed shows **Retry** (the same model) and **Change model** (another one, for this turn) on the card; **Run this seat** starts it. The picker has a box, **Also make it seat X of my saved council** (for the moderator: "Also make it the moderator of my saved council"), which is not ticked; ticked, the pick is also saved into the council. `POST /api/council/retry` (`handleCouncilRetry` in `server.mjs`, `retryPlan` and `retryCouncil` in `lib/council.mjs`) runs that one turn. `runCouncil` and the retry share `councilEnv`, `seatJob` and `moderate`, so a retried seat gets the same prompt, folder setting and gates as in the first run. The card is drawn in place while it runs.
- **The moderator alone.** A council whose moderator failed has **Run the moderator** (or **Run the moderator again** when the last error is the moderator's); the seats are not asked again. The saved row keeps `moderator`, `answered`, `total`, `newer` and `noVerdict`.
- **What the verdict rests on.** The card says "Verdict based on A of N seats" (the numbers are kept in the saved row). When a seat answered after the verdict was written, the card says the verdict does not count it yet and offers **Rerun the verdict**.
- **Files of the question.** A retry has to send the question's pictures and files again, so the server holds them in memory for the two newest councils that carried files (`councilFilesKept`, `COUNCIL_FILES_KEPT = 2`); nothing is written to disk and a restart forgets them.
- **The title bar.** With the side pane open the bar keeps the drive pill's words at 1440 px and takes two rows at 960 px (it took three, since item 121). Fit steps 4 and 5 of the ladder are tried a second time with the chips moved to the first row (`data-chips="top"`, `public/app.js`, `public/style.css`).
- **`loadAll` draws once.** The second `renderAll` at the end of `loadAll` in `public/app.js` is gone; the page test counts one chat draw with the badge and the music drawn.

**Reason.** One failed seat meant asking the whole council again and paying for every seat; a verdict written on two answers out of four looked like one on four; and the title bar was the Open item that made two tests of `mob-21-app` fail.

**Source.** The Open items of items 98, 129, 121 and 141; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-council-retry/NOTES.md`. Code: `lib/council.mjs` (`councilEnv`, `seatJob`, `moderate`, `retryPlan`, `retryCouncil`), `server.mjs` (`handleCouncilRetry`, `councilFilesKept`), `public/app.js`, `public/style.css`. Tests: `build/v06/d-council-retry` (`lib.test.mjs` 8, `server.test.mjs` 9, `page.test.mjs` 11).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. The 28 tests in `d-council-retry` all fail on the old files; `v06/mob-21-app` passes 111 of 111; the pins of `ultra-w0b-page` and `ultra-w0-integration` were corrected. Every run in them is with stand-ins (the offline model, stand-in CLIs, a page in a hidden window); no real provider was asked. `lib/council.mjs` still makes its folders with `mkdtemp`, not `makeScratch`, on purpose: `build/council/test-council.mjs` pins that a Codex or Gemini seat runs in an empty folder, and a marker file would be a file in it (item 138). **Not tested:** the chips beside the real window buttons of the desktop app, and a real retry in a work round. The owner's real check: fail one seat of a real council (sign one CLI out), sign it in, press Run this seat, and see that only that seat is billed and the verdict says the new number.

## 145. Leftovers of the second look: the narrow License Agreement card, the Phones lines, the notices' place names, the Browser pane sentence

**Decision.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 (plan O9) in `public/ext-legal.js`, `public/ext-legal.css`, `public/ext-remote.js`, `lib/notices.mjs` and `public/ext-browser.js`.
- **The License Agreement card at 480 px.** Under 560 px the card has smaller padding, its reading box is at least 40% of the window's height, the short version starts closed, the two buttons share a row, and in a short window the card scrolls with its footer in view. The Legal page's five tabs sit in three columns (three and two), not four and one.
- **Phones lines** (`public/ext-remote.js`): the relay line says it reaches the computer through a relay in your own Cloudflare account and says "Save its address first" only while no address is saved; the "Any address" line says it only changes who can use that way in.
- **The notices' place names** (`lib/notices.mjs`, the `where` lines) use the page's own words and the `›` the page shows, for example "Settings › Phones", "Under the Same network switch", "Settings › Agents, in the Plenum Hub card".
- **Browser pane sentence.** Under `node server.mjs` the pane says the browser is part of the desktop app, that this copy was started with `node server.mjs`, and to open the desktop app.

**Reason.** The second look at the pages found words that named a control the page does not have, and a card that did not fit a narrow window.

**Source.** Plan O9; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-leftovers/NOTES.md`. Tests: `build/v06/d-leftovers` (`legal-box`, `phones-page`, `static`).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. The tests run on a private copy of the old files with `APP_ROOT` to show they fail there; the pins of `legal-page` (2) and `brw-02-browser-pane` (1) were corrected. **The agreement's texts and the terms revision are unchanged**: only the card's layout and the page's own words moved. ## 147. A failed reply offers the button for its cause, and Plenum still never signs in, changes the model or sends again by itself

**Decision.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 (plan O2), closing the "Not done" list of item 114 except what is listed open below.
- **Buttons by kind.** `FAILURE_ACTIONS` in `lib/cli-errors.mjs` lists, for each kind of failure, the buttons in order: **Retry** (puts the message back to send again), **Sign in** (`MuseUI.openAccountsAt`), **Pick another model** (`MuseUI.showModelMenu`) and **Open the log**. Retry is derived from the same table, so it no longer shows where it cannot help. The buttons are drawn in `public/ext-turns.js`; a refusal of the terms shows a notice with the text put back and no Retry. Each button only opens a place: the person signs in, picks the model and sends again.
- **A cut-off ACP reply says so.** In `lib/acp.mjs` an exit or an error after the agent had written text is a failure with the text kept and a message that starts with the cut-off words; a failure with no text keeps its words and gains a kind.
- **The shell tool says what it drops.** `runShell` in `lib/proc.mjs` keeps the start and the end of a long output and returns `droppedChars`; the card's summary in `lib/tools/20-command.mjs` says how many characters were dropped.
- **A refused option is remembered.** `lib/cli-fit.mjs` remembers in memory, for one hour (`KEEP_MS`) and only for the version of the CLI that refused it, an option a CLI refused; the next run that would send the same line to that version is refused in words before the CLI starts. `streamProcess` in `lib/proc.mjs` calls `knownMisfit` before a start and `rememberRefusal` after a `flags-rejected` failure (checked in the code on 13 Nov 2026). `friendly()` in `lib/providers.mjs` marks the two Claude key errors `kind: 'auth'`, so they offer Sign in.

**Reason.** A failed reply named the cause and left the person to find Accounts or the model menu; Retry showed where it could not help; a cut-off ACP reply looked whole; and a CLI that had changed an option was started again with the same line.

**Source.** Item 114 (Not done) and plan O2; the engineer's notes `ultra/work/d-failed-reply/NOTES.md`. The coordinator applied three follow-ups after the notes (the `streamProcess` wiring, the dropped count in the command card, the `auth` kind in `friendly()`): they are done, not open. Code: `lib/cli-errors.mjs`, `public/ext-turns.js`, `public/ext-turns.css`, `public/app.js` (`errorEl`, `openAccountsAt`, `showModelMenu`), `lib/acp.mjs`, `lib/proc.mjs`, `lib/cli-fit.mjs`, `lib/tools/20-command.mjs`, `lib/providers.mjs`. Tests: `build/v06/d-failed-reply` (37: table 4, shell 6, ACP 9, fit 7, page 11).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. The tests fail on the old files; the pins of `cli-04-cursor-core` (acp) and `ultra-f1-guard-paths` (output) were corrected for the new marker words. Every proof is with stand-ins (stand-in CLIs and agents, a page with injected errors): no real CLI hit a real limit and no real refused option was seen. Still open: no reading of a CLI's `--help` (an option is learned only from a refusal); an ACP turn that ended on a limit marker is passed on unmarked; **Open the log** opens the server's log folder (the route Help uses), not the log of that one run; the sign-in errors of `lib/cursor.mjs` and `lib/grok.mjs` carry no kind (the page recognises their sentence). The owner's real check: sign Codex out in its own tool, send a message, and see Sign in under the failed reply, opening the Codex card.

## 148. A long chat is drawn from its end

**Decision.** Built on 13 Nov 2026 (plan O6) in `public/app.js`, `public/style.css` and `public/ext-turns.js`.
- **Drawn from the end.** A chat of more than 60 messages (`CHAT_WHOLE`) draws its last 10 (`CHAT_TAIL`). 12 earlier ones (`CHAT_MORE`) are drawn each time the reader comes within 1,600 px of the top (`CHAT_NEAR_TOP`) or presses **Show earlier messages** (`drawEarlier`). The view does not move. The chat stands exactly at its end.
- **Places.** Each undrawn turn keeps a hidden, empty place (`turnPlace`), so Search and the rows under the turns find the same turns as before; a place asked to scroll into view first draws the chat from its message on.
- **Far turns.** A drawn turn more than 3,000 px from the window (`FAR_MARGIN`, `farWatch`, `setFar`) keeps its height and its contents leave the layout (`.far` in `public/style.css`); a turn that holds the focus or a selection is left as it is.

**Reason.** Drawing 2,000 messages held the window about 19 s at opening, 4 to 6 s at each send, end of run and switch, and half a second per keystroke.

**Source.** Plan O6; the engineer's notes `C:/Users/willi/PlenumWork/mcshots/ultra/work/d-long-chat/NOTES.md`. Tests: `build/v06/d-long-chat` (`behave.test.mjs` 15 of 15, 4 of 15 on the old files; `timing.test.mjs` 6 of 6, run alone).

**Status.** Built on 13 Nov 2026. Measured the same day on this PC in a hidden window with a sealed server and made-up streams, 2,000 mixed messages, median of 5: opening 19.4 s to about 0.5 to 0.8 s (worst long task 5.2 s to under 0.3 s); a keystroke 514 ms to under 5 ms; switching back 0.2 s where the end never came into view before; no long task while a reply streams. Tried and dropped: `content-visibility`, removing the `:has()` rules, a shared time formatter and a different walk in `public/ext-turns.js` (each under the 20% bar or slower). **Known costs and open:** scrolling all the way to the top of 2,000 messages still takes about a minute in steps of 0.3 to 0.5 s; a Search jump to the oldest message is one draw of about 5 s (it needs an async reveal in `public/ext-history.js`); split view's right pane still draws its whole thread; the path for browsers without scroll anchoring (the phone on iOS) is written but not tested; far turns are outside the accessibility tree until within 3,000 px. Not proven with a real model's stream, the owner's own long chat, a phone or a screen reader. This closes the Open item of item 108 about the long-chat redraw cost.

## 150. From a council's verdict to a business that its lead sets up, with everything only the owner can do waiting as prompts

**Decision.** A council card with a verdict offers "Set up as a business" (also under New business, "From a council verdict"). One form shows the ideas the server read from the verdict (rename, drop, add), the folder new businesses are made in, the lead model, the template and one choice of how the lead works. Nothing is made before Start setup.
- **Start.** For each idea Plenum makes the folder, the business from the template, SETUP.md (the verdict and the idea as quoted model text, every line prefixed `  | `) and a thread named Setup, and runs the lead one step at a time as an ordinary chat turn through the routines' own runner. The folder must not lie inside a folder Plenum already works in. A business that was made always has its setup card.
- **How the lead works.** "Ask before every change" is the default and leaves the folder on Ask first. "Work on its own inside this folder" sets Auto-approve on that folder only. The form says that commands then run without a card and are not held to the folder, that a file edit outside it still shows a card, and that leaving sends, posts, payments and sign-ups to the owner is an instruction to the lead, not a lock. If Ask first could not be set and the folder is on another mode, no step starts until the owner presses Continue setup.
- **It keeps going.** The next step starts by itself after a step that names one and after an answer, inside the business's daily limits. At most 12 steps start by themselves in a row and 40 in all, the owner's presses counted. The check is made where a step starts, whatever planned it. A pause says which limit and offers Continue setup. With the scheduler off there is no timer, and the card offers Continue setup.
- **Stop.** Stop on the card, and the routines' Stop for a setup step (which a phone below Full may press), is written to setup.json before it is answered. Whatever the step came to, and across a restart or a crash, nothing of that setup starts again until Continue setup.
- **Prompts.** A text question (`OWNER: ask`), a thing only the owner can do (`account` or `spend` with `link:` and `needs:`; Done / Not now / No), and the usual items. A note that looks like a password, a code, a key or a card number is refused and nothing is saved; the sentence says what to do instead. When prompts of a setup wait, a notice "<Business>: N things need you" goes to the desktop (it follows the existing approval-alerts switch) and to a paired phone as a push.
- **BUSINESS.md stays the owner's.** The lead writes `outbox/BUSINESS-proposal.md` and asks with a plan line. The server marks that item, gives it its own Yes and No, and says on the card what Yes does. Only that Yes, tied to the hash of the text shown, makes the text BUSINESS.md; the old text is kept as `outbox/BUSINESS-before.md`.
- **Done** is the lead's claim checked against the folder: PLAN.md's Setup steps marked, BUSINESS.md a text accepted after the start, STATE.md there, nothing waiting.
- **The floor.** Only the owner's presses start a setup, continue it past a ceiling, change a mode or switch a routine on. No hook, tool or prompt part reaches the setup module. Every setup POST needs Full on a phone.

**Reason.** The owner's words of 14 Nov 2026: "make sure that once the council decides on a business idea or set of ideas it can delegate the setup steps to a model in charge of the agents to autonomously do the setup, asking me in in app prompts for everything it needs." He is away at work, so setup must go on without him and must stop for certain when he says stop. An independent check the same day found that an answer other than Yes could replace BUSINESS.md, that a Stop could be lost at a step's end or a restart, that the ceilings could be passed by answering, and that the Auto-approve sentence promised more than the code holds. Each is closed in the rule, not in the wording, except the last: commands outside the folder are not locked, so the form now says so.

**Source.** `ultra/plan/setup-brief.md`; `ultra/plan/check-setup.md` (2 high, 4 medium, 7 low); the engineers' notes `ultra/work/d-setup/NOTES.md` and `ultra/work/d-setup2/NOTES.md`. Code: `lib/setup.mjs`, `lib/routes/setup.mjs`, `lib/needs-you.mjs`, `desktop/handlers/needs-you.cjs`, `lib/decisions.mjs`, `lib/hooks/72-decisions.mjs`, `lib/routes/decisions.mjs`, `lib/routes/routines.mjs`, `lib/routines.mjs` (`step`), `lib/business.mjs` (`adoptCharter`), `lib/remote/push.mjs`, `lib/remote/index.mjs`, `lib/business-templates/40-from-council.json`, `public/ext-biz-setup.js`, `public/ext-biz-setup.css`, `public/ext-business.js`, `public/app.js`, `server.mjs` (`needsYou`). Tests: `build/v06/d-setup`, `build/v06/check-setup`, `build/v06/d-setup2`.

**Status.** Built on 14 Nov 2026 and corrected after the check the same day. Tests: `build/v06/d-setup` 58 of 58, `build/v06/check-setup` 16 of 16 (they failed 12 of 16 on the first build), `build/v06/d-setup2` 20 of 20 (its 15 lib tests fail on the first build). Every model in the tests is the offline stand-in, so how a real lead follows the playbook, words its OWNER: lines and uses the proposal is not proven; only a real run in the installed app can show it. The desktop notification was tested with a stand-in shell and the phone push's wiring is pinned by source text, so neither has been seen arriving. There is no "Set up from this reply" button for an ordinary reply (the server accepts a single reply as the source). Open: no lock for a command that reaches outside the folder in Auto-approve; Stop all and Stop on the step's chat hold for 30 minutes and are not written down first (after a crash in that window the step goes on after the hold); with a routine switched on, an answer to a Setup item can also start a follow-up run in the Routines thread; a business folder is checked against the folders Plenum works in only through the parent the owner picks; the secret check can refuse an innocent bare number of 4 to 8 digits on a do-this prompt.
