## 152. "Not signed in" is said only when the CLI says so; a refused sign-in is named as that, and a state that is not known reads "checking", then "no answer"

**Decision.** Built on 14 Nov 2026, after the owner's report that an error said Claude Code was not signed in while the setup guide said "Signed in".
- **A reply is never read for a cause.** `streamClaudeCli` in `lib/claude-cli.mjs` read the text of a run's result for "not logged in", "please run /login", "invalid api key" and "authentication" whenever nothing had been streamed, and threw "Claude Code is not signed in" with no kind and no detail. A reply that arrived only in the result and talked about authentication was reported as a sign-in failure. Sign-in words are now read only from the CLI's own report: a result that reports an error, a message the CLI wrote in the model's place (model `<synthetic>`) and its error output. The check for a saved session that is gone reads the same report, not the reply.
- **A refused sign-in is named as that.** When the CLI refuses a run for its sign-in, `claudeCliStatus({ fresh: true })` is asked. If it says "signed in", the failure has the new kind `sign-in-refused` (`lib/cli-errors.mjs`, the fact `signedIn`): "Claude Code's sign-in was refused for this reply, although Claude Code's own check says it is signed in. Send the message again. If it is refused again, open Accounts and choose "Sign in again" on the Claude Code card.", with the CLI's own line under it. `FAILURE_ACTIONS` gives it **Retry** and **Sign in**, and the page's `CLI_ACTIONS` has the same row. If the check says "not signed in" or gives no answer, the old sentence stands. Plenum sends nothing again by itself (item 147). Each refusal writes one line to the log: what the check said and the CLI's own line, keys taken out.
- **The check: no answer is no answer.** `claudeCliStatus` took every output that was not JSON for "signed out". `readAuthStatus` finds the JSON object between other lines, reads words only when they say nobody is logged in, and answers null for everything else: not kept, and never "signed out".
- **The footer says "checking", then "no answer".** The status items at the bottom of the window (`drawStatus` in `public/app.js`) showed "Muse Code not installed", "Claude key needed", "Codex not found" and "Gemini not installed" before the first read after every start, and "Claude sign-in needed" whenever Claude Code's check gave no answer; nothing asked again. An unknown state now reads "<name>: checking" with a neutral dot, and while Claude Code's check has no answer the page asks the status again as it does for a list that is being read: after 3 seconds, then every 6, for 10 quick reads.
- **The asking no longer ends.** Until now the page stopped after those 10 reads (item 91), so an answer that came later was never shown. `planProvidersPoll` now goes on, each wait twice the one before (12 s, 24 s, 48 s and so on), up to every 10 minutes, for as long as a list is being read or a state is not known. From the answer of the tenth quick read on (about a minute, `providersLate`) an unknown state reads "<name>: no answer", because "checking" would no longer be true; the Claude item's title says that Plenum keeps asking. The item puts itself right when the answer comes.
- **The model list, the team, plan use.** `readinessFrom` in `lib/model-info.mjs` counted "no answer" as "Needs sign-in" for Claude; on the Claude Code route it now counts as ready, as for every other CLI. `classifyFailure` in `lib/team.mjs` has the class `refused`: the task moves to the next model with the words "had its sign-in refused for this task, although it is signed in" (`lib/team-run.mjs`), and the provider is not marked "needs sign-in" for 15 minutes. When the small request of the plan-use reading is refused that way, the meter says so in its own sentence (`CLAUDE_REFUSED`).

**Reason.** Three places said "not signed in" without the CLI having said it, and each contradicted Accounts and the setup guide, which wait for the real answer of the same check. A message that tells a signed-in person to sign in sends them to a card that says "Signed in".

**Source.** The owner's report of 14 Nov 2026. Looked at on his PC the same evening: Claude Code 2.1.296 answers `loggedIn: true` (a Claude account; asked with this app's own variables taken out of the environment), and its no-prompt `initialize` answer names the same account with the main chat's flags and with a held run's flags (`--setting-sources= --strict-mcp-config`). Code: `lib/claude-cli.mjs`, `lib/cli-errors.mjs`, `lib/model-info.mjs`, `lib/team.mjs`, `lib/team-run.mjs`, `public/app.js`, `public/ext-turns.js`, `public/style.css`. Tests: `build/v06/fix-signin-truth` (37: run 15, status 5, usage 2, words 8, page 7), one more case in each of `d-failed-reply` (table, page), a changed one in `ultra-f1-cli-failures` (run), and in `ultra-w0b-page` (logic) the schedule test changed with three more beside it.

**Status.** Built on 14 Nov 2026. On the committed files 15 of the library tests and 5 of the 7 page tests fail. The "no answer" state was watched in a window whose long waits were shortened to a twentieth, not for a real minute. Proven with a stand-in CLI and a page whose status answers are changed: no real refusal by Claude Code was seen, and which of the three places produced the owner's message is not known (a failed run wrote nothing to the log, which is why a refusal now writes a line). Not done: Codex, Muse Code and Gemini CLI are not asked again after a sign-in failure, so their sentence is still "is not signed in"; only Claude Code's unanswered check makes the page ask again.

## 153. The plan watch: plan use is read by itself, projected to each reset, and a business is warned before it runs out

**Decision.** Built on 14 Nov 2026, on the owner's words "Add in auto usage checks for all signed in subs with alerts regarding whether business is projected to run out of usage".
- **Readings by themselves.** `lib/plan-watch.mjs` takes the free readings of `lib/quota.mjs` every five minutes (Codex's app server answers `account/rateLimits/read` without a model; Claude Code and Muse Code report with their replies) and keeps each window's values in `plan-use.json` in the state folder (version 1, written atomically, 14 days, thinned). Claude Code on its program route and Muse Code can only be renewed by one very small request: the watch sends one when the newest reading is older than `paidEveryMin` (60 by default; 30 to 360, or 0 for never), through the refresh reason `watch`. One ledger in `lib/quota.mjs` covers every reason (the watch 30 minutes, a person 60, a limit error 15, each counted from the newest request of any reason), a request is never sent while a run of that provider is going or to a program that is missing or signed out, and the time of the request is on disk before it is sent. Gemini CLI, Antigravity, Cursor and Grok Build report nothing (`--help` of the two that are installed was read on 14 Nov 2026); their rows say so. No timer exists with `PLENUM_NO_SCHEDULER=1` or `PLENUM_SMOKE=1`, nothing is read before the License Agreement is accepted, an answer to the page is built from what the last round learned and asks no program anything, and the watch never starts a sign-in.
- **The watch does not spend the plan it measures.** A request of the watch that finds no use (no value rose inside its period, no new period began above nothing, no limit changed its word) doubles the wait for the next one, up to 6 hours; `calm` in the file counts them. Use seen in a reply or in a request ends the longer wait. A window that only reset does not count as use, because the watch's own request can begin a 5-hour window. Idle, a provider is asked four times a day instead of 24 once the wait has grown. Check now does not count and keeps to the person's hour.
- **The projection.** A period of a window ends when its reset time moves by more than two minutes or passes, or its value falls by two points or more. The pace is the recent one (at least three readings covering a quarter of two hours for windows up to 12 hours, of a day for longer ones), else the whole period's (after a tenth of the window, sure only with a rise in the last 30 minutes or three points), else unknown. From it: the percentage at the reset and, past 100, when the window is used up. States: fine, tight (85 to 100 at the reset), short, out, unknown; `sure` is false while the data is thin, and for a used-up time that is already past (the reading is old), which is said as "At that pace it would be used up by now."
- **Businesses.** A routine starts only on a business's leads (`lib/routines.mjs`), each up to the owner's line for its provider. For each lead the watch works out the stretches of time in which it is at or past that line: facts (a window at or over the line, or used up, from now to its reset; a lead that cannot run, for ever) and projections (from the moment the pace reaches the line). A business has nothing left where the stretches of all its leads overlap for ten minutes or more. Its sentence says which lead reaches the line when and which lead takes over, or when it runs out and until when nothing is left. The line is reckoned with the measured pace, so at a line of 100 the business's time is the window's own. The models of its kinds are helpers and never make a business run out. "Nothing left now" is said only from facts: a projection whose moment is past reads "may have nothing left by now" and is an early estimate. A lead that cannot run is named as that ("cannot run now"), and a business whose leads all cannot run is told "Plan use is not the reason" and raises no plan alert.
- **What is worth an alert.** Facts, when they last: a limit that is used up, and a business whose leads are all at their lines, when 30 minutes or more remain (or no reset time is known). A projection only for a window longer than 12 hours: sure, the moment still ahead and at least a tenth of the window before the reset, a tenth over the line at the reset, a quarter of the period gone and the whole period agreeing (used divided by the part gone reaches the line). The same rule serves a plan at 100 and a business at its own line (`onCourse`). A business alert is worked out from facts and such projections alone (`firm`, `alarms` in the view), so a 5-hour guess that comes first does not hide a weekly alert. Each alert is made once for the period of the window that decides it (`fired` in the history file, so a restart does not repeat one); a business alert about the stretch a window alert of the same round already names is not made twice. Every alert carries `current`: one that no longer holds leaves the strip and says so in the list.
- **Where it shows.** The Plan use pop-up (`public/ext-quota.js`, `public/ext-planwatch.js`): cards with bars, the server's sentences, Check now, the three switches. One line on each business card. A strip above the message box (role status, never a dialog). A Windows notification when the window is not in front (`desktop/handlers/plan-watch.cjs`; the server publishes a state event with `planAlert` for it). The phones get it as the existing kind `limit` with the server's own sentence, under their limits switch, each alert with its own tag, and a tap opens Plan use. Every sentence is made on the server, so all four places say the same words.
- **Found on the way.** The phone app's notifier (`mobile/plugins/unifiedpush`) did not know the kind `needs` of item 150 and showed "Plenum has an update." for it; the plugin's list and the Kotlin list are now pinned to `EVENT_KINDS`. The business switcher was 35 px high on the phone and is 44.

**Reason.** The owner runs several businesses on his own subscriptions and works another job. Until now a limit showed itself when a routine failed. A plan-use number alone does not say whether a business gets through the week: that needs the pace, the reset and the business's own line. An alert that is wrong, or that comes 15 minutes before the fact, teaches a person to ignore all of them, so the rules for an alert are stricter than the rules for a sentence on the card.

**Source.** The owner's words of 14 Nov 2026. Research of the same day: Claude Code has no documented way to read plan use without a reply; `rate_limit_event` carries a status, an optional `utilization` and a type, and is sent when the status changes. The independent check `ultra/plan/check-planwatch.md` (its findings 1 to 10 are closed by the fix round `ultra/plan/planwatch-fix.md`). Made-up use against the rules (`ultra/work/coord/sim-lead.mjs`, `sim-week.mjs`; bursts of use, 300 to 400 periods a row): 5-hour windows that truly ended at 85 percent raised an alert ahead of time in 24 to 46 of 100 under every lead that was tried (30, 45, 60, 90 minutes) and with a reading every 5, 30 or 60 minutes, and an alert that was right came a median of 10 to 20 minutes before the limit in most rows, which is why they have none. Weekly windows under the rule that is built: with use through the day, 0 of 300 false alerts at a true end of 60 percent, 47 of 300 at 85, 164 of 295 at 95 (a close call), and every overrun of 115 percent or more told a median of three to four days ahead; with use in the evenings and at weekends, 0 false at 60 and 85, 6 of 279 at 95, and of the real overruns 36 of 274 at 105 percent, 161 of 300 at 115 and 294 of 300 at 140 were told ahead (a median of one to four days). Code: `lib/plan-watch.mjs`, `lib/routes/plan-watch.mjs`, `lib/quota.mjs`, `lib/usage.mjs`, `lib/claude-cli.mjs` (limits), `lib/remote/push.mjs`, `lib/remote/index.mjs`, `public/ext-planwatch.*`, `public/ext-quota.*`, `public/ext-business.js`, `public/ext-phone.js`, `desktop/handlers/plan-watch.cjs`, `server.mjs` (the start, `planAlert` in the state).

**Status.** Built on 14 Nov 2026. Tests: `d-planwatch-s` 167 (the watcher, the rules, the meter's ledger, the routes, a sealed server), `check-planwatch` 61 (the independent checker's, a hidden window among them), `d-planwatch-p` 40 (the page with stand-in routes and with the real routes, the desktop handler), the project's phone tests 207 and 5 for the phone's Plan use. Proven with stand-in programs, made-up readings and a made-up clock: no real week of the owner's use, no real notification toast and no real phone push was seen. First real readings: on 14 Nov 2026, a minute after the installed build (commit `0eea0c3`) started, Plan use showed Claude Code with a status and a reset time and no percentage (asked with one very small request), Codex with a weekly percentage from the free check, Muse Code with a 5-hour and a weekly percentage (one very small request), and Gemini and Antigravity as programs that report nothing. Two things in the words were corrected from that sight: the pace of a limit longer than 12 hours is said by the day (a weekly limit read "0 percent an hour" and still rose a percent), and a state that rests on an early estimate only is labelled "May run short" or "May get tight" on its card (the server's `projected.sure`; the sentence already ended "This is an early estimate.").

## Open items

- **The owner must fill these before anyone else gets a build:** the placeholders `[EFFECTIVE DATE]` (License Agreement, Privacy Notice, Third-party services, `legal/terms.json`) and `[LICENSOR NAME]` are what a new person sees on the first screen of a first start, in the agreement they are asked to accept (item 149). They need the release date and the maker's legal name; nothing in the code can supply them.
- The plan watch (item 153): the cards with real readings were seen in the installed app on 14 Nov 2026. Still owed there: the Windows notification of an alert and the push on a phone (both need a real limit, or a week on course for one), and the line of a real business. Nothing replayed the owner's real week of use.
- The projection knows no weekly rhythm (item 153). With use in the evenings and at weekends a moderate overrun (105 to 115 percent) was told ahead of time in a seventh to a half of the made-up weeks; the "used up" and "nothing left" alerts still come when it happens. A pace by weekday, learned from the weeks in the history, is the next step and needs real weeks to be judged.
- Claude Code reports plan use only in passing (`rate_limit_event`: always a status, a percentage only when it sends one), so for Claude the watch may know no more than "within limit", "close to the limit" and "used up". Then it projects nothing for Claude, and a business that leads with Claude is warned when Claude says it is close or used up, not days ahead; Codex and Muse Code report percentages. The first real reading (14 Nov 2026) came with a status and a reset time and no percentage; how often a real Claude Code sends one is still not known. Each reading the watch asks for costs one very small request (Haiku, one word, about four a day when idle). Claude Code's own `/usage` shows percentages; whether it can be read without a request is not known, and trying it means sending a command to the owner's real Claude Code, which needs his OK.
- A phone below Full does not see Plan use: `GET /api/plan-watch` waits for the License Agreement like `GET /api/quota`, and the phone policy gives every such read to Full only (`lib/remote/tiers.mjs`). The push of an alert reaches every paired phone whose limits switch is on. The phone app's notifier knows the kinds from its next build on; no Android build exists on this PC.
